# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed
- **Dropdown/combobox popovers inside modals** (Invite user, Edit user role picker, and every other `useDropdownMenu`-based menu) no longer get clipped by the modal's own scrolling body. The panel used to render as part of the scrollable content, growing a scrollbar while open and visibly jumping the scroll position when it closed; it now floats above the modal like a normal popover and closes automatically if the modal scrolls under it.
- **Edit user "Role & access" row** - the Add/Change button next to the Role and scope dropdowns no longer sits visibly lower than them.
- **Bounce detection Recent checks** no longer renders as a raw browser bullet list under Last automatic check. The history is a compact styled list with status icons, scrolls inside a capped height when there are many runs (slim on-theme scrollbar only when needed), and the latest run is not duplicated below the summary card.
- **Role/status/sign-in-method/event filters** (Users & roles, Active sessions, Role assignments) no longer show a duplicated caption - these already had their own label around the field, which the new caption below would have doubled up.
- **SearchableSelect/PhoneCountrySelect panels flipped above their trigger** now stay anchored to it as their content resizes (e.g. typing into the search box narrows the list), instead of leaving a growing gap between the panel and the trigger.

### Changed
- **Role/scope pickers** (Invite user, Edit user, Active sessions bulk revoke) show a visible "Initial role" / "Role" / "Event" caption above the dropdown instead of no caption at all, and skip the search box entirely for short option lists (4 or fewer roles) - only longer lists (organizations, events) still show one.

### Added
- **My account — self-service SSO unlink and connect.** The Profile card header now shows an **SSO** menu with **Unlink SSO** (when linked) and one **Connect \<provider\>** item per enabled, not-yet-linked provider — not mutually exclusive, since an account can hold more than one linked provider at once. Unlinking requires setting a new local password in the same step and always demands one proof of identity first: a confirmed TOTP/recovery code if enrolled, otherwise the current local password; an account with neither is refused and pointed at an administrator instead. Unlinking is also refused while any role assignment is still owned by an identity-provider group sync, so a caller can't unilaterally convert IdP-managed access into access they control. Unlike the admin-side unlink, only the account's other sessions are revoked (the one you're using stays signed in) and `must_change_password` is cleared, since the caller chose the new password themselves. Connect reuses the existing self-link flow (password/TOTP re-auth, then the identity provider) that already existed for admins linking a user but had no entry point from this page, and is only offered on accounts that already have a local password to re-authenticate with.
- **My account — phone number.** The Profile card now lets a user set and edit their own internal contact phone number (country code + number), the same field already editable on the account by an admin via Edit user. Optional, internal-contact-only, never shown on tickets — same hint text as the admin-side field. Reuses the existing `PhoneCountrySelect` picker and the admin route's own normalization (trim, empty clears to null). Follow-up (layout polish): the country-code picker and number field now split their row the same way the admin Edit user modal's own phone row does (compact code picker, number field fills the rest, stacks below 640px) instead of both shrinking to their content width. Email also moves out of the read-only info table into the editable-fields column as a disabled input matching Display name/Regional format's own style, laying the groundwork for a future editable email field without changing behavior now.
- **OIDC identity providers gain configurable given name, family name, and phone claims**, alongside the existing email/name/groups claims in Identity provider settings. When the combined name claim is absent from the token, the display name falls back to the given + family name claims instead of being left blank.
- **Bounce detection last automatic check.** Event settings → Mailing shows the latest bounce-ingest run (time, OK/Failed, message counts). **Run check now** in the card header triggers a one-off ingest and updates the card (Test connection still does not). Settings → Health adds a soft **Bounce detection** row (`not_configured` / `ok` / `degraded`) based on enabled events' last runs. Does not affect `/healthz`.
- **Bounce detection Check every** now controls when each event is due for bounce-ingest. The deploy loop wakes on `BOUNCE_INGEST_TICK_SECONDS` (default 60; legacy `BOUNCE_INGEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS` still accepted as the tick) and skips events whose poll interval has not elapsed.
- **Bounce-ingest System Logs bridge.** When `OPS_HEALTH_TOKEN` and `BOUNCE_INGEST_APP_URL` are set, each event run POSTs `mail_bounce_ingest_ok` / `mail_bounce_ingest_failed` into Settings → Logs (mail). Missing config is a quiet no-op (stdout only).
- **Bounce detection Recent checks** keeps the last 20 automatic runs per event (`BounceIngestRun`) and shows them under Last automatic check when more than one run exists.
- **Bounce-ingest Docker HEALTHCHECK** writes `/tmp/bounce-ingest-heartbeat` after each successful tick; compose marks the container unhealthy if the file is older than ~3 minutes. `/readyz` gains soft gauges `bounce_ingest_enabled` / `bounce_ingest_problem` (never alone causes HTTP 503).
- **Delivery details keeps the popup short and readable.** The Overview notice shows the error code plus a plain-English explanation (same glossary as **Also verify bounce**), never the raw bounce/NDR dump. A successful handoff (accepted / sent / delivered) gets a matching green notice: "The mail provider accepted this message for delivery." **Raw fields** still lists the bare error code. The in-modal Delivery timeline is removed (sibling attempts already live on Delivery history / the Communication log). Modal padding is on header/body/footer so any scrollbar sits flush with the panel edge. Footer actions get icons (Export / View sent message / Open attendee); **Open attendee** is hidden when the modal is already opened from that attendee's page.
- **Delivery details → Raw fields explains itself.** **Sent at**, **Batch ID**, **Session ID**, and **Client timezone** get a hover/focus hint explaining what each field means and why it is sometimes blank (e.g. **Sent at** stays blank for most providers, which only confirm acceptance, not final delivery; see **Accepted at**).
- **Event Send test: Also verify bounce.** On Event settings → Mailing → Send test email, an optional switch waits up to 90 seconds for a hard (5xx) bounce for that To address to appear in the event's bounce IMAP mailbox (same parse path as production ingest). Like a plain Send test, it does **not** create an Attendee or EmailDelivery row.
- **SMTP Test connection** on Organisation and Event (dedicated SMTP) mail settings: verifies host/port/login without sending mail (same nodemailer verify as Health check live). Separate from **Send test email**. Requires a saved SMTP config; unsaved form changes disable the button.
- **Event bounce detection (IMAP ingest).** Superadmins can configure per-event IMAP mailbox settings under Event settings → Mailing (below Mail transport) so forwarded delivery-failure / NDR messages mark matching `EmailDelivery` rows as `bounced`, with SMTP code and reason stored for Delivery details. Optional **Use SMTP username & password** reuses the event's effective SMTP auth (host/port stay separate). Dedup uses processed IMAP UIDs in the database (not `\Seen`). Deploy adds a `bounce-ingest` sidecar that runs `ingest-bounces` on a loop (`BOUNCE_INGEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS`).
- **Bounce NDR parser covers mailhop / Synology variants.** Delivery-failure messages that omit the address on the `failed: host … said:` line, wrap it as an unescaped `<user@x>failed:` run in the HTML part only, or use Postfix `<user@x>:` / RFC 3464 `Final-Recipient`, are recognized, so Also verify bounce can mark the probe delivery as bounced when the NDR lands in the configured IMAP folder.
- **Bounce ingest prefers RFC 3464 DSN parts.** IMAP extraction keeps `message/delivery-status` (Final-Recipient / Original-Recipient, Action, Status, Diagnostic-Code) instead of only human `text/plain`, so standard postmaster reports can be correlated without relying on free-form prose.
- **Bounce ingest decodes MIME quoted-printable / base64 before parsing.** Some relays soft-wrap the `failed: host … said: …` diagnostic line mid-sentence (`=\r\n`); without RFC 2045 decoding the stray `=` broke the pattern match even when the wording was otherwise recognized. IMAP extraction now walks the MIME tree, decodes each part's `Content-Transfer-Encoding`, and combines `text/plain` and tag-stripped `text/html` (HTML tag stripping keeps a literal `<user@example.com>` intact instead of treating it as a tag).
- **Organisation Settings → Health check.** Superadmins get a grouped system status view (Core infrastructure vs External integrations) with expand-for-details rows, on-demand **Run live checks** (Nominatim / SMTP verify / Microsoft Graph token / identity providers / Cloudflare Access / local file-storage write probe / Open-Meteo when weather is enabled; Power Automate stays configured-only), **Export** Markdown, and **Copy for GitHub Issue**. External rows use a **role, provider** label pattern (e.g. `Address lookup, Nominatim`, `Map tiles, OpenStreetMap`, `Weather, Open-Meteo`). Each configured identity provider is its own row (`Identity provider, OIDC - Authentik`); empty state is a single Identity provider placeholder. Cloudflare Access is listed separately. Planned placeholders cover Wallet passes (PassCreator). File storage probes the local `UPLOAD_DIR` volume (S3 remains degraded until implemented). The topbar System status menu links to this tab. The user Wiki page is titled **Organisation Settings** (renamed from Instance Settings). Ops `/healthz` and `/readyz` are unchanged (ADR 0026 / ADR 0037).
- **The Delivery log (Communication → Delivery) now gives real diagnostics instead of just a status line.** Each row's new "…" menu opens **View sent message** (the actual rendered subject and HTML in a sandboxed preview — the recipient's real QR code and ticket link are always replaced server-side with a clearly labeled "hidden for privacy" placeholder, since the stored template still carries their literal, unresolved tokens) and **View delivery details** (provider, message id, attempt count, sanitized error/error code, and an honest **Delivery Timeline** built from every real send made to that attendee — Initial send / Resend 1 / Resend 2 … — each with its own timestamp and status, plus **Export as .txt** of those same real fields and its own **Open attendee** footer shortcut). A new **Search** box (recipient name/email) and **Template** filter join the existing Status/Purpose filters inside one collapsible "Filters" menu, and a new **Export log** button downloads the full filtered log as CSV (same row cap as the audit log export). None of this needed a database migration — the underlying data already existed on `EmailDelivery`; only the API and DTOs were extended to expose it. The recipient's name is itself a link straight to their attendee profile (the row menu no longer needs a separate "Open attendee" item for that), the privacy placeholder in **View sent message** uses the shared blue `Notice` component, and both new modals show skeleton placeholders instead of a bare "Loading…" while fetching.
- **The Attendee Detail page's own "Delivery history" card now has the same "…" actions menu as the Delivery log table** — **View sent message** / **View delivery details** per past send, without leaving the attendee's profile to look it up in Communication.
- **The Delivery log gained a few more Logs-parity details after another round of review feedback.** The Card header now also has **Clear filters** (disabled until a filter or search is active) and a **Live** toggle that polls every ~1.75s regardless of which tab is open — the same pattern as Organisation settings' own Logs, and it also fixes the Delivery log tab's own count staying blank until the tab was actually opened, the same fix already applied to Active sessions' tab count. The Sent/Queued column (and its mobile-card equivalent) now shows a second, muted line with the browser-local time of whoever triggered the send, when known, in the same "HH:MM (IANA, UTC±offset)" format as Audit/Security log's own actor-local time, with a "(i)" hint on the column header explaining the two lines; the Attendee Detail page's own Delivery history card stays a single UTC line by design, since it's a compact list rather than a detail table. The active tab (Compose/Delivery log) is now reflected in the URL (`?tab=log`), matching Organisation settings' own tabs, so a refresh or a shared link lands back on the same tab instead of always resetting to Compose. The "View sent message"/"View delivery details" modals' redundant footer "Close" button is gone (the header's own "×" already closed them), the empty "No messages sent yet" state gained a one-line description matching every other empty state on the page, and the Delivery Details modal's own provider-error banner is now the shared `Notice` component (error variant) instead of a bespoke red box.
- **Info tooltips now explain what a section does across Instance Settings, Event Settings, Requirements, Reports, and My account.** Covers General (Instance URL, Support contact), Branding (Organisation branding, Theme), Mail (Mail transport, Sender, SMTP connection, Microsoft Graph, Power Automate, Send test email), Security, Identity providers, Cloudflare Access, and Logs in Instance Settings; Custom attendee fields and Check-in behaviour in Requirements; Basic information, Status, Event logo, Upload images, Ticket types, Mail transport, Send test email, and Danger zone in Event Settings; Attendance confirmation in Reports; and Password in My account. Same hover pattern Event archiving already used, for every card whose title alone doesn't convey a non-obvious rule or consequence, such as changing your password ending your other sessions, or an item issued to attendees not disabling until its return is recorded. Where a card previously explained itself with an always-visible paragraph saying the same thing the new tooltip now says (Instance URL, Support contact, Organisation branding, Theme, Mail transport, Event logo, Upload images, Ticket types), that paragraph is now the tooltip instead of a second copy of it. State-dependent text that actually changes with context, such as why Send test email is disabled or Theme's live color readout, stays visible inline. Instance URL's own input field is now labelled just "URL" instead of repeating "Instance URL" a second time right under the card title that already carries it.
- **New consolidated "Branding" settings tab, replacing the separate "Organisation branding" and "Branding" (theme) cards previously split across the General tab.** Organisation name/logo and instance-wide theme (accent color + font) now live together under Instance Settings → Branding, saved through one shared Save/Reset footer instead of two independent ones — saving attempts both together and reports a partial failure clearly (e.g. "the rest was saved") instead of an all-or-nothing outcome. The bare hex color input is replaced with a curated 12-swatch palette (Admitto blue, Indigo, Violet, Purple, Pink, Red, Orange, Amber, Green, Teal, Cyan, Slate) plus a custom-color tile backed by the native color picker for anything outside the palette. Font selection gets the same treatment: four built-in picks (Admitto Sans/Inter, Manrope, Space Grotesk, IBM Plex Sans), each self-hosted via `@fontsource` so they render identically for every visitor rather than depending on what happens to be installed on their OS, shown rendered in itself with an honest "N styles" indicator (Manrope and Space Grotesk ship no italic on Google Fonts at all, so their pill says so instead of claiming a browser-synthesized fake is real). A "Create font family" flow handles a fully custom brand font instead of a single file: one modal takes as many weight/style variant files as the org actually has (regular, bold, italic, …), guesses each file's weight and style from its filename, uploads every variant (.woff/.woff2/.ttf/.otf, 5 MB limit each, magic-byte validated server-side via a new endpoint mirroring the existing logo upload's validation) with an instant local preview before the network upload resolves, and rolls back cleanly with an error toast if any one upload fails. A live preview block (primary/hover/tint swatches, sample buttons, sample text, plus a Regular/Bold/Italic row flagging any style a custom family doesn't actually have a file for) reflects the in-progress draft before it's saved. Picking a built-in font by name alone still needs no upload at all; a custom family instead needs one or more of its own weight/style files under that name. Follow-up (PO review): the org's chosen theme font no longer leaks onto Admitto's own sidebar/topbar wordmark — it's the app's own typeface regardless of org theme, not customer-branded content. Uploaded font files are now also served back with the correct `font/*` Content-Type instead of falling through to a generic default. Event Settings' own same-named "Event branding" tab and card — which only ever held the event logo and the email-template image asset library, no actual branding controls — are renamed to "Images" and "Event logo" to stop colliding with this new instance-level tab. The Theme card also gained its own **Restore defaults** action, separate from the shared footer's Reset — Reset only discards unsaved edits back to whatever is currently saved (a no-op on a never-configured instance, since "saved" already is the default), while Restore defaults sets the draft back to Admitto's own factory look (default blue, Admitto Sans) regardless of what's saved; organisation name/logo are untouched either way, since neither has a meaningful factory default to revert to. Follow-up (PO review, second pass): the "Create font family" modal shared its CSS classes with the existing Add-attendee modal but not its stylesheet import, so on the Settings route it silently fell back to the browser's own unstyled `<dialog>` layout instead of a centered full-screen overlay — fixed. The "N styles" popover's own open animation briefly clobbered its horizontal-centering transform, flashing off-position for a frame before snapping back — fixed, and its text (10-12px) was genuinely too cramped, sized up. The per-variant weight `<select>` was too narrow to show labels like "Semibold 600" in full, and the Normal/Italic toggle sat visibly uneven against its row (the shared segmented-control's own standalone spacing, same fix already applied elsewhere for the same reason) — both fixed, and the modal widened to give the uploaded filename more room instead of truncating it hard. Georgia and Courier New are dropped from the built-in list per PO feedback (replacing the previous four system fonts with the three self-hosted ones above). Follow-up (PO review, third pass): a font family, once created, is now kept in a saved library instead of being replaced by the next upload — the org can build up several custom families, switch the active one with a single click, and remove any of them, without ever re-uploading files just to go back to a font they'd already set up. Fixed a real font-loading bug affecting every self-hosted built-in pick: `@fontsource` ships many small per-language font-subset files, some of which Vite was inlining as base64 `data:` URIs below its default size threshold — the staff SPA's own Content-Security-Policy doesn't allow `data:` font sources, so those specific subset/weight combinations (most visibly bold) silently failed to load; asset inlining is now disabled for the admin build so every font file is always served as its own same-origin request instead. The live preview's neutral badge no longer sits visibly higher than the buttons beside it (an unnecessary `align-self` override was fighting the row's own centering), its sample line no longer crowds the controls above it, and the sample text itself is now a plain type-specimen pangram instead of a sentence describing itself. The per-variant weight `<select>` in the "Create font family" modal now has its own `id`/`name` (previously flagged by browser devtools as an unlabeled form field). Follow-up (PO review, fourth pass): a saved custom family's "Aa" sample now renders in the actual uploaded font even when that family isn't the active pick — every saved family gets its own local font preview, not just the one currently in use. The remove button on a saved custom font tile no longer overlaps the sample text below it (the tile now has enough room reserved for it). The "N styles" list no longer needs its own scrollbar for a family with several weights while a built-in font's shorter list never did — both now use the same, larger comfortable height. Wording across the Theme card and the "Create font family" modal was rewritten to plain sentences (no em dashes), and a couple of hint sizes that were smaller than the rest of the app (11px against the usual 12px floor) are fixed. Follow-up (PO review, fifth pass): the built-in font tiles had the same invalid button-inside-a-button structure already fixed once for saved custom families, so they are rebuilt the same way, a plain card holding its own select button plus a separate styles control. That also let a leftover click handler on the styles popover be removed instead of patched, since it only existed to stop clicks from reaching the old outer button. Follow-up (PO review, sixth pass): a saved custom font tile no longer needs extra top padding to make room for its remove button, so its sample/label/hint now start at exactly the same height as every built-in tile. The remove button itself now floats as a small circular badge over the tile's top-right corner instead of sitting inside reserved space. The "Primary colour" and "Font" section labels now use the same small-caps style as "Live preview", since all three mark out the same kind of grouping within the card. Branding's own standalone description paragraphs are sized up to match the equivalent text elsewhere in the app. The Regular/Bold/Italic row in the live preview is shown larger, since the weight and style contrast it exists to demonstrate was hard to judge at the previous, smaller size. Follow-up (PO review, seventh pass): "Live preview" no longer sits in its own bordered/shaded box, which had made it look like a separate element and put its own label slightly out of horizontal alignment with "Primary colour" and "Font" above it — it now sits directly in the Theme card's own flow, at the same left edge as everything else. "Organisation logo" (in the shared logo-upload component, also used for Event Settings' own logo and the Setup Wizard's branding step) now uses the same small-caps label style as "Primary colour"/"Font"/"Live preview", instead of being the one section heading left out of that consistency pass. Organisation branding's own internal spacing was uneven for a subtler reason than just different gap values: one of its hint lines was a `<p>`, which (unlike the plain `<span>` every other hint in this panel already used) carries the app's own default paragraph spacing on top of the layout's own gap, silently doubling up. Switched to a `<span>` to match, so every gap in that card is now the same size. Follow-up (PO review, eighth pass): "Organisation logo", "Primary colour", "Font", and "Live preview" all use the same plain label style (matching "Organisation name") instead of the small-caps style tried in the previous pass, and each now has its own short description directly under it before its own control, closing a gap that was inconsistent between sections for a reason similar to the one above (one relied on the card's own default paragraph spacing, the other overrode it, so they landed at different sizes despite looking like the same pattern). A saved custom font tile's own "Aa" sample is sized the same as a built-in tile's (was quietly 4px smaller, which nudged its label and hint down by a few pixels relative to its neighbours). Saved custom font families gained a proper **Edit** action next to Remove, reopening the same upload flow pre-filled with that family's existing files so weights and styles can be added, replaced, or removed without starting over or losing the family's saved name unless deliberately changed. Uploading (or editing in) two files under the exact same weight and style is also now caught before saving: a browser only ever renders one of them, so this used to succeed silently while quietly making one uploaded file unusable - the affected rows are flagged and Save is blocked until it's resolved. Follow-up (PO review, ninth pass): "Organisation logo" and "Live preview" still had a bigger gap than "Primary colour"/"Font" between their own heading and the description directly under it, since that spacing came from the surrounding layout's general gap rather than the tight, margin-free spacing the other two happened to have. Both now match. Follow-up (tenth pass): the public ticket page (`/t/…`, the standalone page an attendee actually opens) never rendered any of the chosen built-in fonts either, including the default (Admitto Sans/Inter) — the four self-hosted picks are only ever bundled into the staff admin's own build (`packages/ui`'s `fonts.css`), and the ticket page is a separate, bundler-free server that never loads that stylesheet, so it silently fell back to the browser's default sans-serif regardless of what the org picked, the same pre-existing gap the Settings UI's own copy already promises isn't there ("font for staff UI and public ticket pages"). The ticket page now self-hosts each built-in font's actual weight/style files directly and inlines a matching `@font-face` (mirroring the same mechanism already used for a saved custom family), falling back to Inter when the saved pick matches neither a built-in name nor a saved custom family (e.g. stale data from a deleted one) so it always has a real face to render, the same anti-lockout guarantee the staff admin already gets from `fonts.css` loading Inter unconditionally. Follow-up (eleventh pass): Manrope, Space Grotesk, and IBM Plex Sans only ever had their Regular (400) and Bold (700) weights self-hosted, but the app's own UI text uses `font-weight: 500`/`600` in many places (Inter, the only font this was ever tested against before, happens to ship both) - picking one of those three as either theme font silently rendered all that medium/semibold text one weight heavier than designed (700, the nearest face actually available) instead of a real match. `fonts.css` (and the ticket page's own matching self-hosted set, kept in lockstep) now load the full 400/500/600/700 range for all three, same as Inter always had. Follow-up (twelfth pass): the font choice is now independent per surface instead of one setting for the whole instance. The existing font picker (built-in tiles, saved custom families, upload/edit/remove) is now a pure library - browse, upload, edit, remove - and no longer picks anything or highlights a tile as "active" on click; a new "Font by surface" section below it lists three rows, Admin panel, Registration form, Ticket page, each a label and short description on the left with a compact dropdown on the right (reusing the same row layout already used elsewhere in Settings, e.g. Mail transport's TLS/STARTTLS toggles), and that's now the only mechanism for changing which font applies where, so there's one control that does the picking instead of two that could disagree. Ticket page defaults to "Same as Admin panel" until explicitly set to a different built-in or saved custom family; Registration form is a disabled placeholder ("The public sign-up page attendees would fill in" - that surface isn't built, with no version number promised for when it will be). The saved custom-font library stays shared - upload once from the picker, then assign it to either surface from its own dropdown without re-uploading. Fixed in the same pass: any disabled `<select>` anywhere in the app (this Registration form row is the first one) silently lost its dropdown chevron, because the shared `:disabled` style set the CSS `background` shorthand, which resets `background-image` to `none` even though only a background colour was intended - it now sets `background-color` instead, so a disabled select still reads as a select, just visibly inactive; and the three new Font-by-surface selects gained an `id`/`name` each, since browser devtools flags any form field missing both as a potential autofill problem. Follow-up (PR review, thirteenth pass): Ticket page's dropdown could not actually be pinned to the default font ("Admitto Sans") independently of Admin panel - since an unset override already meant "same as Admin panel," and the built-in default was itself represented the same way, picking "Admitto Sans" for Ticket page while Admin panel used any other font was impossible; changing Admin panel later would always drag Ticket page's look along with it too. "Admitto Sans" is now a reserved label (alongside the three self-hosted built-ins, blocked from ever being reused as a saved custom family's name) and a distinct, real option in the Ticket page dropdown, separate from "Same as Admin panel" - so Admin panel = Manrope and Ticket page = Admitto Sans, explicitly decoupled, is now possible. Also closed two gaps a patch-coverage check flagged: an unused derived value nobody actually read was removed instead of tested, and a genuine gap - the Ticket page field's own inline validation error had no test forcing it to actually render - is now covered. Follow-up (PR review, fourteenth pass): each Font-by-surface row's dropdown sat flush at the top of its row instead of centred against the two-line label/description beside it, because `.settings-row`'s shared `align-items: flex-start` (correct for its other users, e.g. Mail transport's Switch rows, unchanged here) left a single-line `<select>` visually adrift from the description text under its own row's heading. Fixed with a rule scoped to just these three rows, not the shared class.

- **Instance Settings — Audit log polish: severity badges, actor local time, event scope filter, CSV export.** The Audit log table's Action column now shows a color-coded badge per action type (destructive/revoking actions in red, permission/role changes in blue, everything else neutral gray), matching the Attendee activity timeline's existing tone convention. `ACTION_LABELS` gained friendly text for every real `action_type` the backend already writes that previously fell through to its raw snake_case string (event contacts/resources, bulk revokes, mail settings, retention runs, etc.). A new **actor local time** secondary line renders under the Time column for any row written from a browser request — reusing the `X-Client-Timezone` mechanism already wired for the per-attendee activity log (`AttendeeActionLog.client_timezone`), now also threaded through `AdminAuditLog` via a new nullable `actor_timezone` column and `AdminAuditWriteInput.timezone` field on all ~35 write call sites; older rows and CLI-originated writes (retention, session cleanup) simply show nothing extra. A new **Scope** column and "All events" filter classify each row as **Instance** or a specific event (derived from `metadata.eventId`/legacy `metadata.event_id`, both matched since two different call sites historically used different key casing — no schema change) with a dropdown populated from the event picker's own event list. Finally, an **Export** button downloads the currently filtered view (not just the visible page) as CSV, following the same `Content-Disposition`/blob-download convention as the Attendees list's own export, capped at the same 50,000-row `EXPORT_ROW_CAP`, and self-audits with a new `audit_log_exported` action type. Follow-up (PO review): the Audit log also moves out of the Security tab into its own new **"Logs & audit"** Settings tab, matching the mockup's navigation — the topbar System Status menu's "View system logs" link now points there instead of Security. Second polish pass (PO review against the mockup): the Card header gains a **System/Audit** segmented toggle next to Export — "System" is present but disabled until the App-only System Logs viewer (a later phase) exists, keeping the header's height fixed either way. The Time column now always shows UTC first with the actor's own local time as a fixed secondary line, replacing the separate UTC/Local mode toggle entirely (both were previously shown one-at-a-time behind a switch; showing both together made the switch redundant) — date filters are now always UTC-day bounds to match. The From/To fields use the same `DatePicker` component already used on the event-creation form instead of a native `<input type="date">`. Empty/error states now use the shared `EmptyState` component (icon + title + description, with a Retry action on load failure) instead of plain paragraphs, matching the Attendees list's own convention. The Scope column header gains an info-icon tooltip explaining Instance vs. per-event scope. The Details popover renders metadata as a humanized label/value list (e.g. "Row count: 12") instead of a raw JSON blob, and skips the `eventId`/`event_id` key entirely since the Scope column already shows it. And a **Rows per page** selector (25/50/100/200) joins Previous/Next in the footer, matching the same control already used on the Attendees list and Reports page. Third polish pass (PO review, DevTools inspection): the Card header's height had regressed (81px vs. the shared ~61px standard) — the Export button now uses `size="sm"` like every other header action, and `Segmented` gained a `className` prop so this usage can zero out `.seg-control`'s shared `margin-bottom` (meant for its other usage on the Mail tab) and shrink to the compact header convention without touching the Mail tab's own sizing. The Action/Scope/rows-per-page selects were missing `id`/`name` (Chrome DevTools flagged them); fixed. `DatePicker`'s control was hardcoded to 40px while every Select/Input on the page uses the shared 36px token — fixed at the shared-component level so this and its other usages (event creation, event settings) are consistent; its calendar panel also had no viewport-edge clamping at all (confirmed via `body{overflow-x:hidden}`, which silently hides anything that spills past the edge) — it now clamps to a max-height with its own scrollbar when neither above nor below has room, and flips to right-aligned when the field sits close to the viewport's right edge. A **search box** ("Search actor or event…", matching the mockup) filters by actor email/display name or event title — resolved server-side via two small lookups (`User`/`Event`) before the main query, since neither lives directly queryable on `AdminAuditLog`. The Action and Event filters moved out of the toolbar into a "Filters" dropdown (reusing the same `FiltersMenu` component the Attendees list and Reports page already use) — inline, their selected value was clipping since the toolbar had no room to show it in full; dates stayed inline per PO preference. The footer was rebuilt to match the Attendees list's own convention exactly: "Showing 1–25 of 152" (left) instead of "Page X of Y (Z total)", with a separate "Page X of Y" text between Previous/Next (right). A filter/page change re-fetching used to blank the whole table out immediately (skeleton or nothing) before repainting once the new page landed, even though the previous rows were still valid to look at — it now dims the existing table instead, matching the Attendees list's own re-fetch treatment, only showing the skeleton on the true first load. The Details popover now humanizes camelCase *values* for the specific metadata keys that carry raw field names (`fields_changed`/`secrets_rotated`/`secrets_cleared` from mail settings changes, e.g. "fromAddress" → "From address") instead of just their keys, and an empty array now reads "None" instead of a blank line; each row also gained a **copy** icon next to Details that copies the whole row (time, action, scope, actor, IP, details) as plain text, for sharing one entry without a screenshot. `exportAuditLog`'s request is a GET, so it wasn't sending the `X-Client-Timezone` header the mutation-style helpers attach automatically — its own self-audited `audit_log_exported` row now gets the operator's local time too, like every other write. Fourth polish pass (PO review): the Time column now matches the mockup exactly — a monospace "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS" (UTC) primary line with a compact "HH:MM for actor (City, Country)" secondary line, the country resolved from the actor's IANA timezone via the new `countries-and-timezones` dependency (a small, fully offline static lookup — no network call, no new PII, unlike IP-geolocation, which was researched and flagged back to the PO as a real product/architecture decision given this repo's self-hosted, data-minimizing conventions and its own precedent of not making live external calls from app logic). The "Time" header drops its "(UTC)" suffix (redundant now that the row itself labels both lines) in favor of an info-icon tooltip explaining the two-line format, matching the Scope column's own tooltip convention. The toolbar reordered to Search, From, To, then the Filters dropdown (was Search, Filters, From, To); date fields shrank to a fixed compact width instead of stretching (a date is always the same shape) with search taking the freed space, and the DatePicker's calendar panel width is now fixed instead of tracking its own (now narrower) field, so it stays comfortably sized regardless. "Clear filters" moved out of the toolbar (where it appearing/disappearing shifted the date fields' width) into the Card header as a small button that's always present but disabled with nothing to clear, next to a renamed "Export CSV" button (previously just "Export", which didn't say what format). The Scope header's info icon was vertically offset from its own text (missing `align-items: center` on the tooltip trigger); fixed. The Details column's copy-row icon shifted position depending on whether "View" or "—" preceded it — the row now uses `justify-content: space-between` so the icon always sits at the same right-aligned spot. Below the existing 768px breakpoint, the table is replaced by one card per entry (mirroring the Attendees list's and Reports page's own desktop-table/mobile-card split), and the Card header now wraps to a second line under ~480px instead of clipping the last button off-screen (three header actions plus the title don't fit in one line at phone widths, unlike every other Settings card's one-or-two-action header). `ScrollFadeTabs` (the horizontally-scrolling Settings/Event-Settings tab strip) gained left/right arrow buttons that only render on the side there's actually more to scroll to, and now scrolls the active tab into view whenever it changes — previously a deep link straight to a tab near the end (e.g. `?tab=logs` on a narrow viewport) left the strip scrolled to position 0 with no way to tell which tab was active without manually scrolling to find it. Fifth polish pass (PO review): the Time cell's monospace font (13px, `--fs-mono`) read visibly larger than the rest of the row's 13px proportional text despite the identical nominal size — monospace glyphs are wider/bulkier at the same font-size — now uses `--fs-xs` (12px, the same size already used for badges/table captions) for visual parity. The actor-local secondary line now shows both the tz abbreviation and the city/country together ("16:02 CEST (Warsaw, Poland)", was one or the other across two earlier passes) — hardcoding an "en" locale for the abbreviation gave a raw offset ("GMT+2") instead of a named one, inconsistent with the rest of the app's own `formatEventTime`/`formatEventDateTime` convention (event dates, admitted_at); the same `getPreferredLocale()` call those already use fixes it, and the redundant "for actor" wording is dropped (the Time header's own tooltip already says whose local time this is). The From/To date fields no longer show a "From"/"To" label above them in the toolbar — `DatePicker` gained an `ariaLabel` prop (threaded to both the text input and the calendar toggle button) so removing the visible label doesn't drop its accessible name, with the locale date-format hint folded into the placeholder instead. The System/Audit toggle is now always the last (rightmost) Card-header action instead of the first — right-anchored inside a flex row, only a trailing item's own edge stays fixed regardless of how many conditional buttons precede it; a leading item's position shifts as siblings appear/disappear. `ScrollFadeTabs`' scroll arrows now paint a solid background instead of `none` — the fade gradient behind them is deliberately semi-transparent for most of its width (so scrolling tab text stays legible as it approaches the edge), which let that same tab text show through the arrow icon itself. Switching between the Identity route and any in-page Settings tab fully unmounts/remounts this panel (Identity lives on a separate route, not this panel's own tab-state), re-triggering a genuine first load each time — a sub-200ms fetch (the `useDelayedLoading` grace window) rendered nothing at all in the interim, letting the card visibly collapse then snap back once data arrived; the skeleton now always occupies its own height (a `visibility` toggle, not conditional rendering) so a fast reload never visibly jumps. **Mobile (< 768px):** Clear filters/Export CSV move out of the Card header (title + the always-present System/Audit toggle now comfortably fit one line) into their own row in the toolbar, Export right-aligned; Search and Filters now share their own row (search's flex-basis was resolving off its `width: 100%` input's own intrinsic sizing and claiming the whole row alone — `flex-basis: 0` sidesteps that); From/To move into the Filters dropdown instead of the toolbar, stacked full-width one per row rather than side-by-side, which clipped the "From (dd/mm/yyyy)" placeholder; the Filters dropdown's `<fieldset>` needed an explicit `min-width: 0` (fieldsets get an automatic `min-width: min-content` regardless of an author `width`, which blew the two now-adjacent date fields' container out past 260px and off the left edge of the screen); the mobile card's Time cell was `flex: none`, which forced the row wider than the viewport once the secondary line grew to include both the tz abbreviation and city/country — now shrinks and wraps like everything else on the card; and the row-level Details popover, right-anchored from its own trigger the same way the Filters panel is, could overflow off the left edge when a trigger sat close to a narrow card's own left side — now clamps against its declared max-width so its own left edge can't cross the viewport. The Details popover's raw `provider` value (`export_only`, `smtp`, …) now renders through the same `MAIL_PROVIDER_LABELS` map the Mail settings picker itself uses ("Export only", "SMTP", …) instead of the internal enum string. Sixth pass (independent deep review): the audit-log CSV export now prefixes the UTF-8 BOM, matching `handleExportEventPii`'s existing convention — actor names and JSON `details` values can carry non-ASCII text that previously risked mojibake in Excel. `DatePicker`'s calendar panel measured its own already-clamped height (`offsetHeight`) instead of the natural content height (`scrollHeight`) when deciding whether to clamp it, a feedback loop that visibly oscillated the panel's height while scrolling with it open; and its fixed 18.5rem width — sized for a normal field, not a narrower nested container like the mobile Filters dropdown it now also renders inside — overflowed past that dropdown's edge, so it's narrower below the same 768px breakpoint the mobile layout itself switches on.
- **Instance Settings → Logs & audit: new "System logs" live-tail panel, next to the existing Audit log.** A superadmin can now watch a live, filterable tail of activity across the API, database, cache, mail transport, and admin maintenance actions — "know when something's about to go wrong" rather than only reconstructing it after the fact from the Audit log's per-org write history. Every entry is dual-sunk: it's still written to stdout exactly as before (Docker's own log driver remains the durability mechanism — nothing here is UI-only), and also pushed into a new in-memory, per-process ring buffer (capped at 1000 entries, reset on restart — a live-tail convenience layer, not a persistence store) exposed read-only via a new superadmin-gated `GET /api/admin/system-logs` endpoint. The panel polls every ~1.75s while **Live** (a header toggle that highlights green when active, next to the System/Audit switch), with Search, and Source/Level filters (inline on desktop, tucked behind a "Filters" dropdown on mobile, matching the Audit log's own pattern); **Download .log** exports the current view as a plain-text file, and Copy/Clear view join a "Showing N lines" footer. Only the existing HTTP access log feeds it live for now (the **API** source); Database, Cache, Mail, and Admin are wired into the UI's filters already but will start producing entries in follow-up phases — shown as available sources, not implying activity that isn't there yet. Both views (System and Audit) now stay mounted and toggle via CSS visibility instead of unmounting each other, so switching back and forth no longer re-fetches or flashes stale/empty content, and System is the tab's default view on open (Audit remains one click away). Never abbreviates "DB" to a filter label — the Source dropdown reads "Database" in full, matching the PO's own wording preference. Second pass: the **Database** and **Cache** sources now feed the buffer live too. Prisma queries slower than 200ms, plus every Prisma warning/error, show up under Database — only the parameterized query template and its duration are logged, never the bind parameters, since those can carry attendee PII; queries under the threshold aren't logged at all, to keep the 1000-entry buffer from filling with routine noise. The rate limiter's existing Redis-unreachable fail-open warning (already throttled to once per 60s) now also shows up under Cache alongside its unchanged console warning. Third pass: **Mail** and **Admin** now feed the buffer too. Every SMTP send attempt logs one event under Mail — `mail_sent` (recipient redacted, e.g. `j***@example.com`, the full address is never logged) or `mail_send_failed`. Archiving, unarchiving, and permanently deleting an event, plus exporting the audit log as CSV, each log one event under Admin — a deliberate second, ephemeral signal alongside the existing durable per-org `AdminAuditLog` row, neither replacing the other. Fourth pass (PO review — "I need to see RAW things happening, actually see something"): a sixth source, **Security**, now feeds the buffer by hooking into `emitAuditEvent`, the single function every `auth.*` event in `packages/auth/src/audit.ts` already flows through — logins, MFA (success/fail/recovery/break-glass), logout, rate-limit blocks, OIDC, access-denied, and identity-settings changes all show up with no per-call-site changes needed, alongside session revoke/bulk-revoke and the mailer's SSRF destination-block warning, newly moved here too. Fixed a real gap the PO's own testing surfaced: IP/user-agent were showing as `null` on every login because two call sites (`auth/routes.ts`, `auth/html-routes.ts`) passed a redundant, incomplete third argument to `login()` that fully shadowed its own `ip`/`userAgent` fallback — removed. Mail coverage, which had only ever instrumented the SMTP adapter, now also covers the Microsoft Graph, Power Automate, and export-only adapters (the same `mail_sent`/`mail_send_failed` pair each), plus the admin "test connection" flow's own previously-silent failure path (`mail_test_failed`). Raw ids in Admin/Security entries (`event_archived`/`event_unarchived`/`event_deleted`/`session_revoked`/`operator_sessions_bulk_revoked`/`audit_log_exported`) were unreadable on their own (PO: "I see targetUserId and have no idea who that is") — each now also carries the event's title and the actor/target's full email (resolved server-side at write time) alongside the existing raw ids, which stay for cross-referencing. PO follow-up: these fields, plus `auth.login.success`/`auth.login.fail`/`auth.cf_access`'s own email field and the `user_created` audit-log entry, previously showed a redacted address (`a***@example.com`) — now the full address, since this identifies internal staff/operators for accountability (a well-established legitimate-interest basis, not something GDPR itself prohibits), matching the per-org Audit log's own already-unredacted actor identification; explicitly unchanged and out of scope here: attendee-facing data (a mail's recipient address, import rows) stays redacted/minimized, and MFA/logout/OIDC events still use a one-way fingerprint rather than an email at all, a separate design choice not touched by this pass. UI polish (PO review, several rounds): the dark console now stays mounted with a fixed shell regardless of state — Loading/Error/no-activity render inside it instead of swapping to a separate light `EmptyState` card, so **Clear view** no longer looks like the panel broke; its height now scales with the viewport (`clamp(20rem, 60vh, 42rem)`) instead of a fixed 22rem; timestamps are always UTC (was briefly local-time-plus-abbreviation, reverted once the PO pointed out a live tail watched by people in different timezones needs one unambiguous clock); an entry's `fields` JSON always renders on its own line regardless of message length (was wrapping inconsistently, a bare flex `gap` with no `flex-basis`); consecutive entries get a subtle alternating background stripe instead of the divider line first tried (which read as a grid of rules); and within one entry the header row now flows straight into its fields line with no gap of its own — reads as one continuous block, the same way wrapped paragraph text would — while entry-to-entry spacing stays a deliberately larger, fixed gap so a multi-line entry doesn't get confused for two separate ones. Mobile message text now claims full width like fields already did (was fragmenting into 1-2 characters per line, squeezed by the fixed time/level/source columns). Fifth pass (automated review, CodeRabbit/SonarCloud): the SMTP/Power Automate/Graph mail adapters logged the raw provider error text on a send failure — a real PII risk, since an SMTP server's rejection reply or a Power Automate flow's HTTP response can echo the recipient address or message content back verbatim; the live-tail entry now logs only a stable, safe category (`SMTP <code>`, or the response's own status/error-code prefix with the free-text response body dropped) while the full detail still reaches the admin UI's own error surface via the existing return value, unchanged. `event_archived`/`event_unarchived`/`event_deleted`/`session_revoked` moved their System-logs emission (and the actor-email lookup that enriches it) to after their underlying transaction has actually committed — previously a failure enriching the log line (e.g. a transient error resolving the actor's email) could report a successful action as `audit_failed`, and logging from inside the transaction risked recording an action that transaction could still roll back. The System-logs endpoint itself, polled by the live tail's own `Live` toggle every ~1.75s, no longer feeds its own successful polls into the API access log (still logs on failure) — an idle tab left open on System logs was otherwise continuously filling the 1000-entry buffer with its own polling and evicting real diagnostics, the exact same treatment already given to the `/healthz`/`/readyz` health probes. The live-tail poll now skips a tick if the previous one is still in flight instead of overlapping requests, closing a narrow race where a slow response could resolve after a newer one and rewind the live cursor. A failed initial/filtered load now offers a **Retry** button instead of only a static message. Sixth pass (SonarCloud, second round): `archiveEvent`/`unarchiveEvent` shared one transaction+log helper instead of a byte-for-byte duplicated 32-line body each (each call site's own return type stays exactly as precise as before, via a small generic parameter); `packages/auth/src/audit.ts` re-exports `redactEmail` via `export … from` instead of a separate import+re-export pair; the console's loading/error/empty/lines ternary chain is a plain if/return helper instead of three nested ternaries; and `fetchSystemLogs`'s query-string template literal no longer nests a second template literal inside it, matching every other list-endpoint helper in this file. Seventh pass (external review): unlike Database (only slow queries/errors) and Cache (throttled to 1×/60s), a successful **Mail** send logged unconditionally — a single bulk send can be hundreds of attendees, so a mass send could push hundreds of `mail_sent` entries into the shared 1000-entry buffer, evicting Security/Admin/DB activity from exactly the window (a mass send) an operator is also most likely to want visible. `mail_sent` (success only - `mail_send_failed` is unchanged, still logged every time, since it's the rarer and more important signal) is now throttled into the buffer at most once per 60s across all four adapters, the same interval already used for the Redis fail-open warning; every send still reaches stdout unthrottled, so the durable container log (and any SIEM forwarding it) keeps a full record regardless. Eighth pass (external review): `auth.login.fail`'s email is unauthenticated login-form input, not a verified identity like a successful login or a post-JWT Cloudflare Access failure, so it now stays redacted (`b***@example.com`) instead of full — the full-email exception is specifically for confirmed staff/operator identity, which a failed attempt by definition isn't. The live poll now recovers from a server restart instead of silently going stale forever: a fresh process starts its in-memory buffer's cursor back at 1, so a poll response with a lower cursor than requested now triggers one full snapshot re-fetch (replacing the view) instead of being read as "zero new entries" and quietly never catching up. And a sustained run of poll failures (5 in a row, ~9s) now surfaces an amber banner ("Live updates stopped coming through") with a **Retry now** action, instead of leaving the **Live** pill green while the console silently stops updating — a single missed tick still isn't surfaced, only a genuine outage. Ninth pass (SonarCloud): the poll-degraded banner used `role="status"` on a `<div>` — swapped for `<output>`, which already carries that status semantics natively, matching every other status element in this codebase. Tenth pass (PO follow-up, against a mockup that assumed a day-based retention control belonged here): a persistent notice now sits below the footer, clarifying this is a live-only view — keeps the last 1,000 entries, resets on restart — and pointing to the Audit log/Security audit log for durable history, so the console isn't mistaken for a retention-configurable store the way that mockup was.
- **Admin app-wide — no more spinner/skeleton flash on near-instant loads.** A fetch that resolves faster than a user can consciously register it (localhost, a warm cache) used to still flip a panel's loading state on and off, reading as a glitch rather than an actual loading indicator — first fixed for the two Identity modals via a new `useDelayedLoading(isLoading, delayMs = 200)` hook (only shows the spinner/skeleton once loading has genuinely lasted past the delay; a fast response never shows anything, content just appears). This rollout wires the same hook into every other page and panel with its own loading state: Account, Events picker, Import (page + history card), Requirements, Event Settings (+ Ticket types tab), Event Overview, Reports, Users + Role assignments, Attendee detail, Attendees list, Communication (+ delivery log), Check-in event picker, Identity providers list, Audit log, Sessions, Security, Instance URL, Event archiving, Branding, Organisation branding, Mail transport (organization + per-event, shared `useMailSettingsFormState`), Event image asset library, Add attendee dialog, and the setup wizard's Mail/Branding steps. Left as-is: the top-level auth/session bootstrap gates (`AuthProvider`, `OperatorDeviceGate`, the app shell's route-level `Suspense` fallback) — a different risk category (blocks the entire app shell, not a single panel) than this batch's scope; the setup wizard's system-checks step, whose per-row checklist is useful to show immediately rather than delayed; and any button/action "busy" spinner (Save, Test connection, …), which isn't an initial-load flash.
- **Event Settings — dedicated mail transport per event (closes #511).** Events send through the organization's mail transport by default; a new "Mailing" tab on Event Settings now lets an admin switch a specific event to send through its own SMTP, Microsoft Graph, or Power Automate configuration instead — useful for a co-branded event or a separate sending mailbox. A simple toggle (Organization mail / Dedicated for this event) switches between the two; picking "Dedicated" starts from a blank form rather than a copy of the organization's settings, and reverting back to "Organization mail" removes the event's override entirely rather than leaving stale, unused credentials behind. The transport picker, sender fields, and secret handling (set/change/clear, never round-tripped in plaintext) reuse the same components already shipped for the organization-wide Instance Settings → Mail page, so the two screens behave identically. "Send test email" on the event's Mailing tab verifies whichever transport actually resolves for that event — its own dedicated one, or the inherited organization one — and is available to any admin who can manage the event, not just superadmins (unlike the organization-wide Mail settings, which stay superadmin-only). Deferred to a later change: managing templates for system/administrative email separately from this transport-level setting.
- **Attendees list — rows-per-page control, a single Export menu, row selection with a bulk "Send tickets" action, and a mobile-friendly layout (closes #353, #354, #355).** Operators running large events can now pick 10/25/50/100 rows per page from a compact control next to the pager instead of being stuck at a fixed 25 (jumps back to page 1 on change; the server already supported up to 100). The header's three separate Export XLSX/CSV/PDF buttons collapsed into one "Export ▾" menu, and the button order changed to Import → + Add attendee → Send tickets → Export, putting the first thing an operator does on a fresh event ahead of the exports. A checkbox per row (plus a header "select all" for the current page) reveals a bulk action bar — styled as a distinct tinted strip, replacing the search/filter row in place rather than adding height to the page — showing the selection count and a "Send tickets" action that queues tickets for exactly that subset, filling a gap where bulk send previously only offered "all" or "all undelivered." Selection always resets on any page/filter/search/page-size change or after a send. Below 768px the table becomes a one-attendee-per-card list with the same data and actions, and the three filters collapse behind a "Filters" toggle with an active-filter count badge — the Attendees list previously had no mobile layout at all. Also fixed, app-wide: the shared `PageHeader` component's title and action buttons now stack instead of crowding each other below 768px, on every admin screen that uses it. Post-review polish: the Export menu's three formats now show an icon and a one-line hint each (matching the design mockup) instead of plain text; the Mail column shows "Not sent" instead of a bare dash when no ticket email has gone out yet (same fix in the attendee detail page's Status card, which shares the badge); the Check-in column now stacks a relative day ("Today"/"Yesterday", or the full date further back) above the time, instead of one long line; and hovering a selected row while multiple are selected no longer flashes it back to plain gray (a generic table hover style was winning over the selection tint). Second polish pass: the bulk bar's "Send tickets" button now matches the toolbar's height exactly and keeps its send icon and right-hand position; the Attendees search box and its three filter selects got explicit `id`s (a missing-id/name browser warning); and the Export menu's CSV/PDF hints are now one-line, plain-English descriptions ("Plain text file" / "Ready to print"). Third polish pass: the "RSVP" column header now reads "RSVP status" to read consistently next to "Pass status"; and the rows-per-page control shrank from 100px to about 50px wide (a CSS specificity bug meant its compact-size rule was losing to the shared `Select` component's default height, so it silently stayed full-size after the first attempt to shrink it). Fourth polish pass: the bulk bar's background is now a neutral gray instead of the same blue tint used for selected rows — the two were blending into one indistinguishable blue block; the count text and Send icon stay blue so the bar still reads as active. Fifth polish pass, on the "+ Add attendee" dialog: Email no longer uses `type="email"`, which was triggering Safari's iCloud "Hide My Email" suggestion and password-manager email heuristics for a field that's never the operator's own address — it now uses the same `type="text"` + `inputMode="email"` + autofill opt-out attributes already used for every email-shaped field on the Mail settings screens; Email and Name are marked `*` as required, with a "* Required" hint next to the dialog buttons; each field got a small leading icon; and the Ticket type dropdown now matches the other fields' full width instead of being capped at 384px by a stray global `max-width: 24rem` rule meant for filter-style selects elsewhere in the app (same fix already applied once before on the Account page, now applied here too). Sixth polish pass: the dialog's title area now has a short subtitle ("Enter their email and name — everything else is optional.") instead of the required-hint sitting right under it, which is now down by the action buttons instead. And the header "Send tickets" button (not just the row-selection bulk bar's) now disables itself with a tooltip when the event has no effective mail transport — its own dedicated one, or the organization's — reusing the same resolution Event Settings → Mailing already shows and the same disabled+tooltip mechanism as the archived-event guard. Seventh polish pass: the row-selection bulk bar's "Send tickets" actually sends now, instead of being permanently disabled ("no ticket template configured") on any event without a persisted `ticket`-named template row — which was most events, since one is only created when an admin explicitly customizes the ticket email. The plain "send to everyone/undelivered" header button already worked in that situation by falling back to the built-in default ticket template server-side; the bulk-by-selection endpoint (`POST /send`) required an explicit `templateId` with no such fallback. `templateId` is now optional on that endpoint — omitted, it resolves the event's template exactly the way the simpler endpoint always has (event override → organization override → built-in default) — so both "Send tickets" entry points now behave the same way and share the same (real) blocker: whether mail is configured at all, not whether someone has customized the ticket template. Eighth polish pass: the Ticket column showed a badge-wrapped dash for attendees with no ticket type instead of the plain, unstyled dash used everywhere else in the table for missing data; a handful of tooltip and toast strings introduced during this pass dropped an unnecessary em dash in favor of a period or comma, matching the rest of the screen's plain-English tone. Also added: every sortable column — Attendee, Ticket, Company, RSVP status, Pass status, and Check-in — now has a clickable header with an up/down arrow that toggles ascending/descending, resets to page 1, and starts a newly-clicked column ascending; Mail isn't sortable since its value is resolved per-row from a separate delivery lookup, not a plain column. Ticket sorts by the ticket-type catalog's own curated `sort_order` (the same order its dropdowns already use) rather than alphabetically, so tiers group the way operators actually think about them instead of scattering "General" and "VIP" across the alphabet; attendees without a ticket type, company, or check-in time always sort to the bottom regardless of direction rather than jumping to the top on descending. Sorting by Attendee or Company is case-insensitive, matching how a human reads "A to Z" — the database's default collation is case-sensitive, so a plain sort would put every name starting with a lowercase letter after every name starting with an uppercase one (e.g. a lowercase test entry landing dead last instead of next to its alphabetical neighbors). Ninth polish pass: the three filter dropdowns read "All", "All statuses", and "All types" with nothing to tell them apart at a glance — now "All check-ins", "All RSVP statuses", and "All ticket types". And the Pass status badge's "Registered" reads the same as the unrelated RSVP status column right next to it and stays gray even after the attendee has checked in, so it's easy to read as "hasn't done anything yet" when it actually means the pass is valid and usable — relabeled to "Active" (green, matching the same word already used for non-archived events elsewhere in the app) so it plainly answers "can this attendee still get in," without reading as "already checked in" the way "Validated" would — leaving "Revoked" (red) as the only other reachable state today. Tenth polish pass: reordered both the table columns (Attendee, Company, Ticket, Pass status, RSVP status, Mail, Check-in) and the three filter dropdowns (ticket type, RSVP status, check-in) to the same sequence, matching how operators actually scan the row — identity, then affiliation, then the two independent status pairs, then delivery and attendance. Eleventh polish pass: mobile (below 768px) previously had no way to sort or select-all — it now has a "Select all" row above the cards and a "Sort by" select with a direction toggle inside the existing Filters panel, matching the desktop column headers' behavior. Sorting by Company now matches the value actually shown (custom_data.company takes precedence over the legacy column) instead of the raw column, which could put an attendee in a different position than its own displayed value implied. And the row-selection bulk bar's "Send tickets" now records the same `purpose: "initial"` as every other first-time send to attendees who've never received a ticket, instead of always `"resend"` — without this, those attendees stayed marked "undelivered" and could get emailed a second time by a later "Everyone undelivered" send. Twelfth polish pass: the empty list state was a single line of plain gray text with no visual weight — split into two variants matching the icon+title+description placeholder already used elsewhere (Activity log, Reports): "No attendees yet" with an import hint for a genuinely empty event, and a distinct "No matches" for an empty search/filter result, instead of one string trying to cover both. Thirteenth polish pass: filtering/searching to a narrower result set snapped the whole table upward the instant the shorter page of rows replaced the previous (dimmed) one, since nothing reserved space for the row count that just disappeared — the table area now keeps a ~6-row minimum height so a filter that matches far fewer attendees settles instead of visibly jumping. Fourteenth polish pass: combining two filters where the first one already matched zero attendees (e.g. a ticket type filter, then also narrowing by attendance) flashed the loading skeleton again instead of the same dim-in-place transition every other refetch gets — the skeleton is now reserved for the genuine first load, and a later filter/search landing on zero matches dims the "No matches" placeholder like any other refetch instead. Fifteenth polish pass: the round-13 min-height fix was scoped to `.attendees-table-wrap`, a class shared with Requirements' Event items and Custom attendee fields tables — both picked up the same reserved height and stretched with obvious dead space under just one or zero rows. Moved the min-height to a new Attendees-list-only class, and shrank it from ~6 rows to ~3 — enough to soften the worst-case collapse (a full page down to one match) without visibly padding out every ordinarily-sized filtered result. Sixteenth polish pass (search+filter row redesign, PO review): the below-768px "Filters" toggle/collapse mechanism described above is gone — search and a single "Filters" button now always render together in one row of fixed height at every viewport (desktop, tablet, or phone); the four filter selects (plus, below 768px, the "Sort by" control) live inside a floating panel opened from that button, with an active-filter-count badge, so the row itself never wraps or changes height no matter how many filters are active. Seventeenth polish pass: the "Sort by" control inside that panel rendered narrower than the other filter rows, leaving an unexplained gap before the panel's right edge — its outer field wrapper never picked up the flex-grow the other filter rows get for free from block layout, since the sort row alone lays its select and direction-toggle button out with `display: flex`; it now fills the panel edge-to-edge like every other row. Eighteenth polish pass: below 768px, selecting attendees swapped the search/filter toolbar for the bulk bar in the same slot at a visibly taller height (devtools measurements: 69px vs 97px, since the bulk bar's count+clear and its three action buttons wrapped onto two lines of their own) — the whole card audibly "jumped" the instant a row was checked, and settled back the instant selection was cleared. Fixed by moving "Send tickets" into the "More" menu below that width (still one tap away, just not its own button — Export selected/Change ticket type/Delete already lived there), leaving "Check in" + "More" light enough, with decorative leading icons dropped and compact pager-page-size-control sizing, to share one line with the count; and by giving both the toolbar and the bulk bar the same explicit `min-height` formula so the two can never render at different heights regardless of which controls happen to be inside. Also dropped the bulk bar's vertical separator (it only made sense sitting inline next to the count on desktop) and shortened "More actions" to "More" below 768px — its own trailing chevron survives, since that's the one icon actually signaling "this opens a menu" rather than just decorating an already-clear label. Fixing the wrap surfaced a second, previously-latent bug in the same area: the "More actions" menu panel's off-screen-clipping fix from an earlier pass anchored the panel to the trigger button's own (now differently-positioned) box, which put the panel's edge past the right side of the screen once the trigger stopped being alone on its own line. The panel now anchors to the whole button row instead of the trigger itself, so its position no longer depends on exactly where the trigger happens to land. Nineteenth polish pass: the page header's own "Import" / "+ Add attendee" / "Send tickets" / "Export" buttons had the same problem one level up — below ~640px they wrapped onto a 2nd line, changing the header's own height as the viewport narrowed (devtools measurements: 36px vs 80px). Fixed the same way as the bulk bar (compact sizing, decorative icons dropped, Export's own trailing chevron kept), plus shortening "+ Add attendee" to "+ Add" and "Send tickets" to "Send" below 768px — between the four of them, that's the only combination that fits one line without also touching "Import" or "Export", which were already short enough. Scoped to just this page (a dedicated class on its own `PageHeader` instance) rather than the shared `PageHeader` component every admin page uses, since other pages' own action-button sets haven't been reviewed for the same issue yet. Twentieth polish pass (PO review): that same header row now centers itself below 768px instead of sitting flush left with empty space trailing off to the right — the shared `PageHeader` component already left-aligns it there (a sensible default when a row can hold as many buttons as it needs), but center reads better once the buttons are also the shorter, narrower mobile set. The bulk bar's "Check in" and "More" buttons get their icons back below 768px too — dropped in the previous pass to win back space that's no longer needed now that "Send tickets" moved out of that row entirely; "Check in"'s own leading icon returns, and "More"'s trailing chevron (which never actually left) is unaffected either way. And the "More" menu's "Send tickets" and "Delete" items each gained a one-line gray hint under their title ("Email tickets to N attendees" / "Permanently remove N attendees"), matching "Export selected" and "Change ticket type" — all four items now share the same title+hint shape instead of two having it and two not. Twenty-first polish pass (PO review): restoring "Check in"'s icon (previous pass) reopened the exact height-jump bug fixed two passes ago, just one level up — "Check in" alone grew ~22px, which was enough that the count and the action buttons, while each still fitting their own single line individually, no longer fit together on the bar's one shared line, so the bar as a whole wrapped onto two lines again (69px → 97px) the instant a row was checked. Fixed with tighter gaps throughout the bar (count-to-clear, count-to-actions, and icon-to-label inside each button) and `flex-wrap: nowrap` on the bar itself — the wrap was happening one level higher than the previous fix addressed (the bar wrapping its two direct children, not a child's own buttons wrapping internally), which the previous pass's per-button-only changes couldn't catch. Re-verified with real headroom this time (~19px spare at 360px, the narrowest width tested) rather than the practically-zero margin that broke last time.

- **Attendee detail — read-only profile view with a header Edit modal, a status strip, richer Activity log entries, and extended guest information (closes #361, #364, #365).** The Overview tab's Profile card is now always read-only — Email, Ticket type, Company, Department, Added via, Registered on, in the same order as the design mockup — instead of a wall of always-editable inputs with no visual cue you're viewing rather than editing. A new **Edit** button, first in the page header (matching the design mockup), opens an edit dialog with the same fields (still including Name, which the read-only view leaves to the page's own title instead of repeating); Cancel (or clicking outside, or Escape) discards without confirming when nothing changed, or asks first (the same "Discard unsaved changes?" dialog already used when leaving the page) when it would lose an edit; Save closes the dialog on success. Archived events disable the Edit button itself, with the same tooltip used everywhere else, instead of rendering an unusable form. The header also gained a **More actions** menu (Resend ticket, for now) and the Revoke menu's items dropped the redundant word "Revoke" now that the button itself already says it — "Pass" and "Check-in", each with an icon. Replaced the sidebar "Status" card with a 5-chip status strip across the top of the page (Registration, Attendance, Ticket delivery, Check-in, Wallet), matching the mockup's layout; each chip's label and value now size to their own content instead of stretching to match the widest sibling chip, which had been making short badges (e.g. "Revoked") render abnormally wide. The Attendance chip is a plain read-only badge — the RSVP dropdown that used to sit inline there moved into the Edit dialog as a normal field, saved together with the rest of the form, since a live `<select>` embedded in an otherwise static status strip looked out of place and broke the row's layout. The Activity log's entries for profile edits, pass revoke/restore, and event-day item hand-outs now show what actually changed instead of just who changed it — e.g. "Email, Company · operator 1" or "Active → Revoked · operator 1" — without ever echoing the old/new *values* themselves, since custom fields can hold sensitive text (accessibility needs, emergency contacts). And the Overview tab surfaces more of what the API already returns: every `custom_data` entry — whether from a configured event-item field or a leftover import column — now lives in its own "Additional information" card below Profile (matching the mockup, instead of splitting configured fields into the Profile card itself), and a new "Mail delivery history" card lists every ticket-email attempt with its status, timestamp, and recipient — answering "did mail work?" without switching to the Activity log tab. Not implemented, since neither has a working backend yet: the mockup's "Event-day items" card (fulfillment/hand-out tracking already exists elsewhere in the app but isn't wired into this page) and its "Wallet pass" card with a real serial/link (no wallet-pass integration exists yet) — the Wallet chip shows a static "Not added" placeholder instead. Polish pass: the header no longer shows a "Revoked" pill next to the buttons — the Registration chip in the status strip right below already says the same thing, so the two were just duplicating each other; the **More actions** button now shows its label next to the dots icon (plus a dropdown chevron) instead of being icon-only, matching the Revoke menu's own button style; and the Edit dialog now matches the "+ Add attendee" dialog's look — a tinted circular icon in the title, a one-line subtitle, a leading icon on Email/Name/Company/Department, and the Ticket type / RSVP status dropdowns are full width instead of being capped at 384px by the same stray global rule already fixed once on Add attendee and the Account page. The Edit dialog's Email field also dropped `type="email"` in favor of the same `type="text"` + `inputMode="email"` + autofill opt-out attributes already used on Add attendee and the Mail settings screens, so Safari's iCloud "Hide My Email" prompt and password-manager email suggestions stop showing up over a field that's never the operator's own address. Hardening: `event_items` is read defensively (`detail.event_items ??
