[]`) instead of assumed present, so a stale `apps/web` dev process still running from before this field existed (it has no hot-reload) degrades to an empty Event-day items card instead of crashing the whole detail page. Second polish pass: RSVP status no longer saves itself the instant it's changed while every other field in the same dialog waits for Save changes — that mismatch was a leftover from when RSVP lived on its own outside the form (first the sidebar Status card, then the status-strip chip) and briefly flashed the whole dialog on every change once it moved inside the Edit dialog. RSVP is now a normal field in the same patch as Email/Name/Company/Department/Ticket type, submitted only on Save — one request, one "Profile saved" toast, no more flicker. Third polish pass: the Ticket delivery chip's badge dropped its leading status dot, matching the Registration and Attendance badges right next to it, which never had one — the dot was the one visual inconsistency left in the status strip. "RSVP" was renamed to **Attendance** everywhere it's user-facing (the Edit dialog's field, and the Attendees list's column header, sort option, and filter) since it's the clearer word for what operators are actually looking at — RSVP stays the internal field/API name. Added a real **Event-day items** card — the mockup's hand-out summary (e.g. "Gift bag · T-shirt size: M — Issued") that was previously listed as not implemented — reusing the same enabled-EventItem and per-attendee AttendeeItemState data the check-in flow already tracks, read-only here (no hand-out actions, unlike the check-in card). Both it and Additional information now always render, with an icon+text empty-state placeholder (matching the pattern already used for Activity log and Reports) instead of the whole card silently disappearing when there's nothing to show. Fourth polish pass: moved the Event-day items card into the sidebar, above Mail delivery history — it had landed in the main column next to Profile, but the mockup puts it on the right alongside Wallet pass. Fifth polish pass: re-pulled the exact `.att-item-row` CSS from the mockup instead of estimating it — the item label is now 500-weight at body size (was unweighted at the same small size as everything else, reading noticeably thinner than the mockup), the state text is 600-weight at extra-small size, and the icon is an 8px-radius rounded square, not a circle. The icon and state text now also distinguish `returned` from `issued`/pending as their own third look (a muted gray icon tint distinct from the pending default), matching the mockup's own three icon states instead of treating `returned` as just another shade of "done". Sixth polish pass: dropped the per-item content_fields preview (e.g. "Gift bag · T-shirt size: M") the mockup shows — with several fields configured on one item it reads as clutter next to the item name, and it duplicated the Additional information card, which already lists every custom_data field on its own. Event-day items now shows just the item name and its hand-out state. Seventh polish pass: the whole item row sat indented ~40px from the card's left edge — `.attendee-items-list` never reset the browser's default `<ul>` padding, unlike the two other lists on this same page (Mail delivery history, Activity log), which already do. Eighth polish pass: dropped the "Issued at check-in" subtitle above the item list — it didn't add anything the card title and each row's own state don't already say. And Mail delivery history's empty state is now the same icon+text placeholder used everywhere else on this page (Event-day items, Additional information, Activity log) instead of a bare line of text. Ninth polish pass: the sidebar's left edge (Event-day items / Mail delivery history) now lines up with the Check-in chip's left edge in the status strip above it, at every viewport width — both grids share the same `repeat(5, 1fr)` track and 12px gap, with Profile/Additional information spanning the first 3 columns and the sidebar the last 2, instead of the sidebar being a fixed 380px that only happened to roughly line up at one particular window width. Tenth polish pass: "Registered on", Mail delivery history's timestamps, and every Activity log row (not just on-site check-in/item actions — profile edits, mail sends, imports too) now show in the event's own timezone instead of UTC — an admin managing an event travels, so a fixed UTC (or worse, the admin's own browser-local time) either forces manual conversion or drifts as they move between timezones; the event's own timezone is the one stable reference that stays meaningful regardless of where the admin currently is. Eleventh polish pass: the Tenth pass' "always the event's timezone" turned out to be wrong too — an admin in Zurich (CEST) managing a Bangalore event wants that edit to read as CEST, since that's when and where they actually made it, while a later on-site check-in they do in Bangalore should read as IST, permanently, even once they're back in Zurich looking at the log. Neither a fixed UTC, nor the event's timezone, nor the viewer's current browser clock satisfies both halves of that. "Registered on", every Mail delivery history row, and every Activity log entry now capture and permanently display the acting browser's own IANA timezone at the moment of the write (a new `client_timezone` column on `Attendee`, `EmailDelivery`, and `AttendeeActionLog`, sent by the admin SPA via an `X-Client-Timezone` request header) instead of applying one global rule after the fact; rows written before this change, or by the rare non-browser path, keep falling back to the event's own timezone exactly as the Tenth pass left them. Twelfth polish pass: on an archived event, hovering the disabled Edit/Revoke/More actions buttons in the page header showed a tooltip clipped almost entirely out of view against the top bar instead of next to the button — those controls sit right under the header, inside the same scrollable container whose top edge was cutting off the default upward-popping tooltip, the identical bug already fixed once for the Attendees toolbar. They now use the same `placement="below"` opt-in. Thirteenth polish pass: added the mockup's still-missing wallet card — deliberately titled just **Wallet**, not "Wallet pass" as the mockup has it, since "Pass" already means this app's own QR admission pass everywhere else on this page (the Revoke menu's "Pass" item, the Registration chip); pairing "Wallet pass" there would read as a second, different pass rather than the same Apple/Google Wallet delivery the status-strip Wallet chip already refers to. Sits in the main column below Additional information, not the sidebar, matching how Profile/Additional information already group the attendee's own data separately from Event-day items/Mail delivery history. Same icon+text empty-state placeholder as the rest of the page, since wallet delivery has no backend yet — not a real conditional empty state like Mail delivery history's. Fourteenth polish pass: Additional information showed a `boolean`-type custom field as the raw lowercase string its Select control writes ("true"/"false") instead of a readable "Yes"/"No" — every other field type already showed something a guest could recognize (a size, a dietary note), so the one Yes/No question on the form stood out as raw data. Also normalizes "yes"/"no"/"1"/"0", the same aliases the form's own validation already accepts, in case a CSV import wrote one of those directly. Fifteenth polish pass, three Activity log fixes together: a changed custom field (e.g. "Niepełnosprawność") showed a mangled, diacritics-stripped guess at its name instead of the real configured label — the field-change list only ever humanized the raw `source_field` key, which had already lost its diacritics at slug time (`niepe_nosprawnosc`) and can't recover them; it now looks up the event's custom-field registry first, same as Additional information already does, and only falls back to humanizing for a field with no registry entry. Every icon rendered in the same dark tone regardless of outcome, making the log hard to scan for what actually went wrong — icons now pick up the status-strip chips' own ok/warn/error/neutral color vocabulary (green for check-in/item-issued/pass-restored, red for revoked/bounced, amber for a tentative RSVP, gray for everything routine). And the timestamp column now uses the same monospace font as "Registered on" and Mail delivery history, matching the mockup, instead of the page's regular text face. Sixteenth polish pass: **More actions → Resend ticket** now disables with the same explanatory tooltip as the Attendees list's **Send tickets** button when the event has no working mail transport (same effective-transport check, provider `smtp`/`graph`/`powerautomate` only — a saved but non-delivering `export_only` provider doesn't count as configured either) — previously only the bulk-send path was gated, so a single resend from this page would just fail after the operator already went through opening the dialog and typing a recipient. Scoped to the one item, not the **More actions** trigger itself, since future menu entries (#356) may have nothing to do with mail. Seventeenth polish pass: an item hand-out's Activity log headline always read "Badge/Gift bag issued" — a leftover from before Event-day items became a configurable registry, so any other configured item (e.g. "Gratis") got a headline naming two items that weren't actually issued. Now reads generically as "Item issued", matching "Item returned"/"Item reset to pending" right next to it - the detail line already names the specific item. That detail line itself also now looks up the item's real configured label the same way the custom-field fix above does, instead of only ever humanizing its raw key. Eighteenth polish pass: moved the acting admin's name out of the detail line (where it read as just another piece of the diff, e.g. "Email · Filip Chochoł") to sit under the timestamp instead, with a small person icon - "who" now reads as its own thing next to "when", not mixed into "what changed". A row with nothing else to show (e.g. a plain QR check-in) no longer shows a bare name where the diff text would otherwise be. Nineteenth polish pass: profile edits now show real before/after values for a fixed safe subset of fields — Email, Company, Department, Ticket type — e.g. "Company: Old Co → New Co", matching how Pass/RSVP changes already show their transition. Deliberately doesn't extend this to Name or any `custom_data` field: #364's original PII-safety reasoning stands for those (custom fields can hold accessibility needs, emergency contacts, or other sensitive free text a guest typed in), so an edit to any of those still shows only the field name, same as before. The backend now records each safe field's before/after value alongside the existing changed-field-name list when writing the `attendee_edited` log entry; rows written before this shipped just show the field name, like an unsupported field always has. Twentieth polish pass (bot review): a generic save failure in the Edit dialog rendered its error message on the page behind the still-open modal, completely hidden by its backdrop — moved inside the modal itself. Exiting the dialog via Save on an unchanged form (no fields actually differ from the loaded value) now clears any stale error the same way Cancel already did, instead of only closing the dialog. The stale-write catch no longer also fires a toast alongside the modal's own inline warning + Reload button — one actionable surface, not two for the same error. A long, unbreakable Profile value (e.g. an imported email) now wraps inside its row instead of pushing the card wider. Twenty-first polish pass (bot review): QR check-in and scan-preview Activity log rows never captured the acting device's timezone — the check-in route resolved `X-Client-Timezone` correctly but dropped it when handing off to the shared check-in package, so those rows always fell back to the event's timezone even once a device's own zone is wired up in a later pass. Documented and clarified in [DATA-PROTECTION.md](DATA-PROTECTION.md): the Nineteenth pass' before/after value logging (email/company/department/ticket type) is a deliberate accountability record distinct from routine application log lines, already covered by the same cascade-delete erasure as the rest of an attendee's data — not an oversight, and not extended to `name` or `custom_data` (GDPR Art. 9 special-category data risk stands for those).

- **Event-day weather** on admin/operator event list cards and the public ticket page (ADR 0040). Choose **MET Norway** (default, no API key; User-Agent from Support contact) or **Open-Meteo** under **Organisation Settings → External services**. Forecast horizon is about 9 days (MET Norway) or up to 16 days (Open-Meteo); further out shows “Forecast available N days before the event”. Attribution matches the provider. Health check labels **Weather, MET Norway** or **Weather, Open-Meteo**. Free Open-Meteo host is non-commercial; commercial deploys need a customer key, self-hosted base URL, MET Norway, or weather disabled. **Maps** uses the same panel layout, with a provider select (OpenStreetMap / Nominatim today). Maps enablement, tile URL, attribution, max zoom, and geocoding base URL are controlled in that tab (not deploy env): `LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED` and related `MAP_TILE_*` / geocoding env toggles no longer drive live behaviour; disable maps in the UI if you previously relied on `LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED=false`. Geocoding HTTP timeout (`GEOCODING_TIMEOUT_MS`) remains an optional infra setting. **Test connection** next to each Provider probes the draft settings without saving. Save with no edits shows an info toast instead of doing nothing.

- **Location tab: Pin wrong? Fix link.** When the OpenStreetMap pin is right but the generated Google/Apple Maps deep link opens the wrong place, admins can paste corrected Maps URLs. Overrides are used for Copy links, the public browser ticket, and mail `{{google_maps_url}}` / `{{apple_maps_url}}`; the pin, address grid, and static map image are unchanged. Moving the pin or selecting a new venue search result clears the overrides.
- **Mail templates can now include event location and map tokens:** `{{event_address}}`, `{{directions_text}}`, `{{accessibility_text}}`, `{{event_map_url}}`, `{{google_maps_url}}`, and `{{apple_maps_url}}`. Map and directions URLs render only when the event has a saved map pin.
- **Public static map PNG for events with a saved pin** (`GET /m/{eventId}.png`): server-composites OpenStreetMap/CARTO tiles (same `MAP_TILE_URL` as the Location tab, no API key) with Redis/in-memory cache, for the ticket “Getting there” section and mail `{{event_map_url}}`. `ResolvedTicket` now carries full `EventLocation` fields (coordinates, address, directions, accessibility) for attendee-facing surfaces.
- **Browser tickets now show Getting there details:** tickets with a saved event location show its address, a static map when a pin is available, Google Maps and Apple Maps links, and any directions or accessibility notes. The redesigned ticket also uses inline event icons, disabled visual-only Apple/Google Wallet badges, and a safe shortened ticket reference where available.
- **Event settings has a new Location tab** for setting a venue's name/address, map coordinates, and directions/accessibility notes. The venue name here is now the only place an event's location is set — the separate, shorter "Location" field previously on the General tab's "Basic information" card is removed, since it duplicated the same information without the address/map detail. A single search field (styled after check-in's own attendee typeahead) suggests matching venues and addresses as you type — by name ("Convention Center") or by address, either works — and picking a suggestion fills in the map coordinates too; typing free text with no match still works, no separate "search" step or button. The same search field is reused on the Create event dialog and the setup wizard's event step, so a venue can be searched right when the event is created, not just afterward on the Location tab. An interactive map (click or drag the pin) lets you fine-tune the pin afterward, or the location can be typed by hand with no map at all. Uses OpenStreetMap tiles; no API keys required, self-hosted-friendly. An instance can turn off the map display entirely (`LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED=false`) while keeping search working, since the two are independent. The existing organization Support contact fields (Instance settings → General) are now also used to build an identifiable contact string for outbound geocoding requests, per Nominatim's usage policy — a superadmin gets a prompt to fill them in from the Location tab if they're still empty.

- **Users & roles: internal staff phone number.** Edit user's Profile section gained an optional country-code picker plus a number field, saved via the existing profile PATCH. The picker is a searchable flag+name+code combobox (type a country name or dial code) covering every ISO country/territory (`countries-list`, already resolved in the tree via `ip-location-api` so this adds no new package), not a plain `<select>` with a hand-picked shortlist. For internal contact only - not shown on tickets or any public/attendee-facing page. New nullable `phone_country_code`/`phone_number` columns on `User`. Invite user now offers the same optional phone number field (same picker, same "internal contact only" hint), saved on the initial `POST /api/admin/users` instead of requiring a follow-up edit; its Initial role, Organization scope, and Event scope fields also switched from plain `<select>` elements to the same searchable combobox Edit user already uses, and the two genuinely required fields (Email address, Temporary password) are now marked with a `*` in their label, matching the existing `label="Foo *"` convention already used elsewhere in the admin app (e.g. Add attendee, New event) rather than introducing a new required-field pattern.
- **Users & roles: delete staff account and change email (superadmin).** A "Danger zone" section in the Edit user modal adds a **Delete account** action (confirm dialog, permanently removes the account, sessions, roles, and 2FA: blocked for your own account and the last *active* superadmin; an already-deactivated superadmin can be deleted even if they're currently the only one, since removing them doesn't change the active count), and the Email address field is now editable there (blocked on a duplicate email, same conflict handling as Invite user). Saving a profile edit without actually changing the email now records `user_profile_updated` in Audit history, not `user_email_changed`. The PATCH always includes the current email, so only an actual change is logged as one.
- **Users & roles KPI tiles.** Staff users, Via SSO, MFA coverage, and Active sessions counts above the tabs, matching the tile sizing already shared between Overview and Reports. An **Audit history** header button links to Settings → Logs. Counts come from a new instance-wide `GET /api/admin/users/stats` (superadmin only), independent of the paginated/filtered Staff users list.
- **Branding upload orphan cleanup (interim).** Cancelled crop sessions, replaced logos/headers/fonts, and deleted event image assets remove the unused `/uploads/…` files when safe. Direct DELETE refuses URLs still stored on any organisation/event branding surface, theme fonts, or image assets (including cross-scope reuse). Applied logo and open font-modal files stay provisional until Save or discard. Replacement cleanup re-checks all branding scopes before unlinking and skips deletion if that check fails, so a successful save never becomes a misleading error.
- **Audit log, Security log, and Active sessions now show which country an IP address resolved to**, right under the address (a flag + country name, or "Internal network" for a private/loopback address like `127.0.0.1`). Resolved entirely offline at request time from a small dataset baked into the app at build time — no MaxMind account, no live third-party lookup, and nothing new stored (computed from the IP already on each row, so historical entries get it automatically too).
- **Instance Settings → Logs & audit: new "Security audit log" panel — durable, database-backed auth/security event trail (`SecurityAuditLog`).** Login success/failure, MFA success/failure, MFA break-glass override, MFA recovery-code use, logout, OIDC login success, OIDC superadmin-revoke-blocked, and access-denied now write a durable row (event type, resolved user when known, IP, small metadata object, timestamp) in addition to reaching stdout and the existing in-memory System-logs live tail. Closes a durability gap identified in a security review (#473): previously those ten event types were only ever visible via container log shipping (entirely operator-dependent) or the 1000-entry live tail, which is wiped on every restart — this table survives a restart and is superadmin-gated, paginated, and filterable by event type and date range, next to the existing Audit log panel. A failed login keeps the same enumeration-safe shape already used elsewhere (no `user_id`, redacted email in metadata); a successful, authenticated event records the full email, matching this project's existing staff-accountability exception. Retention is **product-automated** (unlike the operator-run admin audit log below): purged after **30 days** by default (`SECURITY_AUDIT_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`), running at container startup and on the existing daily retention sidecar alongside auth-session and mail-snapshot cleanup, plus a new `purge-security-audit-log` CLI command for manual runs.
- **Central admin audit log (`AdminAuditLog`) coverage sweep (#473).** A handful of admin-facing mutations that previously had no audit trail entry now write one: creating, updating, toggling, and running OIDC discovery on an identity provider, and updating Cloudflare Access settings (`identity_provider_created`/`_updated`/`_toggled`/`_discovered`, `identity_cf_access_updated`); completing first-run instance setup (`instance_setup_completed`); uploading an organization's branding logo or an event-scoped branding image (`org_branding_logo_uploaded`, `event_branding_uploaded`); and actually completing a **forced** password change (`must_change_password`, e.g. right after an admin-initiated reset) — previously only the admin's own reset action was recorded, not whether the affected user ever completed it (reuses the existing `account_password_changed` action type with `metadata.forced: true`). All new action types render with friendly labels in the Audit log panel instead of falling through to a raw snake_case string.
- **System logs coverage sweep: public-ticket/API errors, check-in/bulk operations, staff administration/settings, and identity/rate-limiting.** Unhandled API exceptions, ticket-resolution failures, and QR-generation/lookup failures; rejected check-ins, unexpected operator-action failures, bulk attendee-operation failures, and the two event-wide Danger Zone revokes; event create/update, organization-branding changes, staff-account lifecycle and role changes, MFA/password/session interventions, and system-settings updates; and OIDC/Cloudflare Access configuration failures plus admin/check-in throttle scope — all now emit safe live-tail entries in the superadmin System logs live tail after their durable write commits. Staff-accountability entries include verified actor (and target) identity where applicable; every entry excludes attendee data, configuration values, credentials, scan values, token-bearing URLs, and exception text.
- **Reports page — mockup-aligned redesign: stat tiles, hourly chart, and two new breakdown cards (closes #382, #383, #384).** The four KPI tiles (Total attendees / Admitted / No-shows / Peak hour) now use a compact icon-circle layout scoped to this page, instead of the shared `Stat` component used on 6 other pages. The hourly chart is taller and shows every hour label instead of hiding every other one. The page subtitle is now a fixed "Admission statistics and event-day analytics" instead of repeating the event title/date already shown in the event header (closes #382). The admission log's ticket-type filter moved from a separate toolbar row into the card's own header (closes #384). A new **Check-in details** section adds an **Attendance confirmation** card (RSVP status breakdown, reusing the Attendees list's existing Registered/Confirmed/Tentative/Declined/Cancelled labels and colors), a **Check-in method** card (QR scan vs. manual search, from valid check-ins), and a **By device** card ranking each operator-labeled device/station by admissions handled. Polish pass (PO review): the hourly chart's bars now grow into place on page load instead of snapping straight to their final height, and the peak hour's bar renders in a visibly darker shade than the rest; the separate Export CSV/Export PDF buttons collapsed into one "Export report ▾" dropdown, matching the Attendees list's own Export menu pattern; the admission log's ticket-type filter select shrank to the same compact control height used by every other Card-header action in the app (was rendering ~8px taller, making that one header noticeably bulkier than its neighbors); and the originally-planned static **Wallet adoption** placeholder (Google Wallet isn't implemented in Admitto, Apple Wallet via PassCreator only) was dropped in favor of the more actionable **By device** breakdown above, once the PO pointed out a wallet-platform card with no real data wasn't worth the space. Second polish pass (PO review): the admission log's ticket-type filter text was clipping against the shorter control box introduced in the first pass — a second, more specific `.at-select` rule (not the one originally checked) bakes in vertical padding sized for the default 36px control height, leaving almost no room for a 13px line at the shrunk 28px height; fixed by zeroing that padding and centering the text via line-height instead, with the header staying the same height as before. The admission log's Attendee column now links each name/email to that attendee's detail page (`/admin/events/:eventId/attendees/:attendeeId`), matching the same link already used in the Overview page's Recent activity feed. The Export report menu's CSV/PDF descriptions no longer wrap to two lines (panel widened to fit). **Live updates (ADR 0014):** the Reports page now subscribes to the same admin check-in SSE stream `EventOverviewPage`/`CheckInPage` already use — a live check-in immediately bumps the Admitted/No-shows KPI tiles (with the same admit-dedup guard against a duplicate SSE delivery), then a debounced ~3s reconcile fetch replaces the whole page's data with the server's fully recomputed aggregates (hourly chart, all three breakdown cards, admission log) — recomputing all of that from individual SSE events client-side wasn't worth the complexity, the same call already made for the Overview page's own live feed. An always-on "live" badge (reusing the existing pulsing dot-badge pattern) sits next to the hourly chart. Verified against a real check-in, not just the SSE mock in tests: admitted/no-shows, the ticket-type/RSVP/check-in-method breakdowns, and the admission log all updated within the reconcile window. Third polish pass (PO review): the admission log gained a **Rows per page** control (25/50/100/200, was fixed at 50), matching the Attendees list's own pagesize control exactly; every hour label on the chart now reads as a plain 2-digit number instead of "HH:00", and the peak hour's own label renders bold and dark instead of blending into the row of muted gray labels next to it — both were hard to read together, especially the one label the "peak at HH:00" subtitle above is actually pointing at; the three breakdown cards' progress bars now grow into place on page load, the same treatment the hourly chart's bars already got; admission log table cells vertically center against the two-line Attendee column instead of top-aligning, so the single-line columns don't look stranded near the top of a taller row; the ticket-type filter select was missing an `id`/`name` attribute (Chrome DevTools' Issues panel flagged it as an autofill accessibility gap) — fixed, and its CSS was hardened to the same two-class `.at-select.<page-class>` pattern the Attendees list's own pagesize select already uses, so which rule wins no longer depends on CSS bundle order. **Mobile (< 768px):** the hourly chart no longer squeezes every column down to an illegible sliver as the viewport narrows — it now scrolls horizontally at a fixed, readable bar width instead, the same "scroll instead of squish" choice already used for the admission log table; the admission log itself now renders as one-card-per-admission below the desktop breakpoint (reusing the shared `useIsDesktop()` hook the Attendees list's own table/cards split already uses), instead of a 5-column table requiring constant horizontal scrolling on a phone. Tablet width (≥768px) is unaffected — confirmed both breakpoints render correctly. Fourth polish pass (PO review): the hourly chart's hour labels and per-hour counts were unreadable — the hour label was absolutely positioned to the same bottom baseline as the bar's own bottom edge, so it rendered underneath/inside the bar instead of below it, especially for the peak bar; rebuilt as a plain top-to-bottom flow (count, then a fixed-height contour track behind every bar so even very short bars stay visually anchored, then the label) with no absolute positioning, and the peak hour's count now bolds the same way its label already did. Fixing that also surfaced a real mobile layout bug: `.reports-panels`'s single-column grid track below 960px had no `minmax(0, …)` floor, so it stretched to the chart's full un-scrolled width instead of clamping to the viewport, and the whole page scrolled horizontally instead of just the chart — fixed by clamping the track to `minmax(0, 1fr)`. The admission log's ticket-type filter moved out of the card header into a "Filters ▾" button + floating panel (matching the Attendees list's own Filters menu), which now also filters by device — simpler on narrow screens than inline selects, and an active-filter-count badge shows on the trigger. On mobile cards, the device and issued-items fields were two bare strings back to back with nothing marking where one ended and the other began (e.g. "scanner-01 Badge, Gift bag" read as one string); each now gets its own icon prefix. Fifth pass (independent PR #587 review): `visibleHourRange()` still cropped a sparse dataset down to just non-zero-hours ±1 hour — for example two check-ins 2 hours apart rendered as only 5 columns adrift in the card's full width, the exact symptom #383 was filed against — now pads out symmetrically to a 9-hour minimum (the mockup's own 08-16 example window) before falling back to the ±1 crop, so this actually closes #383 instead of only fixing its height/label symptoms. The No-shows tile's "% of total" rounded to a whole percent while the Admitted tile's "% admission rate" right next to it used one decimal place — aligned to the same precision. The admission log's page number is now clamped to the last real page instead of raw state — if a live reconcile shrinks the filtered/paged set out from under an operator sitting on page 2+ (e.g. an admission gets revoked elsewhere mid-session), it now falls back to the last page that still has rows instead of slicing past the end and showing "No admissions match the filter" despite matching data existing. Sixth pass (`/code-review` xhigh, 15 confirmed findings, all fixed): `by_checkin_method`/`by_device` counted every `VALID` check-in row, not every currently-admitted attendee — an operator revoke never flips the original row's own status (only inserts a separate `UNDO` row), so a revoke-then-re-admit cycle left 2+ `VALID` rows for one attendee and could push these breakdowns' totals past `summary.admitted`; now deduped to each attendee's earliest `VALID` row, matching the admission log's own per-row Device convention. The debounced SSE reconcile fetch wasn't tied to the viewed event's lifecycle — switching events in-SPA within the 3s debounce window (this route doesn't remount on an eventId-only navigation) could let a stale fetch silently overwrite the newly-loaded event's data a few seconds later; now cancelled/aborted on an event switch, the same way the initial load already was. That same reconcile also had no `AbortController` of its own (an in-flight fetch could still call `setState` after unmount) and unconditionally zeroed the optimistic live-count delta on resolve, briefly undercounting if a second check-in arrived while the first reconcile's fetch was still in flight — both fixed. The hourly chart's zero-count hours (deliberately padded into view by the fifth pass's `MIN_VISIBLE_HOURS` fix) rendered as a solid 2px sliver in the same blue as a real admission, since the bar's count-based color conditional was dropped during the track/contour rewrite — the bar element itself is now only rendered for hours with a real count, leaving just the empty gray track for the rest. `visibleHourRange`'s chart also had no horizontal scroll above the mobile breakpoint, so a densely-populated event (many active hours) could visually overflow the card at tablet/desktop widths — `overflow-x: auto` is now the chart's base behavior, not mobile-only. The three breakdown cards (Attendance confirmation / Check-in method / By device) rounded percentages to a whole number while the KPI tiles next to them used one decimal place — aligned via a shared `pctOf()` helper. `rsvpBreakdownRows` only ever showed the 5 known RSVP statuses with no catch-all, unlike the ticket-type breakdown's own "(not in catalog)" handling for the same class of legacy data — an attendee with an out-of-range `rsvp_status` now gets its own fallback row instead of silently vanishing from the card. The empty-state/dashboard gate checked the raw (not-yet-reconciled) admitted count, so the very first live check-in of an empty event didn't clear "No check-ins yet" for the ~3s reconcile window — now gated on the same optimistic `liveAdmitted` value the KPI tiles already use. The admission log's Previous/Next buttons computed the next page from raw (unclamped) page state instead of the same `safePage` value already driving their own `disabled` condition, so a click could silently do nothing once page had drifted past the clamp. Consolidating CSV/PDF into one Export menu had also made the whole trigger's `disabled` state block reaching PDF while a CSV export was in flight, where the two were independently clickable before — the CSV item alone now shows its own busy state. `HourlyChart` and `BreakdownRows` each had an identical grow-on-mount animation bug: the second `requestAnimationFrame`'s cleanup was returned from the inner rAF callback instead of the `useEffect` itself, so it silently never ran — fixed once via a new shared `useMountAnimation()` hook both now use instead of two separate copies of the same (buggy) effect. The Filters dropdown (ticket type + device) introduced in the fifth pass turned out to structurally duplicate the Attendees list's own Filters menu (trigger button, fieldset panel, active-count badge, `useDropdownMenu` wiring) — both now share one `FiltersMenu` component (`apps/admin/src/components/FiltersMenu.tsx`) instead of two hand-maintained copies of the same pattern. And an integration test asserting the CSV export's Items column was empty for an attendee with nothing issued was only checking that *some* cell on the row was an empty quoted string — since that attendee also happened to have no Device, the assertion would have passed even if Items itself were broken; now the row has a populated Device and the assertion checks the line's own trailing cell specifically. Seventh pass (CodeRabbit automated review): the sixth pass's earliest-`VALID`-row dedup for `by_checkin_method`/`by_device` was itself incomplete on two counts — it never excluded an attendee who was revoked and never re-admitted (their original `VALID` row is left untouched by a revoke, so they still counted in these breakdowns despite `summary.admitted` correctly excluding them), and it attributed to the wrong row on a genuine revoke-then-re-admit cycle (the *first*/earliest check-in, when `attendee.admitted_at` — the same field `summary.admitted` and the admission log both key off — always reflects the *latest* admission); both queries (the aggregate query and the pre-existing `loadDeviceIdsByAttendee` used by the admission log's own per-row Device column) now filter to `attendee.admitted_at IS NOT NULL` and order latest-first, so a revoked-only attendee is excluded entirely and a re-admitted one attributes to their current device/method, not their original one. `loadData` could also race a still-in-flight debounced reconcile: reloading the whole page (e.g. switching ticket-type filters) reset the optimistic live-count delta to 0, but a reconcile that had already captured its own pre-reset delta snapshot would still subtract it once its fetch resolved, driving the delta negative and briefly under-counting Admitted/No-shows; `loadData` now cancels any pending/in-flight reconcile before it resets. The `FiltersMenu` extraction's trigger carried `aria-haspopup="true"` — the ARIA spec treats bare `"true"` as equivalent to `"menu"`, misleading for a disclosure button revealing a `<fieldset>` of filter controls rather than an actual menu — removed in favor of `aria-expanded` alone, the same fix applied to the Attendees list's own Filters trigger since both share this component. `by_rsvp_status[].status` was typed as the narrow `RsvpStatus` union even though the backend returns a free-form `String` column value, which could include a legacy/orphaned status outside the known 5 — retyped to `string`, matching how the renderer already buckets an unknown value into its own fallback row. The Export report menu's per-item hover background had no `:focus-visible` pairing, unlike every other menu item pattern in the app, so keyboard-only navigation showed no visible highlight while tabbing through Export CSV/Export PDF — added. Two new integration test cases cover the revoke-without-readmit exclusion and the corrected latest-row attribution on revoke-then-readmit; the existing revoke-then-readmit test's expected device/method now matches the second (later) check-in instead of the first, per the ordering fix above.
- **Event Overview dashboard — mockup-aligned redesign: setup checklist, check-in progress, and a live activity feed (closes #348).** The former "Needs attention" and "Event readiness" cards are now one compact **Setup checklist** card — an `okCount/total` pill plus only the top not-ok items, with a link straight into Event Settings, instead of two full-height cards eating the left column. A new **Check-in progress** card shows a conic-gradient admission ring (checked-in vs. not-yet, with counts), a ticket-type breakdown bar when an event has more than one active type, and "Last check-in" / "Busiest hour" glance stats, all backed by the `last_check_in_at` / `busiest_hour` / `ticket_type_breakdown` fields already added to the overview API. **Recent check-ins** is replaced by **Recent activity**: a day-grouped timeline (Today / Yesterday / date) merging check-ins, mail failures/bounces, and import batches from the new `recent_activity` feed, with an All/Issues filter — and it now hydrates from the initial page load/refresh instead of showing nothing until the next live check-in (closes #373). Live SSE check-ins still prepend on top instantly, deduped against the feed once the server catches up. Follow-up polish (PO review): the wide-left/narrow-sidebar grid is now two genuinely symmetric two-column rows (Check-in progress/Recent activity, then Setup checklist/Notes & contacts), and the former Pinned note, Key contacts, and Important links & files cards are merged into one **Notes & contacts** card with three labeled sub-sections instead of three separate card borders. Second polish pass (PO review): Key contacts and Links & files headers gained a leading icon matching Pinned note's so all three read as one family, the pinned note empty state is now a dashed "Add a pinned note for staff" button instead of a plain sentence, section headers no longer shift between empty/filled states, and the pinned note / contact / link add-edit forms now open as modal dialogs — reusing this SPA's existing modal pattern — instead of expanding inline inside the card. Third polish pass (PO review): the Notes & contacts section divider now stays put regardless of pinned-note state or an open edit modal, and per-row borders in Key contacts / Links & files are gone in favor of spacing (fixing the "looks like a spreadsheet" feedback); the contact and link/file forms gained leading field icons matching the Attendees add flow, the contact Email field suppresses password-manager/Hide-My-Email autofill, the link/file Type select is no longer cramped, an invalid URL now gets a specific inline message instead of a generic failure, and edit/delete icons on Links & files rows stay vertically centered whether or not a row has a description. Recent activity now caps at a 312px scrolling height instead of growing with the sibling Check-in progress card, the All/Issues filter moved into the card header next to a now slow-pulsing "live" dot, and check-in/mail entries link straight to the attendee's detail page. Check-in progress's glance tiles gained clock/trending icons and the ticket-type bar a "By ticket type" label. Setup checklist rows are now circular status icon + two-line title/detail, matching the mockup instead of a flat label/value row. Fourth polish pass (PO review): Recent activity's scroll container now holds a fixed height instead of a max-height, so the card no longer visibly shrinks when the All/Issues filter narrows the list down to a couple of items; the All/Issues filter is now a pill-shaped segmented control and the "live" indicator reuses the app's existing dot-badge pattern (`Badge variant="ok" dot`), replacing the previous plain bordered-button pair and bare dot+text; and the four KPI tiles (Attendees / Tickets sent / Checked in / Failed delivery) now use a bigger icon-square-left layout matching the mockup instead of the generic `Stat` component's small icon-circle-top-right, scoped to this page only. Fifth polish pass (PO review): Recent activity's scroll container reserves an 8px gutter so its scrollbar no longer crowds each row's timestamp, and a filter with zero matches now shows a centered icon+text empty state instead of a top-left line over dead space; Check-in progress and Recent activity's headers are pinned to the same height so the two align in their shared row regardless of one carrying extra filter-pill/live-badge content; the "live" badge now always renders instead of being gated behind the SSE handshake state, which made it flicker away right after page load or during a reconnect; each activity row's relative time now shows the absolute date/time below it in a smaller muted line; and the KPI row's "Checked in" tile — duplicating the Check-in progress card directly below it — is replaced by a **Days to event** countdown tile (reusing the existing `event-countdown` util), with all 4 tiles getting a bigger icon square and larger bold value so the row reads less sparse. Sixth polish pass (PO review): the Days-to-event tile drops its redundant "days to go"/"days ago" sub-line (it also broke the KPI row's icon alignment, being the tile's only 3-line block) — direction for the far-past numeric case now comes from the label itself ("Days since event") instead. Check-in progress and Recent activity now always render at equal height in their shared row (the grid was opting out of the default stretch behavior), with Check-in progress's own content spreading out to fill the extra space instead of leaving a gap underneath. Recent activity's list no longer shifts width when its scrollbar appears or disappears (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`), and the scrollbar itself is now slim and on-theme instead of the browser default. Seventh polish pass (PO review): the checklist link's and Recent activity's attendee-name link's "no underline on hover" fix from earlier passes never actually worked — both had dropped `text-decoration: underline` in favor of a color-only hover style, but never re-declared `text-decoration: none` there, so the app-wide `a:hover { text-decoration: underline }` rule kept winning on specificity once hovering; both now explicitly set `text-decoration: none` alongside their hover color. The contact Email field's Safari "Hide My Email" fix similarly needed one more step: `type="email"` triggers it regardless of `autocomplete`/`data-*` opt-outs, so the field now uses `type="text"` + `inputMode="email"`, matching Add attendee's own field. And Recent activity's check-in rows now use the same action-colored icon circle as every other row type (green `ti-user-check`) instead of an avatar with the attendee's initials, so the whole feed reads with one consistent visual language instead of switching between avatars and icons per row. Eighth polish pass (automated PR review): Recent activity's merge of live SSE check-ins into the server feed now sorts the combined list newest-first and caps it at the same 30-item contract as the server response, instead of always pinning not-yet-reconciled live check-ins above the server's own rows — on an event left open long enough for 30+ newer activities to push a check-in out of the server's own capped window before the next reconcile, the unsorted merge could otherwise strand that stale check-in above genuinely newer entries indefinitely (CodeRabbit); plus a round of internal code-quality cleanup (readonly component props, explicit button types, reduced nested-ternary branching and cognitive complexity, JSX whitespace) raised by SonarCloud and CodeRabbit static analysis, no behavior change; SonarCloud's suggestion to replace this page's `role="dialog"`/`role="presentation"` modal markup with a native `<dialog>` element was left as-is since it's the same established pattern used by every other modal in the admin app (16 other components), not something this PR introduced. Ninth polish pass (PO review): Key contacts and Links & files now show the same dashed "+ Add a key contact" / "+ Add a link or file" empty-state tile as Pinned note instead of a plain "No contacts yet."/"No links or files yet." line, with the header-level Add button now appearing only once the section has at least one row (consistent with Pinned note's own Edit-only-once-filled header action); and Check-in progress shows the same icon+text empty state as Recent activity ("No attendees yet") instead of a permanently 0% admission ring once an event has zero attendees imported. Recent activity's live-check-in reconcile now matches server rows by attendee_id instead of attendee name + timestamp — SSE's admittedAt (the app's own clock at admit time) and the server's occurred_at (CheckIn.checked_in_at's DB-side default) never line up exactly, so the old string match always missed and left a duplicate row for the same check-in visible after every reconcile (CodeRabbit). Tenth polish pass (PO review): the Days to event tile's label now reads "Event countdown" whenever its value is a countdown phrase ("In 7 days", "Tomorrow", "Ended 3 days ago") instead of the static "Days to event", which repeated a "days" already spelled out in the value and, for an event that ended within the past week, kept saying "to event" even once the value read "Ended N days ago" — "Days to event"/"Days since event" now only appear once the value itself is a bare number that needs a unit, beyond that same 7-day window.
- **Check-in scan outcome feedback: a beep and (where supported) a short vibration on every scan.** Operators scanning at a busy door previously had visual-only feedback — a success beep for a valid scan, a two-tone warning for "already checked in," and a low error tone for invalid/revoked, so the outcome is clear without looking at the screen. A short vibration accompanies it where the platform supports it — Android and desktop Chrome do; iOS Safari (and every browser on iOS, since they all share its WebKit engine) has never supported the Vibration API at all, so it's silently skipped there, not a bug. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` (skips vibration) and a new mute toggle (speaker icon next to the camera button on desktop, and in the mobile camera overlay's header) that only affects the beep — vibration is silent by nature, so it's unaffected by mute. Web Audio's autoplay restriction (an `AudioContext` starts suspended until a user gesture unlocks it) means the very first scan of a session may be silent while it wakes up; every scan after that has sound, since a hardware scanner's keystrokes count as a user gesture the same as real typing (#431).
- My account: **Download backup codes** button next to the codes shown during 2FA enrollment — same one-code-per-line `.txt` and `admitto-backup-codes.txt` filename as the server-rendered MFA enrollment page; rendered inline in the backup-codes header row so the 2FA card height stays unchanged (#421).
- **Settings → Security → Sessions gained an Edit action next to Revoke**, letting a superadmin correct a session's device label after the fact — previously a typo made at first login (`DeviceLabelStep`) had no fix short of force-revoking the session and waiting for a fresh re-login to re-prompt. `POST /api/admin/sessions/:id/device-label` reuses the existing `updateSessionDeviceLabel` (`@admitto/auth`), locks the session row and audits both the previous and new label transactionally alongside the change itself (so two superadmins editing the same session at once can't produce an audit entry with a stale previous value), and returns 409 instead of silently no-op'ing if the session is no longer active (revoked or expired). Reports resolves each admission's device label live from the session behind it rather than only the frozen snapshot taken at check-in time, so a correction here is reflected on that session's past check-ins too, not just future ones — a check-in whose session has since been purged (short post-event retention) keeps its original snapshot instead, as does a check-in from before this shipped, unless a session was still findable to backfill its link from the existing check-in action log. Edit now opens its own small modal instead of reusing `ConfirmDialog` — that component is reserved for destructive/irreversible confirmations (AGENTS.md), and correcting a label is neither, matching the same edit-modal shape already used for Users & roles and custom font families. The Edit and Revoke buttons were also briefly rendered right up against each other — their shared wrapper `div` referenced `at-row`/`at-row--gap`, a class actually scoped to `.device-gate` in an unrelated stylesheet and applying no spacing at all here; replaced with a genuinely scoped `.sessions-row-actions` (`display: flex; gap: var(--space-2)`), matching the identical two-button pattern already used by the Users page.
- **New "Support contact" section on Organisation settings → General** — a company or person name plus an email address, reserved for future use (for example, identifying this instance if it ever calls external services). Not wired into anything yet, just captured for when it's needed. The email field uses the same password-manager/autofill opt-out already established for the Mail tab's own address fields (1Password, LastPass, Bitwarden, iCloud "Hide My Email"), since it isn't a login field and shouldn't be offered as one.

- **The sidebar footer's version label now also shows the running build's commit** (`v0.4.12 · a955ac9`, matching GitHub's own short-SHA format) next to the "Report a bug" icon. Production images get the real commit from the CI build that produced them (`GIT_COMMIT` Docker build-arg, since the image has no `.git` directory of its own); local dev/builds read it straight from the checked-out `HEAD`.
- **Attendee Detail page gained a "Notes" tab**, so staff can read and add internal notes on an attendee without going through check-in. It shares the same notes check-in operators already write during scanning (`AttendeeNote`) — a note added from either side shows up on the other. The tab's count badge shows how many notes exist; adding one shows a success toast and any failure shows an error toast. Notes are staff-only and never shown to the attendee. Follow-up polish (PO review): each note now shows the author's avatar and a role badge (Operator/Admin/Superadmin) next to their name, with the timestamp right-aligned. Staff can edit their own note; deleting one follows the same escalation as other admin actions — admins may delete their own note or an operator's, but not another admin's, while superadmins may delete any note — and both edits and deletions are recorded in the audit log. The hint above the note form is now a neutral info callout instead of alarm-styled text, and the Add button moved into its own actions row below the textarea instead of floating awkwardly beside it.
- **Attendee Detail page gained a "Revoke items" action**, resetting every issued item (badge, wristband, giftbag, …) for just this attendee back to pending. Previously this was only possible from the Attendees list's bulk actions menu; it now reuses that same endpoint scoped to a single attendee, with its own confirmation dialog and the same disabled-with-tooltip treatment as the list's version when there's nothing to revoke.

- **Admission log — Items column and export parity.** The admission log table, CSV export, and PDF export now show which event-day items (badge, gift bag, etc.) were actually issued to each admitted attendee, alongside the existing device column.
- **Event Overview API — check-in timing, ticket-type breakdown, and a merged recent-activity feed.** `GET /api/admin/events/:eventId/overview` now also returns `last_check_in_at`, a `busiest_hour` (mirroring the Reports page's own hour-bucketing/timezone handling, scoped to `CheckIn` rows so only successful admissions count), a `ticket_type_breakdown` per the event's ticket-type catalog (active attendees only, zero-count types omitted), and a `recent_activity` feed merging check-ins, mail delivery failures/bounces, and recent import batches newest-first (capped at the most recent 30 combined). Each `recent_activity` entry now also carries `attendee_id` (null for import batches), so the admin UI can link a check-in or mail entry straight to that attendee. No schema changes.
- **Attendees list — bulk revoke items (#551).** The bulk-selection bar's "More actions" menu gains a **Revoke items** entry that resets every issued item hand-out (badge, wristband, giftbag, …) back to pending for every selected attendee at once, after a confirmation dialog — the same effect as the Danger zone's event-wide "Revoke all items issued", scoped to an explicit selection. Independent of check-in status, and always resets every configured item for the selection at once (no per-item picker — that level of control already exists on the check-in screen). Disabled on archived events, when the event has no configured items, and once nothing in the selection has anything issued to revoke. The menu entry and confirm dialog report how many of the selection actually have something issued (not the raw selection size), excluding any attendee whose pass is already blocked — the Attendees list row now carries a `has_issued_items` flag for this. Styled as a caution (amber), not danger (red) — reversible via re-issuing at check-in, unlike Revoke pass/Delete; the confirm button stays locked for 10 seconds after opening, same "don't act on reflex" pause as bulk Delete.
- **Attendees list — bulk revoke check-in (#546).** The bulk-selection bar's "More actions" menu gains a **Revoke check-in** entry that undoes check-in for every currently-checked-in attendee in the selection at once — enabled only when the selection has someone to revoke, following the same CAS-based path (and item-reset cascade) the single-attendee "Revoke check-in" action already uses, behind a confirmation dialog whose confirm button stays locked for 10 seconds after opening. The existing "Check in" button is now disabled, with a tooltip explaining why, once every selected attendee is already checked in — a mixed selection stays clickable since there's still real work to do for the rest. Polish pass (PO review): the menu item sits lower in the list, grouped with the other mutating actions instead of at the top; it's styled as a caution (amber) action rather than full danger red, since it's reversible via Check in again, unlike Delete below it; its hint text now reports only how many of the selection are actually checked in, not the raw selection size; and every disabled reason across the "More actions" menu (Revoke check-in/items/pass, Change ticket type, Send tickets) now shows via a new shared `Tooltip` component (`@admitto/ui`) instead of the browser's plain default title tooltip. `Tooltip` measures the trigger and itself at show-time and picks whichever side actually has room, growing sideways (not above/below) for this densely-packed menu so it never overlaps a neighboring item regardless of viewport size — and on a narrow (mobile) viewport where the trigger itself spans nearly the full width and neither side has room, falls back to above/below instead of running off-screen (PO review). Second polish pass: the archived-event tooltip on "Revoke items" and "Change ticket type" read a different, hardcoded "This event is archived." instead of the shared "This event is archived — editing is disabled." their own sibling items in the same menu already use — now consistent everywhere.
- **Attendees list — bulk revoke pass (#549).** The bulk-selection bar's "More actions" menu gains a **Revoke pass** entry that blocks check-in for every selected attendee at once, after a confirmation dialog whose confirm button stays locked for 10 seconds after opening — reusing the same status-change logic and check-in-clearing cascade the single-attendee "Revoke pass" action already uses, so an admitted attendee's stale check-in is cleared too. Disabled once every selected attendee's pass is already revoked or cancelled (a mixed selection stays enabled, matching "Revoke check-in"'s own gate) — a still-active attendee left in a mixed selection is revoked and reported in the result toast, an already-revoked one is left untouched and reported separately rather than treated as a failure. Blocked on archived events, matching the other bulk actions that mutate state. The menu item's hint text and the confirm dialog's title count only the attendees in the selection whose pass is still active, not the raw selection size, so a mixed selection doesn't overstate how many will actually be revoked.
- **Attendees list — filter by mail delivery status (#522).** A fourth filter select joins the existing check-in / attendance / ticket-type row: **Not sent / Sent / Pending / Failed**, classifying the same latest-delivery status the Mail column badge already shows — so "who never got a ticket email" or "whose mail bounced" is one dropdown away instead of eyeballing badges page by page. The buckets group the raw delivery statuses the way the Overview email-delivery card does (Sent = accepted/sent/delivered, Failed = failed/bounced/rejected), the filter composes with search and every other filter including pagination counts, and both the header Export dropdown and its audit-log entry respect it.
- **Import attendees — import history and success screen, phase C (#358).** The import page's right column gains an **Import history** card listing recent commits for the event — when, which file, and how many rows were created / updated / skipped — read straight from the audit log the commit step already writes, so past imports are visible without digging. A finished import now lands on the mockup's success screen: green check, a one-line created/updated/skipped summary, and **Import another file** / **View attendees** actions (with the support reference ID and any skipped/invalid row details still below).
- **Import attendees — mockup-parity layout, phase A (#358).** The import page now follows the design-system mockup's two-column layout: upload on the left (a real drag-and-drop dropzone replacing the bare file input, with a green file chip once a file is picked and the row preview below), options and the validation summary on the right. A new **Dry run** switch (on by default) makes the existing two-phase flow explicit — "Validate file" runs the no-writes preview pass it always has, and **Commit import** only arms once the switch is turned off after reviewing the summary, so nobody imports on reflex. The summary card shows the to-create / to-update / to-skip counters color-coded like the mockup, and everything else (capacity override, archived-event lockdown, template download, column reference) behaves exactly as before. Columns stack on narrow screens.
- **Attendees list — bulk change ticket type (#521).** The bulk bar's "More actions" menu gains a **Change ticket type…** entry that opens a picker listing the event's configured ticket types and assigns the chosen one to every selected attendee at once — upgrading a whole group to VIP no longer means opening each attendee one by one. The picker is dynamic (built on the per-event ticket-type catalog from batch 04, not the mockup's hardcoded VIP/Standard pair), the server re-validates the chosen type against the catalog at submit time so a type deleted mid-flight fails cleanly instead of writing a dangling reference, attendees who already have the type are left untouched and reported separately in the result toast, and every actual change lands in the attendee's activity timeline exactly like a manual edit. Disabled on archived events and when no ticket types are configured.
- **Attendees list — bulk change attendance status.** The bulk bar's "More actions" menu gains a **Change attendance status…** entry that opens a picker of the fixed attendance states (Confirmed, Tentative, Declined, Cancelled, Registered) and sets the chosen one for every selected attendee at once — same shape as bulk change ticket type, including the three-way result toast (updated / already-set / none-found, the last one meaning the selection no longer resolves to real attendees) and an `rsvp_status_changed` activity-log entry per attendee actually changed, marked `source: "admin"` like the existing single-attendee edit path. Disabled on archived events. Bot review: the picker dialog and the result-toast logic were each independent copy-pastes of bulk change ticket type's own — now a shared `CardPickerDialog` and `notifyBulkAssignResult` back both features, so a future fix to either only needs to land once. The bulk write also now reuses `computeRsvpChange` (the single-attendee PATCH path's own write-payload logic) instead of hand-duplicating it, and the picker's fixed status list is derived from `RSVP_LABELS`' own keys instead of a separately hand-typed array, so it can't silently drift from the `RsvpStatus` union. `BulkRsvpResponse` is now a type alias of `BulkTicketTypeResponse` rather than a byte-for-byte duplicate. Follow-up code review: the "More actions" menu itself had the same duplication one level up — its eight items (Send tickets, Export selected, Change ticket type, Change attendance status, Revoke check-in, Revoke items, Revoke pass, Delete) were each a hand-written Tooltip+button+icon+label/hint block; they're now one shared `MoreActionsMenuItem` component, with no change to any item's tooltip text, disabled logic, or accessibility semantics. The server-side transaction shape was the last remaining duplication: `handleBulkRsvpEventAttendees` and `handleBulkTicketTypeEventAttendees` now share `applyBulkAttendeeChanges` (diff owned rows against a target, write only what changed, log one entry per changed row, report updated/already-set counts) — each still does its own findMany and any pre-transaction validation/locking (the ticket-type catalog lock), but the identical tail end is no longer duplicated. Second bot review: both endpoints read every selected attendee's current value, then wrote every changed row in one blanket `id IN (...)` update — a concurrent single-attendee PATCH landing on one of those rows between the read and the write was silently overwritten back to the bulk's target, and the activity-log entry it wrote recorded a "from" value the row no longer actually held. `applyBulkAttendeeChanges` now writes via a single per-row-conditional `UPDATE ... FROM (VALUES ...)` statement keyed on each row's own value read moments earlier, so a row a concurrent edit changed in that window is left untouched (not clobbered, not logged with a stale "from") and reported back as a new `conflictCount` alongside `updatedCount`/`alreadySetCount`. Code review on #569: `CardPickerDialog`'s Apply button now carries the same 10s "don't act on reflex" arm-before-confirming cooldown (depleting progress bar included) as the bulk Delete/Revoke dialogs — a misclick on a large batch could otherwise overwrite everyone's ticket type or RSVP status in one shot with no confirmation at all. Both pickers gained it together to stay consistent with each other. Follow-up: the new `conflictCount` from the CAS fix above reached the response but not the operator — `notifyBulkAssignResult` only ever read `updatedCount`/`alreadySetCount`, so a raced attendee vanished from the toast with no indication anything was skipped, and a selection where every attendee raced showed the "may have been removed" error (wrong — they were found, just raced). The toast now notes "(N skipped — changed by someone else just now)" alongside the existing already-had-it note, with its own message for the all-raced case. Bot review on the CAS fix: `applyBulkAttendeeChanges` picked `=` vs `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` per caller via a `nullable` flag documenting each column's DB nullability — a future caller passing the wrong value for its own column would silently miscount every currently-unset row as a conflict. `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` is null-safe and behaves identically to `=` for a non-nullable column, so the flag added a footgun without adding a capability; removed in favor of always using it. PO catch: the cooldown's "Please wait Ns before confirming" message rendered via a plain native `title` attribute on both `CardPickerDialog` and `ConfirmDialog`'s own Apply/confirm buttons — a leftover from before this app's shared `Tooltip` component existed — showing the browser's default tooltip instead of the app's own; both now wrap the button in `Tooltip` like every other disabled-reason hint in the app. SonarCloud: `MoreActionsMenuItem`'s class-name string nested one template literal inside another (`` `more-actions-menu__item${variant ? ` more-actions-menu__item--${variant}` : ""}` ``, flagged S4624) — rewritten as an array `filter(Boolean).join(" ")`, the same pattern `Tooltip` itself already uses to combine class names.
- **Attendees list — Export selected as CSV (#520).** The bulk bar's "More actions" menu now has an **Export selected** entry that downloads exactly the checked rows as a CSV — separate from the header Export dropdown, which always exports the whole filtered view. Handy when an operator hand-picks a few attendees (say, one company's group) and wants just those in a file; the menu entry shows a live "CSV of N attendees" hint so it's clear what's about to download. Works on archived events too (it's read-only), goes through the same sanitized export pipeline as the existing formats (formula-injection guards, no tokens, audit-log entry recording how many rows were requested and exported), and ids from outside the event are ignored server-side.
- **Attendees list — manual bulk check-in from the bulk-selection bar (#355 follow-up).** A new **Check in** button sits between "Send tickets" and "More actions" in the bulk bar, letting an admin mark a selection of attendees as checked in without scanning QR codes — useful for a group arriving together or a walk-in with no ticket handy. Reuses the exact same single-use CAS admission path scan check-in already goes through (ADR 0010 §4) once per selected attendee, so every existing guarantee — no double check-in, per-attendee activity log entry, badge issuance if the event has one configured — applies unchanged; there's no bespoke bulk logic to drift out of sync with the real thing. Fires immediately with no confirmation dialog (it's a reversible internal state change, not an email send) and reports back with a toast breakdown (e.g. "4 attendees checked in (1 already admitted)"). Blocked on archived events, matching Send tickets and the single-attendee revoke-checkin action — checking someone into an event that's already over doesn't make sense.
- **Attendees list — clear button on the search box.** The name/email/company search input had no way to clear a typed query except deleting it character by character; a small ✕ now appears at the end of the field once there's text in it.
- **Attendee detail — Delete attendee, a GDPR erasure entry point in the admin SPA (closes #356).** The `DELETE` API has existed since v0.4.6, but fulfilling an erasure request meant using internal tooling or a manual DB script — the admin UI had no delete button. A new **Delete attendee** item in the Attendee Detail page's **More actions** menu (styled red, below a divider) opens a confirmation dialog requiring the operator to type the attendee's exact name before the button unlocks, same "drunk click" safeguard already used for permanently deleting an event. Confirming permanently removes the attendee's profile, ticket deliveries, wallet pass, and check-ins in one transaction and writes an audit log entry, then redirects to the attendees list with a success toast. Unlike every other action in the same menu, Delete attendee is not blocked on archived events — an erasure request can legally arrive after an event ends, and the backend endpoint never blocked on `archived_at` either, so the UI now matches. [DSAR-PROCEDURE.md](docs/DSAR-PROCEDURE.md) updated to point at this new UI path instead of "use your internal tooling." Polish pass (PO review): the confirmation dialog's message no longer references DSAR-PROCEDURE.md by filename — operators clicking the button aren't going to go read a doc — and now lists what gets removed as a proper bullet list instead of one run-on sentence. Deleting an attendee (single or bulk) now also writes an entry to the central admin **Audit log** (Instance Settings, superadmin-only), alongside the existing per-attendee record — previously an attendee's own activity trail disappeared along with the row it was about, leaving no record anywhere of *who* erased them; attendee creation gets the same central record for symmetry. The Attendees list's row-selection bulk bar gained a matching **Delete** action inside a new **More actions** menu (an extra click to even reach it, matching the design mockup and the same danger-item treatment already used on the attendee detail page), erasing every selected attendee at once with the same removal scope, a 10-second arm delay before the confirm button unlocks (matching the Danger Zone's other bulk actions), and the same "not blocked on archived events" behavior as the single-attendee action. Second polish pass (PO review): the central admin audit log entries for attendee create/erase now include the event's title and the attendee's own name/email, not just opaque ids — unlike the per-attendee log (which stays deliberately PII-free, since re-logging the very data just erased would defeat the point), this one-off exception is scoped to the superadmin-only central log and grounded in GDPR Art. 33/34's breach-notification duty to identify affected individuals (documented in [DATA-PROTECTION.md](DATA-PROTECTION.md)).
- **Reports — ticket type badges and breakdown now read the per-event catalog (closes #387).** The admission log's "Ticket type" column shows the same colored badge used everywhere else in the app (Attendees, check-in) instead of plain text, and the "By ticket type" breakdown chart gets a matching color dot per row and colors its bar to match. A configured type with zero admissions still shows up in the breakdown (e.g. a "Press" type nobody has checked in under yet) instead of only appearing once someone actually has it, and the admission log's type filter lists configured types instead of whatever raw strings happen to be in the data. CSV and PDF exports show the catalog's display label the same way. Depends on the "Ticket types" catalog above — a fresh install or an event with no configured types just shows an empty breakdown, same as having no attendees.
- **Event Settings — Ticket types: per-event catalog replaces free-text guest types (closes #351).** Admins now define an event's ticket types (label + color) once on a new "Ticket types" tab in Event Settings, instead of typing a guest type by hand every time — which used to let "VIP", "vip", and "Vip" drift into three different-looking entries, with only "vip" ever getting a colored badge. Each type gets a color from a curated 8-color palette (a small swatch picker), shows a live badge preview and how many attendees currently have it, and can be renamed or recolored anytime; deleting a type is blocked while any attendee still has it. Every place that used to accept a free-text ticket type now reads from this catalog instead: the add/edit attendee form is a dropdown, the attendees list "Type" filter and the Communication bulk-send "by ticket type" filter both list only configured types, and CSV/XLSX import matches a row's `ticket_type` value against the catalog (case-insensitively, by name or key) — an unmatched value is skipped at commit with a clear reason in the import preview, instead of being written to the database as-is. New events get one default "Standard" type automatically; existing events are migrated on upgrade — each event's past ticket-type values are grouped case-insensitively into one canonical entry apiece (preserving today's purple "vip" coloring, everything else neutral), with every attendee's stored value normalized to match, so nothing needs fixing by hand after the update.
- **Requirements — Custom attendee fields registry (closes #372).** Event-wide attendee data fields (dietary requirements, parking, or anything else that isn't tied to a specific hand-out item) now have their own management card on the Requirements page — define a field's label, type (text/select/boolean), required flag, and options once, independent of any event item. Event items reference these fields to show one as an operator hint at check-in (e.g. a gift bag's shirt size) instead of each item separately redeclaring the same field with its own metadata, which is what let two items disagree about the same field before. The check-in operator card, the attendee edit/create form, CSV/XLSX import, and export columns all read a field's definition from this one registry now, so a field defined once is consistent everywhere it's used. A field can only be deleted from the Requirements card, and is blocked (409) while any item still shows it as a hint. Existing events are migrated automatically on upgrade — any field previously declared only inside an item's config is copied into the registry as part of the deploy's migration step, before the server starts serving traffic, with no operator action needed.
- **Event Settings — Danger zone: "Revoke all check-ins" and "Revoke all items issued" (superadmin only):** two new bulk actions to undo things that happened on an event without visiting each attendee one at a time. **Revoke all check-ins** clears the checked-in status for every currently admitted attendee at once (they can check in again afterwards); **Revoke all items issued** resets every issued/returned item hand-out (e.g. badges) back to "pending" for every attendee, independently of check-in status. Both reuse the same per-attendee logic already used by the individual "Revoke check-in" / "Revoke item" actions, are blocked on archived events (same as the rest of the Danger zone's mutating actions), disable themselves when there's nothing to revoke, and confirm with a plain Yes/No dialog stating the live count — no typed confirmation, since the action is fully reversible. Each destructive Danger zone confirm button now also holds a **10-second countdown** before it becomes clickable (a thin depleting progress bar under the button label, same button size) — enough time to reconsider before a bulk/irreversible action fires. A third **"Revoke all Wallet passes"** row is a disabled placeholder for now — Apple/Google Wallet passes aren't built yet.
- **Event Settings → Branding: image asset library for custom email tokens.** Superadmins can upload named branding images (e.g. a sponsor logo) scoped to an event and reference them anywhere in that event's email templates as `{{name}}` — resolved to the uploaded file's URL at send/preview time exactly like the built-in `{{logo_url}}`/`{{header_image_url}}`. Names must start with a letter and contain only lowercase letters, numbers, and underscores; a name that collides with a built-in placeholder or an existing asset is rejected with a clear message. A 2 MB per-file limit and a per-event asset count limit apply. The Communication template editor's "Insert placeholder" list now also lists these custom tokens alongside the built-in ones, and inserts a working image element (not just a bare token) for any image-type placeholder — built-in or custom. An asset can't be deleted while one of the event's saved email templates still references its `{{name}}` — deleting it then would make the image silently vanish from attendee emails; remove it from the template first.
- **Public ticket page shows the event's (or organisation's) logo when configured (closes #419).** The `/t/…` ticket page an attendee opens now shows the event's own logo if one is set, falling back to the organisation's logo, in place of the generic Admitto wordmark; an event with neither configured keeps today's unchanged wordmark. Applies to both ticket link types (internal token and agency-provided public reference).
- Admin/superadmin can now **revoke** any attendee's check-in — regardless of who admitted them or when — from the Check-in page's attendee card or from the attendee's own Detail page, behind a confirmation dialog. Distinct from the operator's existing device-scoped **Undo check-in** (their own accidental-scan safety net, unchanged); the new action clears the admission, rolls back an auto-issued badge, and logs a `check_in_revoked` entry in the attendee's Activity Log. Admin/superadmin only — never shown to operators (#449).
- **Admin/superadmin can now revoke a single handed-out item** on the Check-in page's attendee card. When something was marked issued by mistake (or needs re-doing), a small red **Revoke** next to an already-issued **Badge / gift bag / headset** resets it back to "to hand out", so the operator can issue it again — no need to pick a target state, it just goes back to pending. Admin/superadmin only: it's never shown to operators, and the endpoint independently rejects a non-admin caller (the hidden button is only a convenience, not the security boundary).
