Relatedly, revoking an attendee's whole **check-in** now also resets **all** of their handed-out items back to pending in the same action — previously only the auto-issued badge was rolled back, leaving a gift bag or headset stuck as "issued" after a re-admit; per the PO's ask, this is a blanket reset with no need to trace which items belong to which scan. Every item reset (single or blanket) is logged as `item_revoked` in the attendee's Activity Log. Desktop check-in card only for now — the mobile camera overlay's item-issuing flow is unchanged. Bot review: the Revoke button was missing for an **issued item that still requires return** (e.g. a headset) — it still had a pending "Mark returned" action, so it fell into the wrong branch of the card's item row and had no Revoke control at all, forcing an admin to mark it returned first just to reset it. Revoke is now shown for any non-pending item regardless of whether it also has an operator action pending. **Security review round:** the server-side revoke endpoint never actually checked whether the attendee's pass was blocked (revoked/cancelled) — only the card's own Revoke button hid itself for that case, so a direct API call could still reset an item on a blocked pass despite a code comment claiming the server enforced the same check; it now independently rejects a blocked pass. Also fixed: a single item could no longer be revoked once its item type was disabled in Requirements (409 "Item not found or disabled"), while revoking the whole check-in reset it anyway — the only way to fix one stuck disabled item was the much bigger action of revoking the entire check-in; single-item revoke no longer requires the item to still be enabled, since it's correcting a past hand-out, not offering a new one. A lost race between an admin's revoke and a concurrent operator action now surfaces as an error instead of silently doing nothing with no audit trail. **Round 2:** revoke (single-item and whole-check-in) no longer resets an item sitting in one of the exceptional "lost" / "problem" / "not applicable" outcomes (ADR 0010) back to "pending" — those aren't handed-out states, and silently clearing them would erase a real exceptional result as if it were ready to hand out again; only "issued"/"returned" items are actually revocable. The whole-check-in blanket reset also now scopes its queries by event, matching the single-item path, as defense-in-depth. A revoked item is now correctly logged as event-day activity on the attendee's Activity tab — it previously fell back to a generic row and a UTC timestamp instead of the event's local time, the same gap already fixed for admin check-in revokes. **Round 3:** revoking an attendee's **pass** (Attendee Detail page) shared the same mutation as the explicit "Revoke check-in" action, so it picked up that action's blanket item reset as a side effect — revoking a pass after a gift bag/headset was already handed out, then later restoring it, left the item card falsely claiming it still needed to be given out, even though it physically already had been. Pass-revoke now only clears the stale admission (its original, intended behavior); the blanket item reset stays opt-in, used only by the explicit "Revoke check-in" action where the PO actually asked for it. **Round 4:** the check-in card's Revoke controls (both check-in and per-item) were gated on "is this user an admin of *any* organization," not the current event's — a mixed-role user (e.g. admin of one org, only an event-day operator for another) could see the buttons on an event they don't manage and have every click 403 (Codex review). Now gated on admin status for the event's own organization specifically, matching the server's check exactly.
- Event Settings — **Delete event** (superadmin only): a new Danger Zone action that permanently deletes an event and everything in it (attendees, items, contacts, resources, mail templates, action-log history, check-ins). It is available on both active and archived events, and only enables once the event shows zero real activity — no attendees, no items beyond the default "Badge", no contacts, no resources, no pinned note, no event-specific mail template, and no event-scoped action-log history (e.g. a report export or item-config change with no attendee attached) — so a genuinely-used event, active or archived, can never be deleted, only archived/unarchived. Confirming requires typing the event's exact title (`ConfirmDialog` gained a reusable typed-confirmation mode); the deletion is recorded in the audit trail (#395).

- **The Archiving tab (Organisation settings) now shows who created and who last archived each event, and when.** A "Created" column (date + "by <name>") joins the existing "Archived" column, which gains the same "by <name>" line. Both dates show in the acting admin's own local timezone at the moment of the action (not UTC) when known, falling back to UTC only for events predating this attribution or a since-deleted user. `Event.created_by_user_id`/`created_by_timezone`/`archived_by_user_id`/`archived_by_timezone` are new nullable columns (no FK, matching `AdminAuditLog.actor_user_id` — a deactivated/deleted user must not block reads of an event they touched), best-effort backfilled for existing events from the matching `event_created`/`event_archived` audit-log entry (most recent one wins across an archive/unarchive/re-archive cycle).
- **Versioned public product Wiki source and publishing checks.** User guidance now lives in `docs/wiki/`, is validated in pull requests, and is synchronised to the GitHub Wiki only from merged source content.
- **Instance Settings and Event Settings now have a "Documentation" link in the page header**, opening the GitHub wiki in a new tab — the same link/icon previously removed from the sidebar footer when it moved to instance-level navigation, now given a proper home on both settings surfaces instead of nowhere.
- Event settings: new **Branding tab** lets an event admin upload a custom logo and header image scoped to just this event, overriding the organization's default branding for that event only (falls back to the organization's branding whenever left blank). Reuses the existing organization-logo upload component, now generalized to support event-scoped uploads and a disabled state while the event is archived.
- Codecov Test Analytics: CI coverage run now also emits a JUnit XML per workspace (`--reporter=junit`), uploaded via `codecov/test-results-action` — flaky test and failure-rate reporting, no gate.
- **Branding upload orphan GC (`admitto storage gc`).** Diffs the local `UPLOAD_DIR` volume against organisation/event branding URLs, image assets, theme fonts, and any `/uploads/…` paths pasted into mail templates; deletes unreferenced managed files older than a 48h grace window. Supports `--dry-run`. Interim delete-on-abandon (#700) still handles cancel/replace hot paths; this CLI cleans leftovers and future leaks. Compose already bind-mounts `./uploads` → `/app/uploads`.
- **Logo and event image crop before upload.** After dropping or browsing a PNG/JPG/WebP, a popup lets you trim margins by dragging the selection edges/corners (free-form, any aspect). Transparent PNG/WebP stay transparent (no white fill). Server re-encodes branding uploads through `sharp` to strip metadata and reject undecodable files.

- `eslint-plugin-security` enabled for `apps/*/src` (admin, web, cli) with `detect-object-injection` off as a typed-record false-positive; remaining SAST hits annotated inline.
- `operatorApiErrorMessage()` / `hasApiErrorCode()` helper (`apps/admin/src/api/operator-api-error.ts`) — central mapping for operator-safe admin API error copy; AGENTS.md documents the convention.
- `CheckInPage.scan-queue.test.tsx`: extracted `typeWedge(input, token, { gapMs?, baseTime?, prefix? })` helper — replaces 17 identical character-by-character typing loops; future timing tweaks are a one-line change.

### Changed
- **Bounce IMAP MIME extraction uses libmime / libqp / iconv-lite** for quoted-printable, base64, and charset decoding (including ISO-8859-1 and windows-1252 NDR parts), instead of a UTF-8-only hand-rolled decoder. RFC 2047 encoded-words in part headers are decoded before walking the MIME tree. JS string sources are encoded as UTF-8 octets before the binary walk so unescaped non-ASCII in 8bit bodies (e.g. `boîte`) is preserved.
- **Bounce ingest looks up matching deliveries in one query per folder poll**, instead of one database round-trip per parsed NDR recipient line.
- **Bounce free-text NDR dialects are a matcher table** in `parseBounceLine` (Postfix / mailhop / Synology / orphan `failed:`), so new MTA formats can be added without growing one inline function. Bounce probe reuses the same IMAP open helper as ingest.

- **Mail connection tests now share one admin and API flow**, keeping SMTP and IMAP test state, response handling, and scoped SMTP probing consistent.
- **Bounce detection settings now use the same secret-field metadata as mail transport settings**, keeping dedicated IMAP password masking and reused SMTP credential status consistent.
- **Bounce-ingest UID deduplication now has bounded retention.** Every ingest run removes processed IMAP UID markers older than the UTC calendar day of the same rolling 14-day lookback used to fetch bounce messages (IMAP `SEARCH SINCE` is day-granular), including runs where no event mailbox is currently enabled.
- **Also verify bounce says what to test in plain language**, and the result explains bounce SMTP codes for non-technical readers: "Enter an email address that will fail (for example, one that does not exist). Admitto sends a test message to it, then waits up to 90 seconds to check that it can detect the failure automatically." When a bounce arrives, a new **What this means** row under **Bounce SMTP code** translates the code (e.g. `550/5.7.1`) into a plain-English sentence.
- **Send test email looks like Admitto mail.** Organisation and Event transport tests (and bounce-probe sends) use a simple branded HTML card with the organisation/event logo when configured, otherwise the Admitto mark, a centered green Transport OK mark, richer Diagnostics (recipient, from, host/mailbox when known), and a centered footer. Still not a ticket template.

- **Health check accuracy:** mail queue rows stay degraded when any retryable failures remain (even with a normal queue depth); Redis/rate-limit probe is labelled **Rate-limit storage** (not session storage); optional encryption in development shows as not configured rather than Active; configured mail transports report **Configured** until probed; **Run live checks** probes SMTP (nodemailer verify) and Microsoft Graph (token fetch) without sending mail, with a 15s probe deadline (Graph token fetch uses `AbortSignal.timeout`); live mode no longer reports env mail as healthy when effective org config cannot be resolved; Power Automate stays Configured with live probe skipped. Shared staff **More actions** menu styles live under `components/`; Card headers expose a stable `.at-card__actions` slot.
- **Settings environment locks: card-header badge.** When Instance URL, Security, or Mail transport fields are managed by environment variables, the card header shows a short **From environment** badge (right side) instead of a long label on every field — so mobile rows no longer wrap. Fields stay read-only.
- **Organisation settings card intros (General through Archiving):** Instance URL, Support contact, Organisation branding, Theme, Mail transport/Sender/provider cards, Security, and Event archiving show a visible `settings-card-intro` under the title; the `(i)` HintLabel tip no longer repeats that sentence. Organisation logo field hint size matches Input's `.at-hint` (`--fs-xs`).
- **The Delivery log's table now matches the polish of Attendees/Reports** — a responsive desktop-table/mobile-card layout, status badges, and a "Rows per page" + "Showing X of Y" + Previous/Next footer, replacing the previous bare table and its plain Previous/Next-only pager.
- **The "Managed by environment" badge next to Instance URL and Security's four fields (session lifetimes, trusted-device duration, MFA-required roles) is removed**, along with Instance URL's separate "Instance URL is configured via environment." success banner. Both told an admin something already obvious from the field's own disabled/greyed-out state, and having both a badge and a banner say it for Instance URL specifically was redundant with each other too. Instance URL's warning banner for a genuinely missing URL is unchanged. The equivalent badge on Mail transport's locked secret fields (SMTP password, Graph client secret, Power Automate key) stays, since there it replaces an otherwise-empty action-button slot rather than duplicating a visibly disabled input.
- **General settings → Support contact's two fields (Contact name, Contact email) now sit side by side instead of stacked full-width**, matching the two-column layout Mail transport's Sender card already uses for equally short fields (same `.mail-transport-section` grid, no new CSS).
- **14 hand-rolled inline notice/warning/error boxes across the admin UI replaced with the shared `Notice` component (`@admitto/ui`), instead of 14 independently hand-copied variants.** Covers: Event settings Danger zone; Attendee Detail's Notes-tab disclaimer and its three inline warnings (unissued items, stale-write conflict, changed-primary-address); the Logs tab's live-poll-degraded warning; Cloudflare Access editor's three inline notices; the setup wizard's system-checks-failed retry banner; the Resend ticket, Create event, Edit user, and Invite user modals' error notices; the Communication editor's validation-errors list and the Communication page's bounced-email banner; My account's forced-password-change notice; the Attendee import page's capacity/no-valid-rows banners; and Check-in's offline/reconnecting banners. Along the way this also fixed several boxes that were silently rendering with a hardcoded fallback color because their `--status-warn-*` CSS custom properties were never actually defined anywhere (the bounced-email banner, My account's password notice and its neighboring 2FA-backup panel, Attendee Detail's stale-write warning, the Attendee import banners, and Check-in's offline banners), and every migrated box now consistently shows its variant icon (most previously had none at all). `Notice` also gained a new optional `action` prop so a trailing control (e.g. the setup wizard's Retry button, Attendee Detail's stale-write Reload button) stays a separate, right-aligned item instead of being absorbed into the message text.
- **Event settings' page-header Save/Reset is gone — each card that actually needs saving now has its own.** The single hoisted Save button (tied to the General tab's form, but shown unconditionally on every tab including Ticket types and Danger zone, where it was always disabled since those save immediately on their own) is replaced with a per-card `Save` in "Basic information" and "Event branding" headers, and a `Reset`/`Save` pair at the bottom of the Mail transport card (via the shared `SettingsFooter`, matching the instance-level Mail transport panel's own layout) instead of hoisted to the page header. The "Organization mail"/"Dedicated for this event" mode toggle moves from the card body into the card's own header, shortened to "Organization"/"Dedicated" so it doesn't get clipped on narrow viewports, and matches the `--control-h-sm` height standard other card-header controls already use (mirroring the existing fix for the Audit log/Overview activity Segmented toggles) instead of inflating the card header past the shared standard.
- Cleared 16 verified-safe SonarCloud findings across the mailer adapters, admin/auth API routes, the CLI, and one admin table/test file: removed a dead trailing-whitespace regex in check-in scan normalization that a following `.trim()` already made redundant, dropped redundant `Promise.resolve()`/`?? {}` wrappers, switched two null-guards to optional chaining, reused an already-declared type alias instead of a duplicated inline union, removed one unused import, parameterized three near-identical `SecurityPanel` session-duration tests into one, and added a small CLI table stringify helper so object-valued cells no longer print `[object Object]`. No functional change — full lint/typecheck/test pass across every touched workspace.
- **Admin topbar — system status and account menu redesign:** the sparse row of standalone icons (check-in connection dot, mailer dot, a 2-letter role badge, name, and a bare sign-out button) is now two proper dropdowns. **System status** shows one glanceable "All systems normal" / "Degraded performance" / "Action needed" indicator; superadmins get the full picture (Database, Session storage, Email sending, Data encryption, plus the check-in connection) backed by the existing `/api/admin/setup/checks` readiness endpoint, with a "View system logs" shortcut into Settings → Security; everyone else still sees the check-in connection row, since operators rely on it during check-in. **Account menu** consolidates the role badge, display name, and sign-out into one avatar-triggered dropdown showing name, role, "My account", and "Sign out". `RoleBadge` and `MailerStatusBadge` are retired (folded into the new components); the standalone `ServerConnectionBadge` is retired the same way (folded into System status as its check-in connection row). Code-review follow-up: a failed setup-checks request no longer gets stuck showing a false "All systems normal" forever (now correctly flips to "Action needed"/"Unavailable"); the Email sending row no longer shows a false "Not configured" alarm when mailer status simply hasn't loaded yet, and — since it isn't superadmin-gated data — it's no longer hidden from admins/operators, who lose that visibility otherwise; the status panel now uses `role="group"` instead of an empty `role="menu"` for roles with no actionable item; two independent topbar dropdowns opened via keyboard no longer both stay open at once (`useClickOutside` now also closes on focus leaving the container, not just on an outside click); and repeated instance-health fetches when switching between admin sections are now cached briefly instead of re-querying every time. Bot-review follow-up: a failed **Instance URL** check (`checks.base_url` — required in production so mail can build ticket links) is now a visible row and correctly flips the topbar to "Action needed" instead of being silently excluded from the verdict; and closing a dropdown when focus moves elsewhere (Tab to another control) no longer yanks focus back to the trigger — that fix, shared by `DatePicker`/`TimezoneSelect`/every `useDropdownMenu` consumer, previously only skipped the restore for a true outside click/Escape, so tabbing out of an open date or timezone picker got stuck bouncing focus back into it. Second bot-review follow-up: the dropdown is trimmed to checks a superadmin can act on day-to-day — Instance URL and Check-in connection are dropped (Instance URL stays covered by the setup wizard; Check-in connection has its own dedicated banner on the Check-in page and operator picker), and a failed Instance URL check no longer flips the topbar to "Action needed" now that there's no visible row explaining why. A migrations-pending Database check (schema drift, not a dead connection) now reads "Schema update pending" instead of the misleading "Not reachable" it previously shared with a genuine connection failure. Viewing a specific event's Email sending status now reflects that event's own resolved transport (dedicated override vs. inherited from the organization) instead of only ever showing the organization-level status. Third bot-review follow-up: Database and Session storage can now actually show "Degraded performance" (a slow-but-working response, ≥500ms) instead of only ever jumping straight from healthy to "Action needed" — the UI already had this state designed in, nothing ever triggered it. Email sending on an event with its own resolved transport also degrades instead of reading a flat "Connected" when that event has unresolved failed deliveries still marked for retry. Fourth bot-review follow-up: the panel now polls every 30s instead of only ever fetching once on mount — previously the only way to see it catch up with reality was a full page reload or navigating to a different top-level section; a background poll updates silently (no "Checking…" flash) and a single flaky tick no longer flips a healthy reading to "Unavailable"/falls back off the event-level Email sending row, it just retries next tick.
- Admin check-in header no longer repeats the event title/date/location already shown in the sidebar — the subtitle now describes the screen's purpose ("Scan QR codes and admit guests on event day"). PO review: the server-connection indicator moved out of the page header entirely into the global topbar (`ServerConnectionBadge`, next to `Mailer: SMTP`) — the underlying connection heartbeat is app-wide, not check-in-specific, so it now reads the same everywhere; both it and `MailerStatusBadge` dropped their leading dot (#378).
- **Weather and maps User-Agent** no longer send reserved documentation emails such as `@example.com` (common in local seeds). MET Norway and Nominatim reject those with HTTP 403; the probe now falls back to the support contact name or omits the fake address so Test connection works in demo environments.
- **Event list weather chips use °C or °F from the operator browser timezone** (`Europe/*` and similar → °C; `America/*` / US zones → °F), so an `en-US` locale in Poland still shows Celsius. **Public ticket weather always shows both** (for example `18°C (64°F)`), so two people reading the same link each get a familiar unit.
- **New event dialog matches the Add attendee pattern:** icon title, short subtitle, shared field styling (`Input` / same modal chrome), and a `* Required` footer. **Link name** explains that it is a permanent event ID used in agency ticket links (`/t/{name}/a/…`), while ordinary tickets stay token-only (`/t/…`); it is filled from the title and cannot change after create. Location hint covers searching a venue and setting the pin later under Event settings if search fails. Timezone trigger is one line and uses **UTC±N** (not GMT), with a short hint that values like `Europe/Warsaw` are standard timezone IDs (search Delhi, get `Asia/Kolkata`).
- **Events list map attribution:** list cards show a bottom-right HTML credit from the configured `MAP_TILE_ATTRIBUTION` (via `map_attribution` on the event DTO; pin stays centered under `object-fit: cover`). List-preview PNGs skip the burned-in credit to avoid cropping; ticket/mail maps still burn attribution into the PNG. List previews use a wider 840×256 canvas (height at least one map tile so sharp can composite). The separate HTML line under the grid was removed.
- **Default map tiles are OpenStreetMap again** (`tile.openstreetmap.org`), not CARTO Voyager. Override with `MAP_TILE_URL` / `MAP_TILE_ATTRIBUTION` when you want CARTO or a self-hosted server.
- **Events list cards show map previews.** Admin and operator pickers share a redesigned card: static map (or “No location” / **Maps unavailable** when a pin exists but `LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED=false`), date block, status badge without a dot (**Needs archiving** / **Today** / **In N days** / **Archived**), two-line location slot with empty placeholder, attendee count, and a weather “coming soon” chip. No Archive/Unarchive on the card; list order stays soonest date first (past active events at the top). Max two cards per row; list map previews use a wider zoom than ticket/mail maps (capped by `MAP_TILE_MAX_ZOOM` and a list hard-cap). Cards use a server-built `map_preview_path` (pin + zoom cache-buster, omitted when `LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED=false`) so disabled-map deployments do not request 404 PNGs and a moved pin invalidates the browser cache.
- **Location tab Address card matches the venue mockup:** one Address card (search + Find on map + map + always-visible structured address grid + verified/coords/copy-links footer), Directions & accessibility stay separate with info hints, compact addresses drop em-dashes, and saving location refreshes the sidebar venue name immediately. A timezone mismatch notice appears when the pin's IANA zone differs from the event's General-tab timezone, with a one-click Use action. Copied Google/Apple Maps links include the venue name when set (`Name@lat,lng` for Google) so the pin is titled instead of bare coordinates. The missing Support-contact notice points event managers at Organisation settings (superadmin configures it); superadmins get an Open organisation settings action. Picking a Nominatim POI that only returns a name (e.g. Złote Tarasy) now fills the address grid from the label / a reverse lookup instead of leaving street/city/country blank.
- **Public ticket page polish (mockup alignment):** Admitto mark SVG instead of a plain blue square, top accent strip, QR frame border, no “Registered” status badge, official Apple Wallet badge (`US_UK` RGB SVG) and Google Wallet badge (`enUS` add-wallet-badge SVG), subdued “How do I add this to my phone?” disclosure, street-first Getting There address on two lines (non-breaking postcodes), wheelchair/signpost icons, neutral map-link icons (not Google/Apple brand marks), Nominatim `accept-language=en` for English address labels on new geocodes, static map one zoom level closer with map attribution burned into the PNG corner.

- **The admin events picker (`/admin`) and operator check-in picker (`/operator`) now share one `EventCard` component and the same responsive grid**, replacing two hand-rolled copies of the same `.event-card`/`.event-grid` markup so the two lists stay visually in sync going forward. The admin picker is visually unchanged. The operator picker now matches it: the same attendee-count stat on each card (`GET /api/checkin/events` now returns `attendee_count` when requested via `?includeAttendeeCount=true`, computed the same way the admin endpoint already did — opt-in so the picker's own callers that don't display it, e.g. the operator sidebar, don't pay for the extra query), the calendar/pin icons on the meta line (previously plain text), the same hover/focus lift admin cards have always had (previously none), the full card as the click/tap target instead of just the title, and the same 1/2/3-column responsive breakpoints instead of always a single column. Card sizing stays touch-friendly (`event-card--touch`'s larger minimum height is unchanged) and the rest of operator behavior (auto-redirect straight to check-in when only one event is available, the empty state) is unchanged.
- **Sidebar simplified per follow-up design review.** Removed the desktop pin/unpin collapse-to-icon-rail feature entirely (`sidebar__pin-btn`, `sidebarPinPref.ts`, and the collapsed-width CSS in `shell.css`/`staff.css`) — it visually collided with the footer's "Report a bug" icon and clipped nav icons in its collapsed state; the sidebar is now always fully expanded on every breakpoint. Removed the "Documentation" link from `InstanceSidebarFoot` (a future Instance Settings placement is tracked separately, not part of this change). Removed the not-yet-live "Approval", "Passes", and "Fulfilment" placeholder nav items from the event lifecycle nav in `AdminShell`, keeping "Post-event". The sidebar's brand-mark row height now matches the topbar's `--topbar-h` (56px) exactly (previously 4px taller), so the two chrome bars line up.
- **Tablet portrait (768–1024px, e.g. an iPad held upright) now shows a persistent icon-only sidebar rail instead of the phone-style off-canvas drawer.** There's enough width at this size for an always-visible rail — nav icons (with their labels visually hidden but still readable by assistive tech) center in a narrower `--sidebar-rail-w` (4.5rem) column, wide enough to avoid the clipping the removed pin/unpin rail (above) used to have at its narrower collapsed width. The event title/date card, the "Administration" section divider, and the version/bug-report row are all hidden at this width (redundant with the page's own heading, or not meaningful without their label). Since a touch device has no hover to reveal what an icon-only item means, the existing hamburger toggle/backdrop/close-button (otherwise idle at this width, previously built for the phone drawer) now doubles as a tap-to-expand: tapping it slides the same rail out into a full labelled sidebar over a dimmed backdrop, and tapping a destination (or the backdrop, or the close button) collapses it straight back to icons — no separate control or extra code, it's the phone drawer's own open/close state reused at a width that would otherwise just sit unused. Landscape at the same physical size (e.g. a rotated iPad) falls outside this range and keeps the regular phone-style drawer, which already has more room to work with there; phone widths and desktop are unaffected.
- Unified instance-level admin sidebar (#323): `/account` uses the same sidebar chrome as `/admin` and instance settings instead of a stripped-down `AccountShell`; shared `InstanceSidebarFoot` adds All events (or Check-in for operators), Administration, My account, Documentation link, and app version; `BrandMark` extracted to a shared component. Event sidebar in `AdminShell` shows read-only title, date, and location (linked to Google Maps search).
- **Edit user modal redesigned to match the approved mockup, and role assignment is now exclusive by type.** The modal drops the boxed Card sections for a flat, single-column layout (avatar + name + email header with a close **X**, no footer Cancel), matching Invite user. Role & access shows one Role selector (Superadmin / Administrator / Operator) instead of a flat multi-role list: a person holds exactly one role type at a time; picking a different type shows a warning ("Changing to X removes Y's current Z access") and, once confirmed, replaces every assignment of the old type with the new one in the same transaction (audited as `role_changed` instead of `role_granted`). Within Administrator/Operator, multiple scopes (organizations/events) stay cumulative: add or remove them freely, same as before. Sign-in security shows Sign-in method and MFA as status chips, an **Unlink** action next to Sign-in method for SSO-linked accounts (superadmin-only, `DELETE /api/admin/users/:id/external-identity`, audited as `user_sso_unlinked`; the confirm dialog requires setting the new local password in the same step, since an SSO-only account otherwise has no password anyone actually knows), the active session count, and a real **Recent logins** list (up to 3 most recent successful sign-ins with geo and relative time, reusing the existing security audit log's search-by-email) in place of the placeholder last-sign-in-only fallback shipped earlier. Failed attempts still can't be attributed to a specific user (`user_id` is never set on a failed login, by design, to prevent account enumeration), so this list only ever shows successful sign-ins. Reset MFA / Reset password / Revoke sessions / Disable-Enable account sit in one row, sized to match the rest of the form; Delete account moves next to Save at the bottom of the modal instead of its own row. A follow-up pass moved Reset MFA / Reset password / Revoke sessions / Disable-Enable account / Delete account into a **More actions** menu in the header (the same pattern already used on the Attendees table and Attendee detail page), so the footer holds only Save; the superadmin role no longer shows a redundant "Instance-wide" chip next to the "no scopes to add" notice; the role-assign Change/Add button height now matches the Select next to it; Recent logins rows are two-line (status + geo, with a colored status icon) instead of one; and an empty Active sessions or Recent logins state shows a small icon and label instead of "0 active sessions" or nothing at all. A third pass: the event/organization scope picker no longer lists scopes already granted (they're already a chip below); scope chips show an event/organization icon and the Add/Change button a plus/refresh icon; granting a role or adding a scope no longer closes the modal, so several scopes can be added in one sitting (the still-open modal picks up the fresh assignment through the same list refresh Staff users already relies on); clicking outside the modal no longer closes it, matching the Identity providers modal's own "a superadmin mid-edit shouldn't lose work to a stray click" backdrop (Invite user's backdrop got the same treatment); Active sessions moved out of its own line into a third tile in the Sign-in method/MFA row; and Delete account now requires typing the account's email address to confirm, matching event deletion elsewhere in the app.
- **Staff users shows one role badge per person, not one per scope.** A user with several event or organization scopes of the same role type (e.g. two Operator event grants) previously showed the same role badge twice in the Roles column; roles are exclusive by type, so all of a person's assignments already share one `role`, and one badge is now enough - full detail (which scopes) is on the Role assignments tab, which now shows its own row count next to the tab label like Staff users and Active sessions already do. Role assignments and Active sessions both gained a search-by-name-or-email box in its own toolbar row below the card title (matching Staff users' own layout, not squeezed into the card header next to it), server-side and debounced for Role assignments' paginated list, client-side and instant for Active sessions' already-fully-loaded list.
- **Auth login/MFA/setup/change-password/OIDC link errors use the shared Notice look** (`.at-notice` markup via `renderNoticeHtml`, inline SVG icons so the sign-in path does not load the Tabler webfont) instead of ad-hoc pink/amber boxes. Admin session bootstrap failure uses **EmptyState + Retry**. Setup wizard leftover banners and settings/account load failures follow the same Notice / EmptyState patterns.
- **Event Settings Basic/Images Save+Reset match Organisation settings:** Reset and Save sit in the shared `SettingsFooter` under the form (not a lone Save in the card header). On General, Status sits above Basic information so Save stays at the bottom of the tab; Images Save/Reset sit below the asset library. Event title uses the shared password-manager ignore props (same as mail fields). Date has a field hint so the Date/Capacity row stays balanced. Location coordinates use the mono typeface; Image library intros use the same card-intro size as other settings cards. Tabbed admin pages use a single `--space-3` gap under the tab strip (no stacked `.scroll-fade-tabs` margin + `.screen` flex gap).
- **The hover-tooltip-on-a-title pattern (info-circle icon plus `Tooltip`) is now one shared `HintLabel` component (`@admitto/ui`).** Replaces 7 hand-rolled copies of the same JSX block across Event archiving, the Audit/Security log column headers, and Active sessions' "Logged in" column, plus two duplicate CSS classes (`audit-log-scope-header`, `sessions-col-header`) that defined the identical `align-items`/`gap` rule under two different names. No visual change at any existing call site.
- **Sidebar footer and mobile/tablet nav drawer visual refresh, matching the design mockup.** The footer's plain version text (`vX.Y.Z`) is replaced with a small monospace label and a "Report a bug" icon link to the GitHub issue tracker — shared by all instance shells (Admin, Users & roles, Settings, Operator, My account) via `InstanceSidebarFoot`. The mobile/tablet (≤1024px) nav drawer is narrowed from a full-viewport-width overlay back to a `min(240px, 85vw)` sliding drawer over the existing dimmed backdrop, so the rest of the screen stays visible instead of being fully covered; the brand mark stays visible either way since that fix (#453) was a `.sidebar` z-index bump unrelated to the drawer's width, and the dedicated close button added alongside it is unchanged (still needed since the drawer, narrow or not, still sits over the hamburger toggle).
- **Admin and operator pages no longer stretch edge-to-edge on wide (24"+) monitors.** The shared `.content` wrapper that every admin page renders through — event-scoped (Overview, Attendees, Requirements, Communication, Check-in, Reports, Event settings, Attendee Detail) and instance-level (Events, Users & roles, Account, Settings) alike — had no `max-width` at all, and the operator kiosk shell capped itself independently at a hardcoded `1100px`. Both now share one `max-width` (`--container-max`, 1320px — an existing design token that was defined but never wired up) centered with `margin: 0 auto`, so the whole app reads consistently at any monitor size instead of only looking right on a laptop screen.
- `argon2` bumped to 0.45.1; updated `packages/auth/src/password.ts` for the library's `Options` → `HashOptions` type rename. Type-only fix — `hash()` still returns the same encoded PHC string (not raw bytes) with no `raw` option set, confirmed by the full `@admitto/auth` suite including integration tests that write the hash straight into `password_hash`.
- **"Sessions" and "Bulk revoke operator sessions" move from Organisation settings → Security into Users & roles, as a new superadmin-only "Active sessions" tab**, matching the design system's already-settled information architecture (its own mockup history shows this same move happened there first). Security's Session policy section keeps only its policy fields (admin/operator session TTL, remember-device duration) plus a new "Manage individual sessions in Users & roles →" link. Front-end only — every session route (list, single revoke, device-label edit, per-event bulk operator revoke) was already superadmin-gated identically, so no backend, API, or permission changes were needed. Polished to match the rest of the app's own conventions: the All/Admins/Operators filter is now the shared `Segmented` control living in the Card header (matching the Logs page's own System/Audit toggle) instead of a bespoke button row; Edit/Revoke are now small icon buttons (revoke turns red on hover, edit stays neutral) instead of text buttons; the Role column is color-coded the same way Staff users already colors it (superadmin/admin/operator); the "current session" marker moved from a second badge crowding the Role column into a compact pill under the user's own name; "Logged in" now shows UTC with the viewer's own local time underneath (an "i" tooltip on the column header explains it), mirroring the Audit/Security log's own dual-timezone convention; the disabled self-revoke button explains why via the shared hover `Tooltip` instead of a native `title`; and the table paginates (25/50/100/200 rows per page, mirroring the Logs table's own footer) instead of always rendering every row at once. A second pass fixed the Card header's height to the same `--control-h-sm` standard every other in-header `Segmented` control already uses (it was inheriting a taller, standalone-context size); replaced the "current session" pill (which had pushed the row to a cramped third line) with nothing at all, since the already-disabled, already-tooltipped Revoke button on that same row already says so; added an `Avatar` next to the user's name, matching Staff users' own row layout; renamed "Auth" to "Sign-in" with a key/Local or cloud/SSO icon+label pair instead of a bare "Local"/"OIDC" string; and the "Active sessions" tab label now shows the loaded session count, matching "Staff users"'s own count. A third pass: Edit/Revoke no longer wrap onto their own line and drift left at narrower desktop widths (now a non-wrapping, centered pair); the empty state is now the shared `EmptyState` component (icon + title) instead of a bare sentence; and the table now switches to stacked cards below the desktop breakpoint (matching Staff users/Role assignments' own responsive pattern) instead of staying a horizontally-scrolling table that was unusable on phones and cramped on tablets. Fixed the pager itself disappearing whenever a result set fit on a single page (e.g. 8 sessions at 25/page) — it now always shows, exactly like the Logs table's own footer does, so "Page 1" (Previous/Next both inert) stays visible instead of the whole row vanishing. (A same-round detour that added clickable page-number buttons in place of the plain "Page X of Y" text was reverted — the plain Previous/Page X of Y/Next, identical to the Logs table's own footer, is the intended design here, not a numbered pager.) Bug-bot review follow-up: the empty state no longer claims "No active sessions" when sessions exist but the Admins/Operators filter matches none — that case now shows its own "No sessions match this filter" message instead. A fourth pass, from PO review on a live phone-width build: the mobile card's Edit/Revoke icons move from their own row at the card's bottom onto the same line as the role badge, so the card no longer grows an extra row's worth of height just to hold them; Edit is now the same `sm` icon size Revoke already was (they read as mismatched sizes on the same row otherwise); and the name/email now truncates with an ellipsis instead of overflowing past the card's right edge on narrow phones, once the badge and both icons are competing for the same line.
- **The Users & roles tab bar now uses the same scrollable, arrow-hinted `ScrollFadeTabs` component Organisation settings already uses**, instead of the plain, non-scrolling `Tabs` — "Staff users", "Role assignments", and "Active sessions" no longer wrap onto two lines on a narrow screen; the strip scrolls horizontally with edge-fade + arrow buttons once the labels don't fit, and always keeps the active tab in view. Also fixed `ScrollFadeTabs` itself (shared by Organisation settings too) leaving the active tab's edge just barely clipped under the right-edge scroll arrow's own solid-background overlay when landing directly on the last tab (e.g. via a deep-linked URL) — `scrollIntoView`'s "nearest" only guaranteed bare visibility, not clearing the arrow's 28px zone on top of it; now nudges the remaining distance so the tab's edge lands past the arrow, not just barely on-screen under it. Also fixed 6 form fields flagged by Chrome DevTools ("form field element should have an id or name attribute") — the Staff users search box and role/status filter selects, and the Edit user modal's organization-scope, event-scope, and assign-role selects — accessibility/autofill-only fix, no behavior change.
- **The "Active sessions" tab's count now shows on page load, not only after clicking into that tab** — it's now always mounted (hidden, not unmounted, while another Users & roles tab is active) so its own fetch runs eagerly, matching how "Staff users"'s own count was already ready immediately regardless of the initially-active tab. Fixed a same-round regression this introduced: the "Sessions" and "Bulk revoke operator sessions" cards lost the standard 18px gap between them, because the new always-mounted wrapper div sat between them and the page's own gapped container instead of letting them flatten into it directly.
- **The Sessions table no longer needs horizontal scrolling at tablet-portrait widths (768-1180px)** — it stays a real table there (per PO request, unlike the phone-width card layout), just with the Device, IP address, and Sign-in columns hidden (still fully available in the mobile card view, and Device via its own tooltip on desktop) so the remaining User/Role/Logged in/Last active/Action columns fit without a scrollbar. (Bug-bot review follow-up: the hidden-columns breakpoint started one pixel later than the table itself switches on, so a 768px-wide viewport — a real iPad's own portrait CSS width — briefly showed every column again; both now agree at 768px.)
- **Staff role labels are now consistently capitalized everywhere they're shown to a human, and "Admin" is now spelled out as "Administrator"**: the topbar user menu, Staff users' role filter, Role assignments, Active sessions, the Invite/Edit user role selects, and the Security tab's "Require 2FA for roles" all now read the same "Superadmin" / "Administrator" / "Operator" from one shared source (`auth/role-labels.ts`) instead of each surface spelling (or capitalizing) it independently — two of them previously showed the raw wire value ("admin") unstyled. (Staff users' own per-row role badges intentionally keep their compact two-letter form, SA/AD/OP, since a row can carry more than one role badge at once and the full word wouldn't fit; only their badge color was already reading from the shared source.) The topbar user menu's role badge color also now matches the same superadmin/administrator/operator → red/amber/blue scheme every other role badge in the app already used, rather than its own, different three-color set. Bug-bot review follow-up: Role assignments' revoke-confirmation text and the Edit user modal's assigned-role badge color now also read from the same shared source, closing the last two spots that still showed a raw or unstyled role. Only the label and badge color changed — the underlying role value stored and sent to the API is unchanged.
- Unify the TOTP→backup→emergency step-up fallback chain: `login.ts`'s `completeMfaInTransaction` (core login-time MFA verification) now calls the same `verifyTotpOrRecoveryCodeDetailed` primitive already used by OIDC account-link step-up and self-service MFA reset, instead of an independent third copy of the same lookup/consume sequence. `verify-step-up-code.ts` gains a detailed variant returning which method matched (`totp`/`backup`/`emergency`) so login's per-method audit logging is unaffected; the existing boolean `verifyTotpOrRecoveryCode` is now a thin wrapper, so its two other callers are unchanged (no behavior change).
- Operator mobile camera overlay: a successful check-in (fresh admit or an attendee found already checked in) now steps through any **badge / gift bag / headset items still to hand out**, one at a time — icon, item name, and an "Issue X"/"Return X" button, with Back and Skip to move without acting — instead of leaving a phone operator with no way to issue these items at all (they previously had to switch to a desktop). Skipping doesn't change the item's state — it's still there to action on the next scan or from desktop. A short summary screen follows the last item — "All items issued" only when everything genuinely was (a green checkmark there when items were actually skipped would be a false confirmation); otherwise "N item(s) skipped" with a neutral/warn tone and the same ✓ issued / – skipped list — with **Next scan** and, when this was a fresh admit on this device, **Undo last check-in**. The "Confirm check-in" card itself is unchanged; this step only appears afterward, on a bordered card (not a solid color fill, for readability with a list of items) rather than folded into the result card. "Auto-advance after valid check-in" no longer sweeps this step away before it renders — on the mobile overlay, that setting now defers to whether there's still an item to hand out; desktop's auto-advance behaviour is unchanged. Mobile only — the desktop inline camera keeps its existing item list on the separate attendee card. PO review, round 2: the step now renders inside the camera viewfinder's own frame instead of replacing the whole screen — Manual search and Recent scans stay visible below it exactly like during a normal scan. Every configured item gets a step, including one already auto-issued before this screen opened (e.g. Badge via "Issue badge at entry") — shown as an "Already issued — remember to hand it over" reminder with a **Next** button, so the operator still gets a physical prompt for it instead of it being silently excluded. Navigating Back to an item already resolved during this flow shows the same reminder instead of bouncing straight past it. The icon sits in a round tinted badge (reusing the same light-on-dark treatment as the attendee card's status icon) instead of bare on the dark background; the frame's corners are gently rounded, matching the camera viewfinder and the full-color result card so all three share one consistent shape; the action button is white to read cleanly against the border, and Back/Skip/Next are small chip buttons instead of plain text. PO review, round 3: fixed the summary screen showing a green checkmark and reading as a success confirmation even when every item had just been skipped — it now only shows "All items issued" with the green tone when everything was actually handed out; otherwise it reads "N item(s) skipped" with a neutral/warn tone instead. The step's background is a light card surface (the app's standard page tone — pure white glared against the dark overlay, and the earlier translucent tint read as an empty outlined box) with dark text/icons. Every screen of the flow (a step, or the summary) is one centered column — the same layout pattern the result card already uses for "Confirm check-in" — so the primary action button lands at the same position across items instead of drifting with the content above it; everything that varies between items (description, detail, the already-issued note) reserves a fixed height so it can't shift that position either. Each step now also shows the item's admin-configured **description** from the Requirements page (capped at 500 chars server-side, clamped to two lines in this view so a long text can't break the layout; the card API — including the item's `description` field — needed an actual rebuild of `@admitto/tickets`, not just a type-check, to reach the running server; that's now done), alongside the existing per-attendee detail line (e.g. "T-shirt size: L"). The "already issued, remember to hand it over" note is a plain subtitle line (matching the result card's own status subtitle, not a pill — the pill sat awkwardly against the button). The step's primary button is a softer green (`--status-ok-hover`) rather than the same vivid green as the check-in result background, and rather than white (glared against the now-light card background). Re-scanning an **already-checked-in** attendee no longer takes over the screen with the item flow — the standard "Already checked in" result card shows as before, with a small **Issue items** chip (only when something is still pending) that opens the flow on demand. That chip and **Cancel** (now a real button, not an underlined link, on mobile) always sit together in the same row on every result card, so Cancel's position doesn't move depending on whether Issue items is present. Also fixed the item action label for gift bag items showing the raw internal key ("Mark gift_bag issued") instead of "Give gift bag" — the label lookup checked for the key `giftbag`, but real event items are slugified with an underscore (`gift_bag`); this label is shared with desktop's attendee card, so it's corrected there too. PO review, round 4: the item-issuing card is now a top/bottom-anchored layout instead of a vertically centered stack — the icon sits at a fixed offset from the top (matching the icon's position and 76px size on the main "Confirm check-in"/"Already checked in" cards exactly) and the primary button at a fixed offset from the bottom, on every screen (a step, or the summary), regardless of how much content sits between them; previously both drifted because a centered stack's position depends on total content height, which varies per screen (extra progress/nav rows, a long description, a short vs. long summary list). The step-progress dots moved out of their own row (which either collided with the icon above it or shifted the button below it) into the existing Back/Skip row, laid out with `grid-template-columns: 1fr auto 1fr` so they stay exactly centered regardless of whether Back/Skip are present — flex `space-between` visibly shifted them depending on the two side buttons' widths. The item description now displays in full (previously clamped to 2 lines with the rest silently dropped) — the card scrolls internally if it doesn't fit, rather than truncating admin-configured text. The already-issued state is now the same compact `Badge` + `itemBadgeVariant` component `AttendeeCard.tsx` uses on desktop, not a full sentence ("remember to hand it over") that wrapped awkwardly at card width. Fixed a flicker where the summary screen briefly showed the orange "N item(s) skipped" tone before flipping to green "All items issued" — `onItemAction`'s API call resolves and updates the `items` prop a tick after the synchronous step advance, so the summary's first render saw stale data; a local optimistic mark (self-corrected if the call actually fails, detected via the `pending` prop settling with the item still showing as pending) removes the gap. The incomplete/skipped summary's background and primary button now also switch to the light-warn tone (previously only the border and icon did, leaving a green background under an orange border). The mobile "Confirm check-in"/"Already checked in" card's ticket-type text is now the same `TicketTypeBadge` component desktop's `AttendeeCard.tsx` uses (e.g. VIP → purple) instead of a plain, uncolored `<span>` — ticket-type coloring is sourced from one place, not redefined per surface; fixed that badge (and the person-card layout generally) rendering stretched to the card's full width from `.ck-overlay__result-card`'s default flex `align-items: stretch`, instead of hugging its own content. Fixed a recurring Chrome console error ("AbortError: the play() request was interrupted because the media was removed from the document") — the camera `<video>` element used to unmount whenever scanning paused (every scan result, every entry into the item-issuing screen), which could race ZXing's in-flight `video.play()`; the video now stays mounted and is hidden via the `hidden` attribute instead. Recent scans: the count badge now matches the actually-rendered rows instead of the raw fetch total (the mobile overlay renders 6 of the up-to-8 fetched entries, so the badge previously read "8" while only 6 rows were visible); timestamps show "Today HH:MM" / "Yesterday HH:MM" for admissions in the last two calendar days instead of a full date, more useful for a live feed than `formatAdmissionDisplay`'s "same as the event's calendar day" rule; each row also shows the operator's device label (`checked_in_at`'s `device_id`, e.g. "Entrance A" — set at login, optional) next to the ticket type, so scans from multiple simultaneous operators can be told apart. Code review pass before merge: item-action failures (e.g. a network blip while issuing a gift bag) previously showed no error at all on the mobile overlay — the only error surface rendered in a part of the page hidden behind the overlay's own full-screen layer; failures now show inline inside the overlay itself. "Auto-advance after valid check-in" could still sweep the item-issuing step away before the operator saw it when every configured item was already auto-issued (e.g. a single Badge item with "Issue badge at entry") — the auto-advance check now matches the item screen's own "has any items" rule instead of only checking for ones with a pending action. Manual search's Enter-submit (raw token / exact match) now closes the search screen once the check-in actually succeeds, and stays open with the inline error otherwise — it previously never closed on its own. Switching into Manual search no longer tears the camera's `<video>` out of the document (it's hidden instead, same fix as the earlier AbortError fix above, which only covered the other trigger paths). A fast double-tap on "Issue" or "Undo" no longer fires the request twice — neither button's disabled state was actually driven by whether that request was in flight. Navigating Back to an item marked done only by the optimistic local mark (server hasn't responded yet) now shows the action just taken ("Already issued") instead of the stale pre-action state ("Already pending"). Scrolling the mobile overlay to see recent scans below the fold could carry the header — and its only "Exit camera mode" button — off-screen; the header now stays pinned to the top of the scroll area. "Yesterday HH:MM" on recent-scans timestamps could resolve one day too far back in the ~1 hour window right after a spring-forward clock change, from computing "yesterday" as a fixed 24-hour subtraction instead of a genuine calendar-day-minus-one (#434).
- My account page (#324): Profile section redesigned — 2-column grid on desktop (editable fields left: display name + regional format; read-only right: email, sign-in method, roles); `<select>` replaced with `Select` from `@admitto/ui`; "Date format" label renamed to "Regional format" with updated hint; new read-only "Sign-in" row shows `Local password`, `Identity provider (SSO)`, or `Local password + Identity provider` based on `has_local_password` and `roles[].is_oidc`; 2FA status badge and action button are now on a single compact row; Password and Two-factor authentication are two independent Cards side by side in a CSS grid — both cards always stretch to the same height, and the 2FA card never grows taller than the Password card during enrollment; the "Set up authenticator" enrollment view shows the QR code in the left column and backup codes + TOTP input in the right column (6 individual digit boxes, auto-advance/backspace/paste, matching the MFA wizard style); backup codes grid is 2-column so all 10 codes fit without overflowing the card height; `DELETE /api/account/mfa/totp/enroll` endpoint added — the Cancel button calls it before hiding the enrollment UI so a fresh "Set up authenticator" always generates new backup codes. Active sessions table scrolls within the card at `max-height: 380px` with sticky header.
- **Reports' admission log, "By device" breakdown, and CSV/PDF exports now attribute each check-in to the operator who performed it (`Attendee.admitted_by`), not just a self-declared device label.** An admin-role check-in from the admin SPA never prompts for a device label (`DeviceLabelStep` only gates the `/operator` kiosk route), so it previously showed as "(unlabeled device)" in every report even though the real authenticated operator was already recorded server-side. The breakdown card is now "By operator" (operator display name, falling back to email, then "Deleted user" for a since-removed account, then "(No operator)" for the legacy/emergency bearer check-in path with no authenticated session); device label becomes a secondary annotation shown next to the operator only when a session actually set one. CSV keeps operator and device as separate columns (raw data); the on-screen table and PDF export merge them the same way ("Anna Kowalska (Tablet 1 — main entrance)").
- **Health check File storage is live for local uploads.** Core infrastructure probes `UPLOAD_DIR` (must be an existing searchable/writable directory; **Run live checks** also writes and deletes a tiny probe file). A missing root is **degraded** (local adapter creates it on first branding upload), not an outage; a path that is a regular file is **down**. `STORAGE_PROVIDER=s3` (and unknown providers) report degraded until S3 ships. Health dump **version/commit** come from the served admin SPA build (`dist/build-meta.json`, same identity as the sidebar footer), then `GIT_COMMIT`, then `git rev-parse HEAD`.
- **Event Settings card copy and field hints:** visible card descriptions on Basic information, Address, Images, Ticket types, and Mailing; info tooltips no longer repeat that copy; per-field hints under Basic information match Address (`.at-hint` size and spacing).
- **Requirements' "Event behaviour" card is renamed to "Check-in behaviour."** All four toggles it holds (issue badge at entry, confirm on scan, manual lookup, auto-advance after a valid scan) are specifically about how the check-in screen behaves for operators, not the event in general.
- **Event Overview's 4 KPI tiles and Reports' 4 stat tiles now share one icon/value/label size and a fixed card height** (PO review), replacing two independently-tuned sets of pixel values (56px vs 44px icon, 28px vs 26px value, 16px vs 13px label). Both tiles now render at Reports' smaller size. Card height is also now a fixed constant (76px on desktop, 96px on Overview / 108px on Reports at the 2-up mobile breakpoint — Reports' tile is one line taller, carrying a sub line Overview's doesn't) instead of following whatever the value/label happen to render at: previously, a long Overview value (e.g. the countdown tile's "Ended today") could wrap onto a second line and, via CSS Grid's default cross-axis stretch, grow every sibling tile in that same grid row along with it. At desktop widths values/labels now truncate with an ellipsis rather than wrap; at the mobile breakpoint, where each tile has more relative width to work with, they wrap onto a second line instead of truncating.
- **Overview page — the four stat tiles (Attendees, Tickets sent, Event countdown, Failed delivery) no longer wrap their value onto two lines at tablet widths.** `.overview-stats` shared its 2-up collapse breakpoint (900px) with the two-card row below it, but these tiles carry a fixed-width icon square plus value/label text rather than just a title, so they need roughly twice the per-tile width before wrapping looks acceptable — raised to 1024px; the two-card row below keeps its own 900px breakpoint, since its content (timeline list, donut + legend) stays readable at 2-up down to a narrower width.
- **Overview page's Recent activity "All"/"Issues" filter now uses the same segmented-control standard as Instance Settings** (`Segmented`, e.g. the Security → Audit log "System"/"Audit" toggle), replacing a bespoke pill-shaped button pair that was the only place in the app still using that one-off pattern. The adjacent "Live" badge already used the app's established dot-badge pattern (`Badge variant="ok"` with a pulse), so it was left as-is.
- **Overview page's check-in progress ring and its "Not yet checked in" legend dot were hard to see against the card's white background.** Both used `--surface-sunken`, a near-white "recessed well" token that nearly disappeared here — swapped to `--border-strong`, a neutral gray with real contrast against white.
- **Attendee Detail page's actions reorganized to match the design mockup.** "Revoke check-in", "Revoke items", and "Revoke pass" (or "Restore pass" once the pass is already revoked) no longer live in a standalone red "Revoke" header menu — all three now sit together, in that order and with no dividers between them, inside the "More actions" menu, directly above "Delete attendee". "Restore pass" moves into the same spot and now goes through its own confirmation dialog instead of applying immediately on click. Below the 768px breakpoint, "Edit" folds into the same menu too, since there's no longer enough header width for Edit, Revoke/Restore, More, and Back as separate buttons at once; every item in the menu also gains a short one-line description under its label, matching the Attendees list's own bulk "More actions" menu. "Revoke check-in" now stays visible and simply disables with a tooltip when there's nothing to revoke, instead of disappearing, matching how "Revoke items" already behaved.
- **"Registration" renamed to "Pass" on the Attendee Detail page's status strip**, for consistency with the rest of the app, which already calls this concept a "pass" everywhere else (Revoke pass, Restore pass, Wallet pass, the pass status badge).
- **Ticket-type chart colors in the Overview and Reports breakdowns no longer reuse the same near-black gray as ticket-type badge text.** The "Gray" ticket type's badge label needs `--at-gray-600` for text contrast, but reused directly as a chart-bar/legend-dot fill it was the darkest, most attention-grabbing color in the whole ticket-type palette — a new `ticketTypeChartColor()` helper (`@admitto/ui`) softens it to `--at-gray-500` for graphical fills only, so a "Gray" ticket type now reads as a neutral/default swatch instead of visually dominating the chart.
- **Prisma upgraded from 5.22 to 7.9.1** (direct 5→7; none of v6's own breaking changes affect this schema, so there was nothing to gain from an intermediate step) across all 8 packages that depend on it. Driver adapters are now mandatory in v7 (`@prisma/adapter-pg` + `pg`, replacing the built-in Postgres query engine); the previous connect/idle pool timeouts (5s / 300s) are set explicitly, since v7's own new defaults (no connect timeout at all, 10s idle) would otherwise let a request hang indefinitely against an exhausted pool instead of failing fast. A generator `output` path is mandatory too — the generated client moves from `node_modules/@prisma/client` to `packages/db/src/generated/prisma`, re-exported from `@admitto/db` (and a new side-effect-free `@admitto/db/client` subpath) instead of the 250 call sites across the repo that previously imported `@prisma/client` directly; a new `@admitto/db/testing` subpath centralizes adapter construction for the ~86 test files that build their own client. Config (schema/migrations path, seed command, datasource URL) moves to a new `packages/db/prisma.config.ts` — `package.json#prisma` and `datasource.url` inside `schema.prisma` are both hard errors in v7. `prisma.$use()` middleware is removed outright in favor of `$extends()`; the one test relying on it (simulating a check-in TOCTOU race) is rewritten around a mutable, per-test "armed" flag on an extended client built once. `docker-entrypoint.sh`/`Dockerfile`/`infra/scripts/apply-test-schema.sh` all gain an explicit `--config packages/db/prisma.config.ts` (v7's config-file auto-discovery only checks the CLI's own working directory, not parent or child directories); the Docker builder stage now snapshots `node_modules` before `npm prune --omit=dev` and restores whatever pruning removed, since the new config loader (`@prisma/config` → `c12`/`jiti`) pulls in several new, hoisted transitive devDependencies the entrypoint still needs at container startup — replacing the old hardcoded `prisma`/`@prisma`/`.prisma` copy-dance (the last of which v7 no longer even creates). Caught one bundling regression before it ever shipped: two `@admitto/tickets` modules unreachable from the admin SPA's own code (dead re-exports off its barrel) were still pulled into the browser bundle, because no package in the repo had ever set `sideEffects: false` and Rollup couldn't prove they were safe to drop — one of the two uses `Prisma.sql`, which v7's generated client only exposes through the same runtime module that ships the WASM query-compiler, regardless of whether a `PrismaClient` is ever constructed. Adding `sideEffects: false` to `packages/tickets/package.json` (audited: the package has no top-level side effects) let Rollup drop both dead files entirely, taking ~4.8MB of unused WASM back out of the admin bundle (restoring the pre-upgrade dist size byte for byte).
- **The Archiving tab (Organisation settings) is redesigned end to end, matching the visual weight of the Logs & Audit view.** Active and Archived are now one toggle in the card header (like Audit's System/Audit/Security switch) showing one table at a time, instead of two tables permanently stacked on the page; each view paginates (25/50/100/200 rows per page, Previous/Next, matching Audit's own footer) instead of rendering every event at once. The event title links to its overview page; the "Event" and "Event date" column headers gain an info-tooltip (clarifying the line below the title is the URL slug, not an internal ID, and that the date is the event's own, not its creation date); a new Attendees column reuses data already fetched. Archive/Unarchive actions are right-aligned `size="sm"` buttons, matching the app's other compact row actions (previously full-size, reading as unusually heavy for a settings table); the "Action" column header is now screen-reader-only rather than a redundant visible label. The card's own "Archive completed events…" hint moves into an info-tooltip on the "Event archiving" title instead of sitting as a permanent paragraph. The empty state is now the shared `EmptyState` component (a centered icon and message) instead of a bare, iconless sentence. Below 768px the table is replaced by a one-card-per-event list (matching Attendees'/Audit's own desktop-table/mobile-card split) instead of letting narrow columns wrap cell text into unreadable fragments. The "Data deletion is a separate ops step" hint line is removed — deletion is already its own, unrelated, safety-gated flow elsewhere.

- **Admin SPA — route-level code-splitting cuts the initial JS bundle by ~60%:** every admin/operator page (Attendees, Communication, Reports, Check-in, Event/Instance Settings, Users, Account, Setup wizard, …) now loads on demand via `React.lazy` + dynamic `import()` instead of shipping in one entry chunk. The entry chunk drops from 847 kB minified (239 kB gzip) to 342 kB (107 kB gzip), eliminating Vite's "some chunks are larger than 500 kB" build warning; guards, auth/connection providers, and layout shells stay in the entry so route protection is unchanged. In-app navigations keep the current view while the next page's chunk loads (React Router wraps navigations in `startTransition`); only a cold deep-link into a lazy route briefly shows the existing shell-loading spinner.
- **Check-in — desktop attendee card: item list is now labeled and shows each item's description:** The **Badge / gift bag / headset** rows under Confirm check-in previously had no heading and no description — just an icon, a label, and an action link, easy to read as inert status text rather than something the operator needs to act on (Jadzia/PO review). The list now has an **"Items to hand out"** section label (same small-caps style as the Notes section) and, when configured on the Requirements page, each item's admin-written description directly beneath its row — matching what the mobile check-in overlay's item-issuing screen already shows. PO review, round 2: the item icon is now centered against the label-and-description pair as a whole (previously only against the label line) and a touch larger (16→20px) so it reads as its own element rather than disappearing next to two lines of text. The action link ("Issue badge") is now a real green button reading **"Mark badge issued"** / **"Mark gift bag given"** — the operator is confirming a hand-over that already happened, not instructing the system to perform one, so the button should read as an attestation rather than a description of an automated action (Jadzia review); this label is shared with the mobile item-issuing screen, so the wording changed there too. PO review, round 3: the button's visible text is now just **"Mark issued"** / **"Mark given"** — the item name it used to repeat is already the row label right next to it (desktop) or the heading above it (mobile), and repeating it made the button too wide; the full "Mark badge issued" / "Mark gift bag given" phrasing lives on as the button's `aria-label` so screen readers navigating by button list still get the item name. The button is now also centered against the label-and-description pair as a unit, same treatment as the icon. The Confirm-check-in screen's second, block-width **Clear** button directly under Confirm check-in — visually competing with it — is gone; Clear now lives only as the small ghost button next to Add note, the same place it already appears on every other card state. PO review, round 4: the card's status badge (Ready to check in / Already checked in / Revoked / Invalid ticket) is now **one consistent treatment for every status** — right-aligned in the head, centered against the name+ticket-type pair, no leading dot — instead of a dotted badge shown inline only for the positive (ready/valid) states plus a separate full-width colored bar duplicating the same text for every other state. The bar is gone; a revoked/invalid ticket's reason (e.g. "Ticket is not admittable") now reads as a plain line under the head instead of a tinted banner repeating the status title first. PO review, round 5: that reason line is gone too — the badge already reads "Revoked" / "Invalid ticket", so repeating "Ticket is not admittable (cancelled or revoked)." underneath it was redundant.
- **Requirements — Event items: select-type options textarea accepts Enter for new lines:** The options textarea for select-type attendee data fields now correctly preserves newlines when typing — pressing Enter adds a new option line. Options are stored one-per-line and displayed one-per-line in the editor; existing comma-separated options in the database are still loaded correctly.
- **Requirements — Event items are empty by default, except "Badge":** New events no longer auto-seed giftbag / headset — operators add them manually via **+ Add item** in Requirements (#367, #368). The **"Badge"** item is still auto-created for every new event, and lazily backfilled for legacy events missing it (as soon as their Requirements list is opened, or on their first check-in): the "Issue badge at entry" behaviour toggle is hard-wired to an item with key `badge`, so without it the toggle would be a no-op with no way to enable it from the UI. Legacy events keep their other existing rows unchanged; the delete guard now applies only to the "Badge" item (other items are deletable when no issued/returned states exist).
- **Requirements — "Badge" item can't be deleted, only disabled:** Deleting it used to silently "succeed" and then reappear with blank fields on the next Requirements load (the backfill immediately recreated it) — confusing, with no error shown. The **Delete item** button is now disabled for "Badge" with a tooltip explaining it's a default item; the API also rejects the delete (`409 default_item`) as a server-side backstop. To remove Badge from check-in, turn its **Active** toggle off instead — doing so automatically turns off **"Issue badge at entry"** in Event behaviour too, so the two settings can't drift out of sync (#367 follow-up).
- **Requirements — "Issue badge at entry" toggle now stays in sync when Badge is disabled:** Previously the toggle could stay ON (with only a passive warning banner) after the "Badge" item itself was turned off, which was confusing — nothing was actually happening. The toggle is now disabled with an explanatory tooltip whenever "Badge" is inactive, so it can't be switched on until "Badge" is re-enabled; the API enforces the same rule server-side (`409 badge_item_inactive`). Also fixed a clipping bug where hover tooltips inside the item edit modal (e.g. on the disabled Delete button) could spill past the modal's rounded right edge and get cut off — tooltips now grow leftward from their anchor instead of centering (#367 follow-up).
- **Requirements — Badge sync + tooltip readability, round 2:** The "Issue badge at entry" toggle is now also disabled (with tooltip) when the Badge item has **"Issue on check-in"** turned off, not just when it's fully disabled — previously that combination left a dead toggle with no visual cue. The disabled toggle itself is now visibly greyed out (track and label dim to the disabled color) instead of staying fully colored while non-interactive — the passive orange warning banner underneath is removed, since the toggle's own disabled state now explains itself. Fixed a readability bug where disabled-button tooltips (e.g. Delete on the Badge item) rendered with a washed-out, low-contrast background — disabled elements dim their entire subtree via `opacity`, which was also fading the tooltip's dark background; tooltips on disabled controls are now hosted on a non-disabled wrapper so they keep full contrast (#367 follow-up).
- **Requirements — Event items list confirms Active toggle with a toast:** Turning an item's **Active** switch on/off in the Event items table previously changed silently with no confirmation that it saved — now shows a "Item enabled — saved" / "Item disabled — saved" toast, matching the existing confirmation pattern used for Event behaviour toggles (#367 follow-up).
- **Requirements — Event items list shows item icons:** The item name column in the Event items table now displays the item's tabler icon alongside its label and key (#370).
- **Requirements — Event behaviour copy and spacing:** Each behaviour toggle row has more vertical padding (space-4) and clearer subtitle copy: "Issue badge at entry", "Require confirmation on scan", "Allow manual lookup", "Auto-advance after valid check-in" (#369).
- **Requirements — Edit item: centered modal instead of side drawer:** The edit/delete panel for an event item now opens as a centered overlay modal (with backdrop and focus trap) instead of a right-side drawer, matching the AddAttendee modal pattern. Attempting to delete an item that is in use now triggers a toast warning instead of an inline banner. Delete confirmation dialog now correctly renders above the modal overlay (z-index fix: modal 200 < ConfirmDialog 300). All API-level errors (save and delete) fire toast notifications; inline error is reserved for client-side validation only (#371).
- **Requirements — Attendee data field row polish:** "Required" checkbox now sits on the same line as the "Display label" caption (previously a separate row, misaligned with the label text); the row's remove-row icon button is compact-sized to match; the label caption and its input are grouped with tight spacing so the row no longer has an oversized gap before the input. Field type picker icons (Text/Select/Boolean) show a tooltip on hover — a hover-only `overflow: hidden` on the button group was silently clipping the tooltip. Event item description column in the Requirements list is wider (46%) and wraps up to 2 lines with ellipsis instead of truncating to a single line (#371).
- **Requirements — "Issue badge at entry" also stays in sync when only "Issue on check-in" is turned off:** The ops-config toggle previously only auto-flipped off when the Badge item itself was disabled; turning off just its "Issue on check-in" setting (Badge staying Active) left `badge_at_entry` stuck `true` in the database even though the UI already showed the toggle as unusable — reproducing the exact drift this feature exists to prevent. Also closed a race where enabling "Issue badge at entry" and disabling Badge at nearly the same time could leave the two inconsistent — the check and the write now happen inside one Serializable transaction. `ensureBadgeEventItem` now checks for an existing row before writing, instead of attempting a write on every Requirements load and check-in.
- **Requirements — Event items table: headers restored, Active toggle guards against double-submission:** The Item / Description / Active column headers, dropped during the modal rework, are back. Rapid double-clicking an item's Active switch no longer fires two overlapping save requests.
- **Requirements — Add item modal: focus trap, Escape-to-close, and inline validation:** The "Add item" modal now traps focus and closes on Escape like every other modal in the app. The item-name and contents-row validation errors (client-side only) show inline again instead of as a toast, matching the toast-vs-inline convention (AGENTS.md); server-side errors (name conflicts, save/delete failures) remain toasts. Disabled controls that explain themselves via tooltip (Issue badge at entry, Delete item) now also expose that text to screen readers via `aria-describedby`.
