- **Requirements — badge_at_entry sync gap for events that drifted before the sync existed, plus review-round polish:** Disabling the badge item on an event where `badge_at_entry` was already stuck `true` from before this sync landed (e.g. because only "Issue on check-in" had drifted it, not the Active toggle) now correctly re-syncs it — the previous fix only re-synced on a fresh usable→unusable transition, missing already-drifted events. The "is the badge item usable" rule now lives in one shared `isBadgeItemUsable()` (`@admitto/tickets`), used by both the admin API and the Requirements page instead of three independent copies. The Add item name and contents-row inline errors now clear as soon as the user edits the field (previously stuck until the next submit) and are announced to screen readers (`role="alert"`, `aria-describedby`/`aria-invalid`), matching the pattern already used elsewhere (My account). The per-item Active-toggle double-submit guard is now a shared `useInFlightIds()` hook, also adopted by the Identity providers list. `PATCH /ops-config` retries on a Postgres serialization conflict (concurrent badge-item/ops-config writes) instead of surfacing it as a 500.
- Check-in camera: the inline viewfinder (admin desktop and operator desktop) shrank from a full-width 4:3 hero to a QR-sized square (max 460px, matching the design-system mockup) instead of a mostly-empty video panel; the header/toolbar button now reads **Disable camera** while active instead of only offering an X on the video, and toggling it off from the header clears any displayed scan result so reopening the camera doesn't show a stale card. PO review: the camera box now hides entirely once a scan or manual-lookup result is showing (previously it sat there as an inert, empty viewfinder next to the result, competing for space with the typeahead dropdown) — it reappears once the card is dismissed. The attendee card's footer also gained a **Clear** button for states with no other way to dismiss the card (Already checked in, Revoked) — PREVIEW already has its own block-width Clear button, so the footer one is hidden there to avoid a duplicate (#381).
- Check-in sidebar aligned with the design-system mockup (`ci-*` reference): Recent scans rows use the mockup's three-column layout — status dot, name + ticket type on the left (ellipsized), status label + monospace time right-aligned — so the full date+time format for off-event-day scans (#359) no longer wraps into the name line; the list scrolls inside the card (`max-height: min(440px, 48vh)`) instead of growing the page; the header gets a separator; manual lookup is visually separated from the list with a top border; stats follow the mockup row (same-size number columns with labels beneath, percent flush right).
- Operator mobile camera overlay: **Recent scans is no longer hidden** on phones (≤768px) — it was unconditionally `display: none`, so an operator scanning on a phone had no way to see who'd just checked in. The camera frame now top-anchors instead of stretching to fill (and vertically centering in) the remaining screen height, matching the design-system kiosk mockup's stack order: camera → manual entry → recent scans (#432). PO review: header now uses the real Admitto brand mark (was an unstyled placeholder square) and drops the event name (was cramped onto one line with the check-in count and close button); manual entry starts as a small **Manual search** button instead of an underlined text link. Also fixed the camera hint text ("Point the camera at the attendee's QR") wrapping off-center on narrow frames — `left:50%` with no width constraint let the browser compute wrap width against only half the available space.
- Operator mobile camera overlay: the scan-result card is now a **full-color status card** (green/yellow/red background, 76px icon, matching the design-system kiosk mockup) instead of a dark translucent panel with only a tinted icon — mobile only, scoped so the desktop inline camera (which reuses the same result-panel component) is unaffected. "Confirm check-in" also switched from the standard blue button to white, which the blue clashed against on the new colored backgrounds (#430).
- Operator mobile camera overlay: **Manual search is now a full-screen, live-search view** (mockup `ManualSearch.jsx` parity) instead of a small expanding token-entry field. Typing 2+ characters searches by name/email/company/ticket type after a short pause (debounced, matching the desktop typeahead's timing) and lists matches with an avatar, name, company/ticket type, and a "checked in" marker for already-admitted attendees — matching the desktop typeahead exactly (name + meta, no separate action button). Tapping a row opens that attendee's card through the same pipeline as a QR scan — never a one-tap admit straight from the list; whether check-in then needs an explicit "Confirm check-in" tap on the card or admits straight away still follows the event's "Require confirmation on scan" setting. Pasting a long token (or pressing Enter on an exact query) still routes through the existing scan-bar pipeline. Respects the event's "Allow manual lookup" setting (#433).
- Staff admin content now fills the full width beside the sidebar on wide monitors: removed `max-width: 1100px; margin: 0 auto` from `.screen` and the per-page `max-width: 720px` from `.event-settings-page` (Event Overview, Communication, and Event Settings pages are all affected); dropped the now-redundant `.events-picker-screen` rule. Intentionally narrow surfaces (operator check-in `720px`, auth forms, danger-zone description text) are unchanged.
- Attendee Detail page (#449 review): the separate "Revoke pass" and "Revoke check-in" header buttons are now one red **Revoke** button with a small menu (pass always offered while not already revoked; check-in only when currently admitted) — same two confirmation dialogs as before, just one entry point since it's the same underlying action on the attendee. "Restore pass" stays its own button once the pass is revoked.
- Check-in scan bar: the hint under the field no longer says "Keyboard wedge auto-submits" — an event-day operator reading that had no idea what a "keyboard wedge" is (PO review). It now describes what actually happens in plain language: **"Scan a code — it submits itself · type a name, then press Enter · Esc clears the field."** Pressing Enter on a pasted or typed ticket token, ticket URL, or agency-issued QR code/UUID now actually works, matching what the hint says — previously, an explicit Enter additionally required either proof the value arrived as a genuine hardware-scanner burst (so a real pasted/typed code fell through to a name/email lookup and failed with a misleading "No attendees matched that search"), then a client-side "does this look like our token" shape check that couldn't recognize a ticket URL or an externally-issued agency code either, since neither shares the internal token's shape. An explicit Enter/Search submit on a long value now tries a **scan first** and only falls back to a name/email lookup if that comes back invalid — delegating "is this a valid code" to the server's resolver, which already recognizes every valid shape (raw token, full ticket URL, agency QR payload/UUID), instead of the client re-guessing an incomplete subset of them. The silent auto-submit that fires with no Enter at all is unchanged — it still requires a genuine hardware-scanner burst, since that path has no user confirmation at all (bot review, rounds 3-6; verified live against the dev DB throughout: a pasted token, a pasted full ticket URL, and a pasted long email all now do the right thing). Round 7: the mobile camera overlay's manual-search screen closed itself the instant a long entry was submitted, rather than once the outcome was actually known — the scan attempt (and its lookup fallback) fired in the background, so a no-match or an error landed behind an already-hidden screen; it now waits for the real result before closing (bot review). Round 8: fixed a real regression from round 5's own "consistency" cleanup — the desktop camera's no-match toast is keyed on the scan status again (`INVALID` specifically), not merely "no card in the response": a `PREVIEW` response can legitimately arrive without one too (the server's own card lookup can come back empty; the client already re-fetches it a moment later), and the broader check was intercepting that legitimate, pending scan as if it were a dead end — showing the wrong toast and discarding the scan result the upcoming card fetch needed. Also removed a dead branch in the scan bar's change handler for a value containing an embedded CR/LF — `<input type="text">` strips those characters from `.value` before any JS ever sees it (HTML spec), so the branch could never run; confirmed via direct JSDOM testing before removing it. Round 9: two more gaps in the "try a scan first" logic, both about a genuine hardware-scanner burst specifically. A wedge that appends its own Enter/CR terminator reaches the Enter handler *before* the no-Enter auto-submit timer gets a chance to fire, so it went through the same "might be a search" fallback logic as manually-typed text — a burst-scanned invalid code reported as a failed name/email search instead of an invalid scan. Separately, that same length-first check meant a *short* code (an agency-issued QR payload or external UUID isn't length-constrained the way an internal token is) never even attempted a scan, regardless of how it arrived. Both are now decided by whether the current input is a genuine burst, not by length alone — a burst always attempts a scan, at any length, with no lookup fallback; non-burst input still needs to be long enough to be worth trying as a scan before falling back to a name/email search, so a short manual query doesn't pay for a wasted round-trip. Scoped precisely to the main scan bar's own two submit points (Enter, the Search button) — the mobile camera overlay's separate manual-entry field never receives wedge input and continues to always fall back on a no-match, unaffected (bot review). Round 10: the lookup fallback also needs manual lookup to actually be turned on for this event — without that check, a bad code in a **QR-only event** (manual lookup deliberately disabled) still fell into the fallback, which immediately bailed with "Manual lookup is disabled for this event" instead of the correct "This code is not valid" — confusing wording for an operator who scanned a code and never attempted a name search, and it skipped the normal invalid-scan feedback entirely (bot review).
- Check-in — desktop camera no-match: when a scanned QR matches no attendee, the camera now stays a **plain live viewfinder** and the result shows as the same **toast** manual lookup's no-match already uses, instead of a plain dark result panel borrowed from the mobile overlay (PO review round 1) or, briefly, an inline status card covering the feed (PO review round 2: on desktop the camera is scan-only — unlike the mobile overlay, it never doubles as an operator-actions surface, so no result should render on top of it). The full-color result panel is still used on the mobile camera overlay, where it belongs. Fixed along the way: the no-match toast previously never fired at all once the camera had been toggled on, because the check reading "is the camera active" lived inside a `useCallback` that isn't recreated when the camera is toggled — it kept seeing the camera as off (its value at first render) no matter what; and opening the camera while a stale no-match result from before it was turned on was still showing carried that result straight into the camera as a paused overlay with no way to dismiss it short of closing and reopening the camera (bot review, round 2) — the camera is now cleared the same way it already was when the camera got turned off. Also: submitting a new scan from the scan bar while an earlier scan's attendee card was still showing and the new one came back no-match left that stale card sitting on screen under the toast — the camera being "on" doesn't mean it's what's currently visible, since a shown card hides it the same way it pauses it (bot review, round 3); a new scan now always clears whatever card/result preceded it, whether or not the new one gets one of its own. Round 5 (self-review after the above): the no-match toast was also firing for the **Confirm check-in** button, not just scans — if the attendee row vanished server-side between the card loading and the click, that returns the same card-less "invalid" shape a no-match scan does, so confirming showed a misleading "check the QR code" toast and wiped the card the operator was just looking at; the toast now only fires for an actual scan attempt. Separately, the stale-result clearing above keyed off the camera being toggled on, not the desktop camera view actually being on screen — a tablet rotating past the desktop breakpoint while the camera stayed "on" the whole time could skip it and reproduce the original stuck-panel bug via orientation change instead of the camera button; now keyed off the same condition that decides whether the camera view renders at all.
- Check-in — Recent scans sidebar: each row is now **clickable and reopens that attendee's card**, so an operator can revisit someone they just scanned (e.g. to hand out an item they missed) without re-scanning the QR (PO review). Works on both the desktop sidebar and the mobile camera overlay's Recent scans list; rows are real buttons (keyboard-focusable), not click-only `<div>`s.
- Internal cleanups from the #454 follow-up review (no operator-visible change): the double-submit guard hook `useInFlightIds()` now owns **both** halves of the guard — the synchronous ref that blocks a same-tick double-click *and* the state Set that drives `disabled` — and `start(id)` returns whether the caller should proceed; the check-in card and mobile item-issuing overlay now use it for all their per-item and undo guards instead of six hand-rolled ref+state pairs. The attendee-card DTO's `warnings: string[]` (only ever read as a "is this pass blocked" boolean since the check-in card redesign stopped rendering the strings) narrowed to `blocked: boolean` across producer and consumers. The per-item action label/aria-label helpers collapsed to one shared "gift bag is *given*, everything else uses the raw verb" fact instead of re-listing every item key in two places.
- **Event settings reorganized into tabs:** the single long scrolling page is now five tabs — **General** (basic info + status), **Branding**, **Wallet**, **Integrations** (superadmin only — reserved for the upcoming Ingest/RSVP API tokens, not built yet), and **Danger zone**. Basic information pairs Date and Capacity into one row on wider screens instead of a single spread-out column (#390). The General tab's Status card is a compact 3-column summary (current status with a plain-language hint, organization, and the event's Created date) instead of a paragraph plus a duplicate attendee count already shown on the Attendees page; the now-redundant "Event items" card (a duplicate of the Requirements page) is removed. Danger zone is a bordered card matching every other card's styling exactly, with a small red notice below it explaining these actions can affect the event, and each destructive action (Archive/Unarchive, Export personal data) as its own row with its own plain-language explanation. Copy across the whole page was rewritten to avoid jargon ("immutable", "PII", "audit trail") for readers with intermediate English (Jadzia/PO review).
- **Unhandled API errors return JSON `{ "error": "internal_error" }`** (mapped in the admin SPA) instead of plain-text “Internal Server Error”. System logs for those failures now include `error_name` and, when present, a stable `error_code` (still no stack or exception text in the live tail).
- **"Settings" renamed to "Organisation settings"** (sidebar nav label and page heading) — the bare word "Settings" was easy to confuse with the separate, per-event "Event settings" page.
- **Organisation settings → General now has exactly one Save button.** Instance URL and the new Support contact section (below) are combined under one shared Save/Reset footer (`Promise.allSettled`, partial-failure-safe — the same pattern the Branding tab already uses), matching the "one Save per tab" standard already applied to Event Settings' Mail tab. Instance URL's own **Clear** action (which acts immediately, unlike the page-level Reset) now lives in the card's own header instead of a card-local footer.

- **Admitto product wordmark** in the expanded desktop staff sidebar, the mobile topbar brand link, and as the transport-test email header when no organisation/event logo is set. Staff chrome keeps a fixed-size mark (32px sidebar / 28px topbar) beside a text-only wordmark (tablet rail: mark only). Brand SVGs live in one place (`packages/ui/src/assets/`) and are imported or served from there.
- **The click-outside-to-close overlay behind every admin modal is now one shared `ModalBackdrop` component (`@admitto/ui`) instead of 16 near-identical, independently hand-copied `<div>`s.** No visual or behavioral change — same markup, same CSS (`position: absolute; inset: 0; background: rgba(15, 23, 42, 0.45)`, now declared once instead of 9 separate times), same click-outside-closes-the-dialog behavior everywhere it already worked. Consolidates a SonarCloud-flagged accessibility-rule pattern that had spread to 12+ files.
- **Staff-facing copy is now shorter and more consistent across the admin UI.** Dialog actions use the surrounding dialog title for context, date/time labels use plain punctuation, and item hand-over consistently uses **Mark issued** rather than a gift-bag-specific synonym. This also aligns the accessible item-action labels with the state already used by the API and status badge.
- **Mobile/tablet topbar now shows the Admitto brand while the nav drawer is closed.** Previously the hamburger toggle was the only thing in the topbar below 1024px — no brand mark, so the app looked unbranded until the drawer was opened. `StaffShell`'s topbar now renders the same brand mark + wordmark next to the toggle (hidden again once the drawer opens, since the sidebar already shows its own copy); shared across all four instance shells via a new `BrandLink` component that replaces the four near-identical inline brand blocks. The "Admitto" wordmark is nudged down 1px (`.brand-link__label`) to correct an optical illusion where its descender-less letterforms read as sitting slightly high next to the solid, symmetric brand-mark icon — applied once in the shared component so the sidebar and topbar stay pixel-consistent.
- **Sidebar/topbar chrome-height alignment refined further, and "My account" removed from the sidebar footer (#454 follow-up).** The earlier fix above matched `.sidebar__brand`'s total height to the topbar's 56px, but `.sidebar`'s shared flex `gap` (also spacing every other sidebar row) added 6px under `.sidebar__event`'s divider that `.topbar` doesn't have, leaving the two chrome bars' bottom edges still visibly offset by that amount — cancelled for just this one row (`margin-bottom: -6px` on `.sidebar__brand`) rather than changing the shared row spacing everywhere else. `InstanceSidebarFoot`'s "My account" link is removed per design review; the account page stays reachable from the topbar's user menu, so it no longer needs a second entry point.
- **The "Upload images" dropzone (event image asset library) now matches the "Event logo" dropzone's sizing, border, and typography**, so the two upload controls read as the same component instead of two visually different ones.
- **`npm run dev` for `@admitto/web` loads `apps/web/.env`** via Node `--env-file=.env`, so lab flags such as `ALLOW_PRIVATE_MAIL_DESTINATIONS` apply without a manual export. Production `npm start` is unchanged (container env only).
- CI: `scripts/test-migration-backup.sh` Scenario B (bare `app` restart → retention cleanup log line) now polls for up to 30s instead of a fixed 5s sleep before grepping logs — the fixed delay was a flaky race (container restart-to-ready time varies with CI load), most visible after #611 added a third startup retention query.
- CI: `github/codeql-action` (`init`, `autobuild`, `analyze`, `upload-sarif`) bumped to v4.37.3 across `codeql.yml`, `semgrep.yml`, and `publish-container.yml` in one commit, keeping all four pins in sync — Dependabot had split this into separate per-action PRs (#601, #599) which each individually mismatched the pinned config version and broke the `analyze` job.
- **Branding file I/O goes through `@admitto/storage`** (local adapter), including orphan GC via `list` / sweep. Upload validation, routes, and operator behaviour are unchanged; S3 remains a later option.
- **Settings → General: new "Organisation branding" card:** Superadmins can now update the organisation name and logo after completing the setup wizard, without redoing it — a new card in Settings → General reuses the wizard's existing logo upload and org-branding API. The existing instance-theme `BrandingPanel`'s hint now points to the new card instead of the setup wizard (#396).
- `SECURITY.md`: document Semgrep-on-PRs decision (Option B chosen — CodeQL remains the sole PR SAST gate, Semgrep stays on `main`-push + weekly); update required merge checks list to include `analyze` (CodeQL) and `migration-safety`.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE-FOR-AUDITORS.md`: sync Semgrep trigger note with the recorded decision.
- `Button` (`@admitto/ui`) gained a `hasMenu` prop — a trailing chevron-down so any button that opens a menu/submenu reads the same way everywhere, instead of each caller picking its own icon. Applied to the new Revoke button above.
- SonarCloud maintainability cleanup across `apps/web` (routes, auth, rate-limit, admin API handlers, page templates): removed unused/duplicate imports, replaced `parseInt` with `Number.parseInt`, simplified manual null-checks and ternaries to optional chaining/`??`/`??=`, and hoisted a nested function declaration out of `createApp` — mechanical, no behavior change.
- **Settings → Audit log and System logs: every filter dropdown is now searchable.** Action, Event (with an event icon, matching the Users & roles picker), and Security's Event type filters in Audit log, plus Source and Level in System logs, all switched from a plain dropdown to the same searchable combobox used by Users & roles' event/organization pickers, for visual consistency.
- **Settings → External services: Weather and Maps provider fields are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Attendees: every filter and the Sort by control are now searchable dropdowns.** Sort by, ticket type/attendance/check-in/mail-status filters, and rows per page all switched from a plain dropdown for visual consistency with the rest of the admin SPA.
- **Reports: the Admission log's ticket type/operator filters and rows-per-page control are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Users → Active sessions: the rows-per-page control is now a searchable dropdown**, matching the sign-in method filter already on the same page.
- **Attendee detail page: the Attendance and Ticket type fields are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Every "Rows per page" control built on the shared pagination footer is now a searchable dropdown**, including Settings → Audit log, Event archiving, and the Communication delivery log, plus a CSS fix for two earlier rows-per-page conversions (Attendees, Active sessions) whose sizing rule still only matched the old plain `<select>`.
- **Regional format (Account), resource Type (Event overview), mail Transport (setup wizard), and bounce-detection Check every are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Add attendee's Ticket type field and Communication's Send email dialog (Recipients, Attendance status, Ticket type) are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Custom attendee data fields, SSO group→role mapping rows (Role, Scope), and per-font Weight pickers are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.
- **Communication's delivery log filters (Status, Purpose, Template) are now searchable dropdowns**, matching the rest of the admin SPA's picker style.

### Fixed
- **SSO sign-in now re-syncs the account's display name and phone number from the identity provider on every subsequent login**, not just the first one — a name change in the IdP (e.g. after marriage, a corrected typo) previously never reached the local account. A superadmin's own manual edit to a user's display name or phone number in the Edit user modal still wins over the IdP and is not overwritten on the user's next sign-in. Accounts already stale from before this fix (their linked identity's last-known IdP name/phone had already drifted from the account under the old, first-login-only behavior) are repaired by a one-time backfill so they aren't mistaken for a manual override and permanently skipped.
- **Event item / ops-config admin API now retries on every Postgres serialization conflict, not just Prisma's direct `P2034` code.** Under Prisma's driver adapters, a genuine conflict can instead surface wrapped (Postgres `40001` / `TransactionWriteConflict`), which this endpoint previously treated as a hard failure instead of retrying — now it uses the same detection already relied on for OIDC role-grant revokes.
- **Bounce-ingest System Logs POST aborts after 2 seconds**, so a hung app cannot stall the mailbox poll from the ingest `finally` block.
- **Audit log shows friendly labels** for bounce detection and other previously unmapped actions (support contact, weather/maps settings, SMTP/bounce probes, location, session device label) instead of raw snake_case.
- **Edit user modal: four more review-flagged gaps closed.** Staged Role & access edits (Add/chip-remove buttons) now also disable while a profile save is already in flight, not just while the role request itself is busy - clicking Add during that window previously staged a scope that never made it into the in-flight save loop and was silently lost when the modal closed. A role-type change now closes the modal on success, same as every other immediate account action here (Reset password/Disable/Delete) - it previously stayed open showing the just-replaced role's stale chips until the next unrelated refresh, and could sit that way indefinitely if the target dropped out of a filtered Staff users list. `useOverscrollBounceGuard` now re-attaches once its dialog's own scroll element actually mounts (an `open` parameter re-runs the effect) - a dialog that renders nothing while closed previously saw a `null` ref on the hook's first run and never registered the wheel guard at all once it opened, silently disabling edge-bounce suppression for it. `useDropdownMenu`'s upward-flip check now also accounts for the nearest ancestor that clips overflow (e.g. a modal's own scrollport), not just the browser viewport - a trigger near the bottom of a tall, scrolled modal could previously open "downward" because the viewport had room even though the modal's own edge clipped the panel first.
- **Also verify bounce only counts NDRs that arrived for this check**, not an older hard bounce for the same test address still sitting in the 14-day IMAP window (which previously could report success while forwarding was broken).
- **Also verify bounce returns a failed probe result when the bounce mailbox cannot be opened** (bad credentials / unreachable host after the test send), instead of a generic server error.
- **Bounce detection matches NDR recipients without caring about email letter case**, so a manually added attendee like `John.Doe@Example.com` still gets marked bounced when the MTA reports `john.doe@example.com`.
- **Also verify bounce no longer reports a false timeout** when the bounce-ingest sidecar has already seen the same mailbox UID; the probe examines messages in its own session instead of sharing “already processed” credit with the sidecar.
- **Also verify bounce treats a brief IMAP disconnect as a retry**, instead of turning a transient mailbox error into a generic 500 for the admin.
- **Event Mail tab Save no longer skips a pending bounce-settings save** when the mail card only opens the “Revert to organization” confirmation dialog.
- **Bounce detection panel refreshes after you save the Mail card**, so switching to Dedicated SMTP unlocks “Use SMTP username & password” without a full page reload.
- **Also verify bounce keeps one IMAP login for the whole wait**, instead of reconnecting every few seconds (hosted providers often throttle that rate and the probe then times out).
- **Bounce-ingest sidecar rejects a bad `BOUNCE_INGEST_INTERVAL_SECONDS`** (empty, zero, or non-integer) at startup instead of spinning or exiting from `sleep`.
- **Send test email no longer looks identical on every click.** Each transport test gets a unique subject and body stamp, so corporate SMTP relays that suppress duplicate From/To/Subject/body (e.g. a second send to the same address) actually deliver again.
- **Attendee Detail → Delivery history** (and the Delivery details modal opened from it or from Communication) shows send times in the actor's browser timezone (falling back to the event zone), always lists the snapshot recipient as `→ email`, uses a compact row with a purpose icon (ticket vs resend) tinted by delivery status, and avoids stacking status/time/recipient as three separate full-width lines. The compact status badge is gone: the row icon carries the color, failures use Tabler's `mail-exclamation`, and hovering the icon shows the status label. The compact row no longer shows the raw SMTP/error code inline (it broke the row layout on narrow screens); the code stays on Delivery details → Raw fields, with the plain-English explanation in the Overview notice. The list scrolls inside a fixed-height panel (~5 rows) so many deliveries do not stretch the page, and the card header shows compact sent / bounced icon counts (no word labels, so the header stays on one line on mobile).
- **Bounce NDR parsing no longer lets an unrelated paragraph (e.g. a confidentiality disclaimer after the diagnostic line) leak into the stored bounce reason.** Collapsing all whitespace when stripping HTML tags removed the line break the reason capture needs to stop at, so the whole following paragraph (including un-decoded `&#347;`-style HTML entities) was saved as the bounce reason. HTML block tags (`<br>`, `</p>`, `</div>`, …) now become real line breaks before stripping, and numeric/named HTML entities are decoded.
- **Resend / bulk send mail destination failures return a clear operator message** instead of a bare “Internal Server Error” when the SMTP or webhook host cannot be resolved or resolves to a private address (SSRF guard). Power Automate destinations are checked at `createMailer` (same as SMTP), and send-time destination failures rethrow `MailDestinationError` so API mappers see them. Local lab SMTP on RFC1918 still needs `ALLOW_PRIVATE_MAIL_DESTINATIONS=true` in the web process environment.
- **`ALLOW_PRIVATE_MAIL_DESTINATIONS=true` is honored at mail-config validation** as well as connect time, so a lab SMTP host that is a literal RFC1918 address (e.g. `192.168.1.10`) can be saved instead of only hostnames that later resolve privately.
- **Four correctness bugs in the new Delivery log feature (found reviewing #669), fixed before merge.** The Search box claimed to match "name or email" but only checked the attendee's current profile fields, so a resend sent to a different address — or an attendee whose email changed since a delivery went out — could never be found by the address actually shown in the table; search now also matches each delivery's own recorded `recipient_email`. **Export log** read the search box's live, still-typing value instead of the same debounced value the table itself queries with, so exporting right after typing could silently download results for the previous (or empty) search; it now uses the same debounced value the on-screen rows reflect. The Delivery Details modal's timeline labeled steps by position ("first row = Initial send, the rest = Resend N"), which mislabels a first send made through a named custom template (recorded with `purpose: "resend"`, see `resolveNoDeliveryScopeAndPurpose`) and any later delivery that's genuinely `purpose: "initial"`; each step is now labeled from its own recorded purpose instead. A failed initial delivery-log load rendered a dead-end plain-text error with no way to recover short of reloading the whole page — especially stuck if Live polling was already paused — replaced with the same inline `EmptyState` + Retry pattern Attendees/Requirements already use for this exact case (AGENTS.md's documented convention for initial-load failures).
- **A deleted custom mail template's past sends could no longer be told apart from genuine default-template sends (follow-up to #669, out of scope there as a schema-level fix).** `EmailDelivery.template_id` is deliberately `SetNull`'d when its template is deleted (templates can be deleted even once used, by design — see the "DELETE allows ... when deliveries exist" tests), but the Delivery log table, the Delivery Details modal, CSV export, and the Template filter's "Default ticket template" option all read a delivery's template name off `template_id`/its live `MailTemplate` join, both null after the delete — so a deleted-template send silently reported as a default-template one everywhere. Fixed with a new nullable `EmailDelivery.template_label_snapshot` column, populated from the resolved template's label at send time and preferred over the live join wherever a delivery's template name is shown, exported, or filtered on; this also keeps the label accurate if a still-live template is renamed later, and the "Default ticket template" filter now requires a null snapshot too, so a deleted custom template's past rows no longer collapse into it. A new idempotent backfill (`backfillEmailDeliveryTemplateLabelSnapshot`, wired into `db:migrate` and the Docker entrypoint like the app's other post-migration backfills) populates the snapshot for every pre-existing delivery from its still-live `MailTemplate` join, so an upgraded installation's existing deliveries are also protected the next time one of their templates gets deleted — a template already deleted before the backfill runs has nothing left to recover from and is the one case that stays unfixable.
- **Event Settings → Mailing could show two independent page scrollbars at once when switching mail transport provider** (most visible SMTP → Microsoft Graph), the second letting you scroll past the actual end of the page into empty space. `.shell`'s own `overflow: hidden` only contains its own children — nothing stopped the outer document itself from also picking up a sliver of independent scroll if a transient reflow left it a few pixels taller than the viewport. `html`/`body` now get `overflow: hidden` too, scoped to pages that actually render `.shell` (via `:has()`) so the Setup Wizard — which has no dedicated scroll container and relies on natural body scroll on short viewports — is unaffected.

- **Instance Settings → Identity — row icons, toggle flicker, login page icon.** Toggling a provider's Enabled switch no longer flashes the list's hint text to "Saving changes…" and back. Each identity provider row and the Cloudflare Access summary now show a small icon tile (shield for OIDC providers, the Cloudflare logo for Zero Trust) so the two sections read as distinct types of identity source at a glance. The `/login` page's "Continue with SSO" buttons no longer show a Google logo regardless of the actual configured provider — replaced with a neutral shield icon, since the icon implied a specific vendor (and specifically Google) that most self-hosted providers aren't. PO review: the "Edit a provider to configure…" hint paragraph sat directly under the last row with no separation, reading like part of that row — removed (the row's own Edit link and switch already make this self-evident). The Cloudflare Access card's "Manage" button moved out of the card header and into the Cloudflare Zero Trust row itself, next to its icon and name, matching how every provider row already carries its own Edit/Enabled controls; the Active/Inactive badge stays in the card header. Fixed a shared `Switch` (`@admitto/ui`) bug: a disabled switch's track always painted the neutral "off" gray regardless of `checked` (an equal-specificity CSS rule order issue), so a switch that was actually on — mid-toggle, or permanently enabled-but-locked (e.g. Cloudflare Access's env-locked Enabled switch) — visually looked off while its label still read "Enabled". The disabled state is now a plain dimmed overlay (opacity, matching `IconButton`'s disabled treatment) instead of overriding the on/off color, so the switch's true state stays visible while busy or locked. PO review round 2: the icon tile wasn't vertically centered against its row's two-line name+issuer text (top-aligned instead) — fixed. The provider row's static "Enabled" text label is removed (the dim during an in-flight toggle still read as "looks off" at a glance even with the color fix above, and the constant label text next to it made that worse) — the switch keeps an `aria-label` naming which provider it toggles for assistive tech, and its on/off position is the only visual state now, same as every other icon-only toggle. The disabled dim also now transitions instead of snapping, so a fast (near-instant, e.g. localhost) toggle round-trip reads as a quick fade rather than a flash. PO review round 3: the same static "Enabled" text is dropped from the provider editor's Basics card and the Cloudflare Access editor's Configuration card too, each replaced with a `Tooltip` (a11y `aria-label` kept) explaining what the switch does, matching the list. The modal panel's scrollbar rendered as a hard rectangle cutting across the panel's rounded corner instead of following it — the panel is now an `overflow:hidden` frame around a separate inner scroll container, so the scrollbar clips to the rounded shape in every browser instead of relying on the browser's own (inconsistent) corner-clipping of overlay scrollbars. A hard trackpad swipe past the top/bottom of that same panel could still overscroll into a visible rubber-band gap even with `overscroll-behavior: contain` (that CSS only stops the gesture from chaining into the page behind the modal — it doesn't stop every browser's own bounce on the scrolling element itself); a new `useOverscrollBounceGuard` hook blocks the wheel gesture right at the boundary so the bounce never engages. PO review round 4: on narrow viewports (≤640px) each provider row's generic mobile column-stack turned it into 3 stacked lines — icon+name, the issuer URL, then Edit+Switch dangling alone on their own line below both. Icon, name, and Edit+Switch now share one line via a mobile-only `display: contents` un-nest (with `order` controlling placement independent of DOM order), so only the issuer URL — often the longest piece — gets forced onto its own full-width line beneath. Desktop layout is unchanged; this is scoped to the same `max-width: 640px` query as the existing column-stack rule. The same fix is applied to the Cloudflare Access summary row too (icon + name + Manage on one line, team domain and the "Managed by environment" badge below). Bot review: `aria-labelledby` on both modals pointed at a title element that only rendered once the form had loaded, leaving it dangling (pointing at nothing) during the loading/error/not-found states — the title (and Close button) now render in every state, not just once data is ready, which also gives loading and error states a mouse-usable way to close the modal that didn't exist before (previously Escape was the only way out if the fetch hung or failed, since clicking the backdrop is deliberately not a close action — a superadmin mid-edit shouldn't lose work to a stray click). Also caught two stale claims in this same changelog entry from an earlier pass: backdrop click never actually closes these modals (intentional, not a bug — corrected above), and the SSO login-button preview was fully removed, not "laid out in one row" as originally written (also corrected above). Fixed a real, separate mobile bug the same review surfaced: the group→role mapping repeater's row (`.identity-mappings__row`) had no `flex-wrap`, so on a narrow modal it silently overflowed instead of wrapping — also removed a dead post-flex-conversion media query (`grid-template-columns` on a flex container, and a `--hidden` cell modifier no longer used anywhere) that looked like it handled this but didn't. Second code review: Cloudflare Access's "Save changes" never navigated back on success (only `IdentityProviderEditor`'s save did), so the modal stayed open and the list behind it never refreshed, contradicting this same changelog's own "closing... refreshes it in place" claim — it now closes and refreshes like every other save/cancel path. The env-managed "Managed by environment" header badge, present on the old full-page Cloudflare Access header, was dropped when that header was rebuilt as a modal — restored. The audit log's Details popover (added below) is now positioned from its trigger's own on-screen position instead of being absolutely positioned inside the table's horizontal-scroll wrapper, which was silently clipping it on rows near the bottom of the page (that wrapper's `overflow-x` forces a matching `overflow-y` per the CSS spec); dismissing it now reuses the existing `useClickOutside` hook instead of a full-viewport invisible backdrop button, which incidentally fixed a second bug where clicking a different row's "View" while one popover was open just closed the current one instead of opening the new one. The audit log's Previous/Next scroll-restore (below) read and wrote `window.scrollY`/`scrollTo`, but this app's shell never scrolls the window itself (the real scrollable ancestor is `.main-scroll`), so the restore was a silent no-op in the shipped app; it now scrolls the panel itself into view, keyed to the specific page-change that requested it so an unrelated reload finishing around the same time (a filter change, Clear filters, Retry) can no longer apply someone else's stale scroll offset. `useOverscrollBounceGuard`'s bottom-edge check had a 2px sub-pixel tolerance the top edge was missing, despite a comment claiming both edges matched `useScrollFade`'s precedent — top now gets the same tolerance. Identity provider Add/Edit and Cloudflare Access again load as a separate chunk from the read-only list (lost when the routes moved from `App.tsx` onto this panel, since that's where the `lazy()` boundary lived), and the "Add provider" link's path is now derived from the same route constant `resolveModal` matches against instead of a second hardcoded literal that only happened to still agree with it. Third code review: both identity modals' header Close button and Escape key (unlike their footer Cancel button) stayed active while Save/Test/Discover was in flight — confirming "Discard unsaved changes?" and leaving mid-save no longer just navigates away while the un-cancellable write silently commits anyway in the background; both close paths are now disabled (and `handleCancel` itself is a no-op) for the same busy window the footer Cancel already respected. The audit log's Details popover (fixed-positioned below its trigger, above) didn't account for the viewport's bottom edge — opened on one of the last rows, it could still extend off-screen with no way to scroll it back into view; it now flips to render above the trigger when there isn't room below, the same technique already used by `DatePicker`.
- **Shared `Tooltip` component (introduced for the bulk-revoke "More actions" menu, above) now backs every tooltip in the admin SPA, replacing the old app-wide `.at-tooltip`/`data-tooltip` CSS `::after` pseudo-element system and a separate, one-off pseudo-element implementation local to the Requirements page's field-type picker — down from three competing tooltip mechanisms to one.** Prompted by a real mobile bug in the "More actions" menu: on a narrow viewport where the trigger itself spans nearly the full width, the sideways-growing tooltip had nowhere to go on either side, and rendered off-screen (`left: -87px`) instead of falling back to above/below like the fix already did on wider screens — `Tooltip` now checks whether either side actually has room before committing to a horizontal placement, falling back to the already-viewport-clamped vertical logic when neither does. `ArchivedGuard`'s `placement="above"/"below"` prop is gone entirely — it existed only to work around the old CSS system's inability to reposition itself away from a clipping ancestor or a nearby viewport edge; a real, portal-rendered, JS-measured tooltip picks the correct side itself and is never clipped by an ancestor's `overflow`, so the 9 screens that opted into `placement="below"` (Attendees list and detail, Communication, Import, Requirements' event-item delete button, Mail transport settings) needed no visual changes, just the prop removed. Status badges (mail transport connection state, check-in connection banner) and dense form fieldsets (Communication template editor, Attendee edit form, Import upload/options) moved onto the same component with no behavior change either. Bot review follow-up: the Mail transport tile grid (Instance Settings and Event Settings → Mailing) rendered each tile's clickable area smaller than its actual grid column once wrapped in `Tooltip` — the wrapper span stretched to fill the column as intended, but the button inside it kept its own shrink-to-fit sizing (unlike a div/span, a `<button>` doesn't auto-fill its container just because `display` is overridden), leaving a dead, hover-only gap next to shorter labels like "SMTP". Fixed by forcing the wrapper to block and the button to `width: 100%`.
- **Attendee detail header — page title repeated the attendee's name (once more in an unused breadcrumb trail — the only place in the app using `PageHeader`'s `breadcrumb` prop), gave no explanation of the page's purpose, "Resend ticket" read as a plain text link instead of a real action, and the page had no delete/erase entry point (partial #360).** Dropped the breadcrumb line (redundant with the H1 and the already-active "Attendees" nav item), added a purpose subtitle under the title instead (matching the pattern already used on Attendees/Reports/Communication), and "Resend ticket" is now a proper secondary button with an icon instead of a ghost/text-link style. (GDPR erasure entry point stays tracked separately in #356, out of scope here.)
- **Resend ticket (single or via the Attendees list's "Send tickets" bulk action) showed a raw "Internal Server Error" instead of an understandable message when no mail transport is configured for the event or organization.** `resolveMailConfig()` throws a plain error in that case, which none of the three routes that call it (`handleResendEventAttendeeTicket`, `handleBulkResendTickets`, and the Communication-layer `/send` bulk route) caught — all three now map it to a `mail_not_configured` API error via a shared `mailNotConfiguredResponse()` helper, instead of an unhandled 500. Shown in-context inside the still-open Resend dialog (matching `AGENTS.md`'s toast-vs-inline rule and this page's own `revokeError`/profile-save-error pattern — the dialog stays open for the operator to retry), in a bordered/tinted alert banner with a warning icon instead of plain red text, matching the existing `setup-wizard__check-error-banner` style.
- **Post-review pass on the batch above (CodeRabbit + SonarCloud):** the Attendee detail sidebar showed the new consolidated "Wallet pass" status row *and* the old standalone "Wallet pass — coming soon" placeholder card, duplicating the same information — the old card is gone. The Pass status column's nested-ternary badge selection was extracted into a `PassStatusBadge` lookup component (readability; also fixes a Sonar complexity flag), and the single-resend route's cognitive complexity was brought back under the project's limit by extracting the mail-config-error check into the same shared `mailNotConfiguredResponse()` helper used by both bulk-send routes.

- **Instance Settings → Mail transport redesign (closes #406, #407, #408, #409, #410, #411, #412):** the whole panel was one long single-column form regardless of provider — every section (Sender, SMTP connection, SMTP tuning, Microsoft Graph, Power Automate) stacked its fields one per row, wasting desktop width on natural pairs like host+port. Sections now use the same two-column grid already established for the Identity provider editor, collapsing to one column on narrow viewports. The SMTP option was labeled "SMTP (DuoCircle)", leaking an internal relay vendor's name into product copy — now "SMTP (recommended)", matching the setup wizard. TLS/STARTTLS switches sat in two separate full-width rows under one shared, easy-to-miss hint — they're now side by side, each with its own short caption. The nine "SMTP tuning" fields were a flat, undifferentiated list — grouped into toggles / connection limits / timeouts. Secret fields (SMTP password, Graph client secret, Power Automate flow URL/key) showed a confusing "Not set" next to a "Replace" button even when nothing existed yet to replace — the masked "Set ••••" indicator now lives next to the field label as a badge, and the action button reads "Set" or "Change" depending on whether a value already exists. "Send test email" could be clicked with no transport configured, always producing a confusing failure — it's now disabled with an explanatory tooltip until a transport is saved. Sending a test only ever showed a toast that vanished — there's now a persistent result panel below the button showing status, timestamp, provider, and (when available) the provider's message ID. The Microsoft Graph section had zero guidance on the required Entra app registration — added a collapsible "Entra app registration steps" walkthrough explaining the app-only (non-interactive) authentication model. PO review round 2: the Transport selector sat alone in a full-width row, leaving a large void of empty space beside it — it's now paired with the provider's one-line guide text in the same row, each sized to its content instead of stretching. The Sender section's default grid row-stretch made a short field (Reply-to, no hint text) match the height of its taller sibling (Envelope from, which has a hint), leaving a stray gap of empty space directly above "Allowed from domain" — grid rows now align to the top instead of stretching. The lone "Cancel" button shown while editing a secret gave no indication of how the new value actually gets saved — it now carries a caption explaining that the value saves with the page's own Save changes button. "Send test email" now sits in its own subtly shaded card instead of a plain top border, matching the visual weight of the rest of the panel. The result panel now also shows the recipient address and, on a failed send, whether the failure is retryable. The result panel is cleared when the transport provider is switched, instead of leaving a stale result attached to the newly selected provider. Fixed an inconsistency where a load failure both toasted and showed the same message inline with a Retry control — it's now inline-only, per the project's toast-vs-inline convention. Added `MailTransportPanel.test.tsx` (previously untested) covering secret-field state transitions, test-send gating, the result panel's success/failure/network-error paths, and the toast/inline distinction. PO review round 3: rebuilt the panel to match the actual Figma mockup (`ui_kits/admin/Screens.jsx`'s `Settings()` mail tab) instead of an ad-hoc layout — it turned out an authoritative design existed and hadn't been checked thoroughly enough in the first two rounds. The single card is now four: "Mail transport" (badge showing Configured/Not configured), "Sender", a provider-specific card ("SMTP connection" / "Microsoft Graph" / "Power Automate"), and "Send test email", each a real `<Card>` instead of `<h3>` sub-headers inside one. The round-2 transport selector+guide-text pairing is replaced by a clickable icon tile grid (one tile per provider, matching the mockup) — the empty-space complaint from round 2 doesn't apply to tiles. The secret-field "Set ••••" indicator is now a monospace pill instead of a plain badge. TLS/STARTTLS toggles reuse the same `.settings-row` pattern already established for the Identity provider list instead of a bespoke layout. The nine SMTP tuning fields are now behind a collapsible "Advanced tuning" disclosure, matching the same collapsed-by-default treatment already used for the Graph section's Entra setup steps. The test-send result (round 2's label/value table) is now a "mail preview" card simulating the received message on success — subject line, a green checkmark hero sentence, and a summary block (recipient, transport, host/mailbox, sent-at, message ID, and — on failure — whether it's retryable) — deliberately not fabricating a step-by-step "connected/authenticated/sent" checklist the mailer doesn't actually report. Save/Reset moved out of the last card's footer into one footer below all four cards, now also showing "All changes saved" / "Unsaved changes" so that state doesn't require reading a banner further up the page. PO review round 4: the SMTP/Graph/Power Automate password fields were flagged by browser password managers (Bitwarden and similar) as a login form, because a labeled "Username" field sitting next to a `type="password"` field with `autocomplete="new-password"` is exactly that heuristic — these are operator-entered relay credentials, not anyone's personal login, so `autocomplete` is now `off` and the password/username inputs carry `data-1p-ignore`, `data-lpignore`, `data-bwignore`, and `data-form-type="other"` to opt out of the major password managers' form detection. The secret field's "Set ••••" pill plus two full-size Change/Clear buttons on the next line is replaced with a single field-height box — same border/height as every other input — showing the masked value with "Change"/"Clear" (or "Set") as small text-link actions on the right, so it sits in the grid like a normal field instead of always forcing a full-width row. Editing now shows the password input with compact checkmark/X icon buttons instead of a text "Cancel" button; confirming collapses back to a compact "pending save" row (the value still isn't sent to the server until the page's Save button is clicked — confirming only tidies the field, it doesn't save early). PO review round 5: the "Saves with Save changes below" hint sat on its own line under the field while editing, growing that one row taller than every sibling field around it — it's now inline, to the right of the "Password" label, small and muted so it doesn't compete with the label the way the field names do, and the field's height never changes between its idle and editing states. Bot review (CodeRabbit, Codex, SonarCloud): a test result stayed visible and kept reading live Host/Mailbox values after any non-provider edit (e.g. changing SMTP host post-test), which could make an unsaved, untested value look validated by a stale success card — both `draft` and `secrets` edits now clear the result, the tested Host/Mailbox/Transport are snapshotted onto the result itself instead of read live, and an in-flight test-send response is dropped if the config changed before it resolved (CodeRabbit and Codex both flagged this independently). Each secret editor (SMTP password, Graph client secret, Power Automate flow URL/key) now has a field-specific `aria-label` — previously all three read as "New password" to assistive tech, indistinguishable from each other. The transport tile picker gained roving-tabindex keyboard navigation (arrow keys move and select, wrapping at the ends) — `role="radio"` alone doesn't get this from the browser for free. The result panel now labels its timestamp "Attempted at" on a failed send instead of always "Sent at", and renders as a semantic `<output>` element instead of a plain `role="status"` div. Fixed a CSS specificity bug where the transport tile's guide-text rule (`.transport-tile span`) unintentionally overrode the icon wrapper's font size, since the icon wrapper is also a `<span>`. `MailTransportPanel.tsx`'s cognitive complexity (31, over the 15 lint threshold) is down after splitting the four provider-specific sections and the result preview into their own components, and three nested ternaries became plain if/else assignments. Follow-up Sonar pass on that same commit: the main component and `handleTestSend` were still over the threshold (21 and 24) — extracted the "Mail transport" tile card and the save/reset footer into their own components too, and moved the test-send result construction and error-message mapping into small top-level functions instead of inlining six repeated per-field ternaries and an AND-chain directly in the handler. Every extracted component's props are now `Readonly<...>` per the project's lint rule for component props. Bot review (Codex): the shared `TestSendResponse` type was reused by both this transport test-send and the unrelated event-template test-send (`testSendEventTemplate`/`testSendEventTemplateById`) — making `provider` required on a successful send was correct for the transport endpoint (which always includes it) but not for the template endpoints (`communication-api-routes.ts` returns only `{status: "sent"}`, no `provider` at all), so TypeScript was giving template test-send callers a compile-time guarantee that doesn't hold at runtime. Split into `MailTransportTestSendResponse` and `TemplateTestSendResponse`.
- **Mail transport panel: raised test coverage from ~86% to ~98% of lines** (Codecov flagged the merged PR's patch coverage at 77%). Removed two now-dead code paths found along the way rather than writing tests to justify keeping them: a `hint` prop on the secret-field row that no caller ever passed, and its backing `FieldHint` component. Added tests for real gaps: keyboard navigation is correctly a no-op when the transport is locked, the "Clear" and "Change from pending-save" secret-field transitions, an invalid recipient email blocks the API call, an `ApiError` `validation_failed` test-send rejection maps to the friendly message, the stale-response guard on the rejection path (not just the success path), Retry actually re-fetches, and every Sender/SMTP-tuning/Graph field is wired to the save payload (not just the couple of fields prior tests happened to touch).
- **Archived events now grey out every mutating admin control, not just the ones already covered.** Being "archived" previously only stopped check-in — Import, Add attendee, Send tickets, Restore/Revoke pass, Resend ticket, the attendee profile form, Requirements' Add item / per-item Active & Edit / all four Event behaviour switches, Communication's Send email / New / Delete / Preview / Save / Send test and its editor fields, and Import's file upload / Preview / Commit / capacity-override checkbox all kept working on an event that was supposedly locked down. Every one of these ~30 controls is now disabled with a dark hover tooltip ("This event is archived — editing is disabled.") once the event is archived, via a new shared `ArchivedGuard` component; read-only actions (exporting, viewing, navigating, downloading templates, paging the delivery log) are intentionally left untouched. The tooltip reason always wins over any other disabled state the control already had (e.g. a save-in-flight spinner), so operators never see a stale or contradictory explanation. The tooltip's own font-weight is now set explicitly instead of inheriting from its container, fixing an inconsistent bold rendering on the Communication page. Controls that sit at the very top of a scrollable page (Attendees toolbar, Import's upload area) now grow their tooltip downward instead of upward — the default upward placement was getting visually clipped by the page's scroll-container boundary before it could render.
- **Topbar connection/mailer status indicators redesigned as compact colored icons instead of text pills** — the old "Server connected" / "Mailer: SMTP" labels didn't fit the topbar on narrow viewports alongside the account controls, worse once a mailer is actually configured. Both are now small circular icon badges (green/orange/red/gray, matching the app's existing status-token colors) with a hover tooltip carrying the full message, consistent at every breakpoint rather than only cramming on mobile. Fixes a real gap found along the way: the connection badge previously disappeared entirely once the connection degraded or dropped — it only ever rendered while healthy — so a genuine outage looked identical to a perfectly fine connection at a glance; it now shows in every state instead of only the happy one.
- **Users & roles: two real gaps closed.** Inviting or editing a staff account never checked the email actually looked like an email (new `isValidEmailFormat`, checked client-side and server-side on both create and PATCH, `invalid_email` code) - a value like `tewt` previously saved successfully. Staff users' **Active** status badge rendered gray instead of green: `StatusBadge status="ok"` isn't a real lookup key in the shared status map (only used as a *value* on real domain keys like `sent`/`admitted`), so the lookup silently fell back to the neutral variant; swapped for `Badge variant="ok"` directly, which doesn't go through that lookup.
- **Weather / Maps connection-test toasts no longer show raw machine codes** such as `invalid_base_url`; operators get clear URL guidance instead.
- **Venue search, date picker, and timezone panels use `position: fixed`** so an `overflow: auto` modal (New event) neither clips them under the footer nor grows scroll height around the popover.
- **Location map pan/zoom no longer steals the pin:** single-click explores freely; **double-click** drops or relocates the pin; drag still fine-tunes. Zoom-only draft updates no longer `panTo` the pin (so exploring away from the marker stays put).
- **Location “Verified” restore works when only `geocoding_provider` is sent** (same OSM result re-select): the PUT no longer rejects that body as an empty patch.
- **Static map attribution stays readable on dark custom tiles** with a light text outline (cache-buster `?v=5`).
- **Malformed tile URLs with userinfo are redacted in error logs** (e.g. `https://user:secret@` no longer leaks credentials when `new URL` fails).
- **Location pin provenance is always visible:** with coordinates the Address footer shows **From OpenStreetMap** (search pick / successful reverse) or **Set manually** (pin without OSM provenance, or after a free-text venue rename). With no pin, that slot shows a neutral outline **Not filled** badge (coordinates still use `-`). Empty address-grid fields use muted **Not filled** text instead of a bare dash. Re-selecting the same map pin now also persists `geocoding_provider` again so OSM status survives reload.
- **Static map tile outages no longer break ticket/mail map images:** `GET /m/{eventId}.png` retries tile composite once, then serves a short-cached “Map unavailable” placeholder PNG (HTTP 200) instead of an empty 502, so mail clients do not show a broken image while a dead tile CDN recovers.
- **Static map attribution is burned into the PNG only** (bottom-right, no white bar), so the ticket no longer shows a duplicate HTML credit under the image; mail still gets the required OSM/CARTO credit from the image itself.
- **Ticket venue/date meta stays centered with the pin inline** so long wrapped names keep a balanced layout (no icon drifting away from the text).
- **Getting there / `{{event_address}}` no longer repeats a name-only venue** when structured address components only have `object_name` and a richer `formatted_address` is already stored.
- **Public `/m/{eventId}.png` cache-buster includes map zoom** so a zoom-only Location save does not leave mail clients and browsers showing a day-old zoomed image.
- **Static map PNG attribution strip keeps provider names** by stripping HTML tags (not just `<`/`>`) from `MAP_TILE_ATTRIBUTION` before burn-in.
- **Public ticket map failure shows “Map unavailable”** instead of a broken image icon (`<object>` fallback; CSP allows same-origin `object-src`).
- **Ticket venue labels strip accidental HTML** so escaped tags are not shown as visible markup; wallet help copy states the badges are placeholders and not tappable yet; long address tokens wrap with `overflow-wrap: anywhere`.
- **Static map image omitted when `LOCATION_MAPS_ENABLED=false`:** ticket pages and mail `{{event_map_url}}` no longer embed a `/m/...png` that always 404s; Google/Apple Maps links still work when coordinates exist.
- **Static map tile downloads abort once they exceed 512 KiB** (streamed, same pattern as Nominatim), so a misconfigured or malicious tile server cannot force unbounded `arrayBuffer()` allocations on the public `/m/` route.
- **Free-text venue rename no longer restores “Verified on OpenStreetMap” after save,** and keeps `address_components.object_name` in sync with the display name so Getting there / `{{event_address}}` do not keep a stale OSM POI name.
- **Street & number often stayed without a house number for large venues** (stadiums, hotels): when Nominatim already returned city/country we skipped label merge and reverse enrichment, so a street-only tag never picked up `addr:housenumber` from the label or reverse. Label merge always runs now, reverse re-runs when the street line has no number, and glued segments like `Wybrzeże Szczecińskie 1` are parsed.
- **Location tab keeps the map pin when you edit the venue display name** (so missing OSM POIs can use “drop pin / street search, then type the name”), with a stronger no-match notice and field hints for that workflow; Create event / setup wizard still clear a picked geocode when the location text changes (no map pin there). Related Location hardening: a failed or empty reverse geocode after a pin move no longer leaves the old address/grid paired with new coordinates; the map is disabled while a save is in flight; `MAP_TILE_URL` must be CSP-compatible (`https:` in production) or the default OpenStreetMap tiles are used; Nominatim responses are size-capped while streaming and User-Agent construction shares the geocoding timeout; Nominatim's 1 req/s budget is enforced on real upstream calls (not Redis cache hits) so search-then-reverse enrich is not blocked by the API rate limiter.
- **Picking a venue suggestion applies the pin and name immediately,** then enriches the address grid in the background — so Save during that reverse lookup no longer persists only the typed query while the selected coordinates appear later as unexpected unsaved changes.
- **Map zoom on the Location tab is persisted:** Leaflet zoom controls update `map_zoom` in the draft (dirty + save) instead of only changing Leaflet's internal view.

- **Location tab: Support-contact notice, compact Nominatim addresses, and map-pin reverse geocoding.** The Nominatim usage-policy notice ("No Support contact…") now appears as soon as the Location tab loads — previously it only showed after the first address search. Venue search results and the Address line under the field use a short `Country, City - place` form built from GeocodeJSON structured fields (`addressdetails=1`) instead of Nominatim's long hierarchy label (districts, voivodeships, etc.). Clicking or dragging the map pin reverse-geocodes the new point and updates the Address (and fills an empty Venue name), while keeping a name the admin already typed or picked. **Clear map** sits in the Address card header like other settings Clear actions.

- **Event Overview — undefined `btn`/`input`/`select` classes, a duplicate-date KPI tile, a warn-tinted pinned note, and a stale "Registered" label (closes #344, #345, #346, #347, #350, #374).** The Pinned note, Key contacts, and Important links & files cards used raw `<button className="btn btn-ghost btn-sm">`-style buttons and plain `<input>`/`<select>` elements that never had matching CSS anywhere in the app (rendered completely unstyled); they now use `@admitto/ui`'s `Button` and `Input`/`Select`. Key contacts' tel/mail action icons had a stray underline on hover (the global link-hover rule was winning a cascade tie); the row's contact name now wraps to two lines instead of hard-truncating so it doesn't fight the always-visible action icons for space in the 300px sidebar. The Pinned note's filled state rendered on a custom warn-tinted background instead of the standard card surface — now a plain `Card` with the pin icon kept as a small title accent. The "Event date" KPI tile showed the identical formatted date in both its value and sub-line for events more than a week out; it's replaced by a **Failed delivery** tile (using the existing `email_failed`/`email_bounced` fields), and all 4 KPI tiles get an icon via `Stat`'s already-present but previously unstyled `icon` prop. The Attendees KPI's "Registered" sub-label is now "Active" (the count already excluded revoked/cancelled attendees; only the label was misleading), and it no longer falls back to the events-picker's raw attendee total (which includes revoked attendees) while the real overview data is loading — showing a loading placeholder instead, to avoid a visible number flash once the real count arrives.
- **Local test runs no longer emit ~11 `ExperimentalWarning: localStorage is not available because --localstorage-file was not provided` lines from admin test workers (dev-only, no runtime change).** Node 25+ ships an experimental global `localStorage` whose mere access prints that warning once per worker process, and which also shadows jsdom's own working `localStorage` in Vitest workers because Vitest skips window keys that already exist on `globalThis` (upstream vitest-dev/vitest#8757) — so the module-load read of the scanner mute preference in `scanSoundFeedback.ts` hit Node's broken storage instead of jsdom's whenever a reused fork ran a check-in test file. Fixed by disabling Node's webstorage in admin test workers (`execArgv: ["--no-webstorage"]` in `apps/admin/vitest.config.ts`, applied only when the running Node actually defines the global, since older Node rejects the flag), which restores the intended semantics (jsdom files get jsdom's real `localStorage`, node files get none), plus defensive `typeof window !== "undefined"` guards before the `localStorage` reads/writes in `scanSoundFeedback.ts` and the sidebar pin preference (extracted from `StaffShell.tsx` into `sidebarPinPref.ts` so the windowless code paths are unit-testable) — guarding on `window`, not by probing `localStorage` itself, since on Node 25+ the global "exists" and probing it is exactly what triggers the warning. No behavior change in the browser: the guards are no-ops where `window` exists. Also backfills unit tests for the previously untested `StaffShell` (render, pin toggle + persistence, hover expand, mobile nav) and adds a node-environment test locking in the windowless mute-preference defaults.
- Activity Log: a `check_in_revoked` entry (from the new admin revoke action, #449) was missing from the timeline's icon/label/timezone maps, so it rendered with the raw lowercase action-type string ("check in revoked") and in UTC instead of the event's local timezone like other on-site check-in activity.
- **Users & roles' three search fields (Staff users, Role assignments, Active sessions) showed a visibly doubled focus ring** - a crisp inner line and a separate, softer outer band with a gap between them, instead of the single ring every other focus state in this app renders. Root cause (confirmed by isolating the border and box-shadow with two contrasting debug colors): unlike every other consumer of the shared `--ring` token, which applies it via `:focus`/`:focus-visible` directly on the focused element itself (`.at-input`, `.at-select`, `SearchableSelect`'s and `PhoneCountrySelect`'s own triggers), this field's border and ring lived on the wrapping `<label>` via `:focus-within` instead of on the `<input>` - identical CSS values, but rendered less crisply on a plain wrapper than on the actual focused form control. Restructured to match `.at-input`'s own pattern: the border, background, and ring now live on the input itself, with the search icon and a new clear button absolutely positioned over it (same icon, position, and hover state as the Attendees list's `.attendees-toolbar__search-clear`) instead of laid out as flex siblings. Also switched these three inputs from `type="search"` to plain `type="text"`, matching Attendees' own `<Input>`, since the native search field's built-in "×" - the only way to clear these three fields before this fix - depends on `type="search"`'s own rendering and would have silently disappeared without a replacement.
- **A Filters button's tooltip stayed open once its own panel was open**, most visible on Users & roles' three "Filter by ..." tooltips (Role/Status, Event, Sign-in method) - clicking the button to open its panel doesn't move the mouse away, so the hover that revealed the tooltip never ends, leaving a floating hint sitting awkwardly over or beside the now-open panel it was describing. `Tooltip` now watches its wrapped trigger for `aria-expanded="true"` (a `MutationObserver`, since the trigger's own open/close state is internal to the child component and doesn't re-render this ancestor) and hides itself the instant the trigger's own panel opens - generic to every `Tooltip`-wrapped disclosure button in the app, not special-cased to Filters.
- **Opening any `useDropdownMenu`-based popover (Filters panels, kebab menus, `SearchableSelect`, `PhoneCountrySelect`) could jump a scrolled page, or open off-screen at the bottom of one.** Moving focus into the freshly-opened panel used the browser's default `focus()`, which scrolls its target into view even when - as here - it's already fully on-screen next to the trigger the user just clicked; most visible on a control near the bottom of a tall page (Active sessions' bulk-revoke card). All six focus calls in the shared hook (open, close, and the four arrow-key/Home/End menu navigation cases) now pass `preventScroll: true`. A second, related bug in the same hook: a panel opened near the bottom of a scrolled page rendered mostly or entirely below the viewport instead of flipping upward - confirmed on that same bulk-revoke Event picker after scrolling to the end of a long Active sessions list, opening it only lengthened the page by the panel's height without scrolling it into view. The hook now measures the trigger's position and the panel's own height when it opens and flips the panel above the trigger whenever there's more room above than below and it wouldn't otherwise fit - the same collision-detection behavior most native dropdowns already have. Every consumer (Filters panels, kebab/more-actions menus everywhere they appear, `SearchableSelect`, `PhoneCountrySelect`, the topbar's account and system-status menus, and the Attendees/Reports export menus) now renders fully on-screen regardless of where its trigger sits on the page; `DeliveryRowMenu`'s own pre-existing fixed-position flip/clamp logic already handled this and was left as-is. `SearchableSelect` and `PhoneCountrySelect` also flip their panel's own internal layout when flipped upward - the search box stays anchored right next to the trigger the user just clicked instead of jumping to the panel's far edge, with the option list occupying the space that opened up above it in its own unchanged order.
- **Active sessions' bulk-revoke Event picker was too narrow for real event titles**, sized to fit its own "Select event…" placeholder instead of the full-width row it sits in with room to spare (now grows up to 24rem, matching `.at-select`'s own filter-control max-width). Its "Revoke all operator sessions" button gained the same logout icon used for "Revoke sessions" elsewhere and dropped the redundant "operator sessions" from its own label - the card title and hint above it already say that.
- **Users & roles filter and picker dropdowns now use the same searchable combobox, one field per row.** Staff users' Role and Status, Role assignments' Event, Active sessions' Sign-in method filter, and Active sessions' bulk-revoke Event picker were still plain `<select>` elements after `SearchableSelect` shipped elsewhere on this page; converted for consistency. Staff users' Filters panel also stacks Role above Status instead of placing them side by side - the two-column layout doesn't fit this component's own trigger+panel shape as cleanly as a plain `<select>`. Role assignments' Scope column also gained a small icon badge (event calendar vs. organization building, matching Staff users' and Active sessions' own avatar-circle treatment for their User column) ahead of the scope name.
- **Edit user: Unlink SSO moved into the More actions menu**, alongside Reset MFA, Reset password, Revoke sessions, Disable, and Delete - it was the one account action still living as a separate inline text link on the Sign-in method chip instead of there with the rest. Same confirmation flow and self-account guard as before; now also visible (disabled, with a reason) for a local-only account instead of disappearing entirely, matching how Revoke sessions and Delete account already handle their own not-applicable cases.
- **The `add-attendee-modal` shell (Edit user, Invite user, and 8 other modals) rendered with a wider gap on the right than the left.** Its inner scroll container reserved permanent space for a vertical scrollbar (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`) even on modals whose content never scrolls - the common case - leaving a dead ~15px strip on the right that read as lopsided padding. Removed; the reference pattern this shell was built from (`.identity-modal__scroll`) never reserved it either.
- **Users & roles mobile follow-ups.** The "Invite user" header button now stays beside the title on mobile at a compact size instead of dropping to its own full-width row below it (same pattern as Reports' "Export"). Staff users' per-row "Reset sessions" icon is removed - it duplicated Edit's own "Revoke sessions" action and, on mobile cards, squeezed into too little space beside the status badge and Edit icon, wrapping the badge onto an unrelated line. Search and the Filters button now share one row on phones whenever there's room instead of always stacking (`.users-page__toolbar` no longer forces `flex-direction: column`; the search field's flex-basis matches Audit/Security logs' own 12rem, its wrap-based layout's reference). Edit device label's modal title gained the pencil icon its own trigger button already has, matching every other modal in this family. The Role/event/organization search-combobox inputs (`SearchableSelect`, `PhoneCountrySelect`) gained `id`/`name` attributes (Chrome DevTools autofill improvement).
