- **Edit user modal: Role and scope selection now live in one row.** The Role picker, the event/organization scope picker, and the Add/Change button sit side by side instead of stacking as two separate rows, and Role now uses the same searchable icon+label combobox already built for the phone country-code and event/organization pickers instead of a plain `<select>` (new generic `SearchableSelect` component, extracted from `PhoneCountrySelect`'s pattern). Removed the redundant "Changes here save immediately" hint line under the section heading. When Superadmin is both the current and selected role (nothing left to add), the Role field now fills the full row instead of sitting alone at a fixed narrow width. Removing a role assignment (a chip's × button) no longer closes the whole modal - like adding one, it now stays open so several scopes can be removed in one sitting. On mobile, this row stacks to full width instead of squeezing three controls onto one line, and the phone country-code + number row gets the same mobile stacking fix.
- **Role assignments gained an event filter and a clearer Granted column.** A new event selector narrows the list to grants scoped to one event (superadmin/admin only, server-side). The Granted column shows the grant instant in UTC with the viewer's own local time underneath (same convention and column-header tooltip as Active sessions' Logged in column), instead of UTC only.
- **Staff users', Role assignments', and Active sessions' filters moved behind the shared Filters button** (`FiltersMenu`, the same trigger-plus-floating-panel already used by Attendees and Audit/Security logs) instead of sitting inline in the toolbar row - Role/Status on Staff users (side by side in one row, not stacked), Event on Role assignments, and a new Sign-in method filter (Local/SSO) on Active sessions. Each Filters button has an explanatory tooltip. The Admins/Operators toggle on Active sessions stays in the card header next to the title (it's a view switch, not a filter) - the toolbar row below it now holds only search + Filters, matching the other two tabs exactly.
- **Toolbar rows no longer shift width when a tab's content does or doesn't need a scrollbar.** The shared staff-shell scroll container now reserves scrollbar gutter space (`scrollbar-gutter: stable`) whether or not it's currently scrolling, so switching between Staff users / Role assignments / Active sessions (or any other page whose content height varies) no longer nudges the search box and Filters button a few pixels left or right.
- **Users & roles mobile cards were badly broken: a search box roughly 200px tall.** `.users-page__search`'s `flex: 1 1 220px` sets a sensible starting *width* in the desktop toolbar's row layout, but the same numeric flex-basis becomes a *height* once the toolbar switches to a column layout on narrow screens - the mobile rule only added `width: 100%` without resetting the basis for the new axis. Fixed by resetting `flex-basis` alongside the width override.
- **Every modal on the `add-attendee-modal` shell (Edit user, Invite user, Add attendee, and 7 others) no longer has its scrollbar cut across the panel's own rounded corner.** `overflow: auto` directly on a rounded, `border-radius`-clipped box doesn't reliably clip that box's own native scrollbar to the curve in Chromium/WebKit - it rendered as a hard straight edge poking past the top corner, right next to the header's kebab menu and close X. Scrolling now lives on a new inner `.add-attendee-modal__scroll` wrapper instead, with the outer panel just clipping (`overflow: hidden`) - the same split `.identity-modal__panel`/`.identity-modal__scroll` already used, now shared by both instead of solved twice. Each modal also gained the matching `useOverscrollBounceGuard` so a trackpad swipe past the panel's own top/bottom stops cleanly instead of rubber-banding into the page behind it.
- **Users & roles mobile cards decluttered.** Staff users' and Role assignments' Edit/Reset-sessions/Revoke actions moved from a full-width labelled footer into small icon buttons next to the status/role badge in the card header - the same compact pattern Active sessions' own cards already used. The two-column card detail grid (Roles/Sign-in, MFA/Last login, Device/IP address, etc.) no longer collapses to one column on mobile, so cards read as a short, dense list instead of a long scroll of single-field rows.
- **Edit user modal: role/scope changes are clearly separated from the Profile Save.** Adding, changing, or removing a role or scope always saved immediately (its own request, its own toast) - the footer's **Save** button only ever covered the Profile fields (name, email, phone) above it, but the shared label made that easy to misread as "nothing saved yet" while a role add left the footer button looking unchanged. The footer button is now labelled **Save profile**, and a short hint under the Role & access heading states that changes there save immediately.
- **Users & roles: signing in with no role assignment no longer dead-ends.** A staff account with no usable admin/operator role assignment (zero assignments, or one that doesn't resolve to anything - e.g. an org admin grant missing its scope) used to be silently redirected back to a blank sign-in form after a successful login, with no explanation and no way to reach My Account. It now lands on My Account (`/account`, which only ever required a valid session, not a role) with a clear notice that no role is assigned yet and to contact an administrator; password and two-factor settings stay usable from there.
- **Users & roles readability and cross-tab consistency.** Staff user role badges show the full role name (Superadmin/Administrator/Operator) instead of two-letter codes, with scope shown in the badge tooltip. The Disabled status badge uses a higher-contrast neutral tone so it no longer blends into the table next to Active rows. Revoking a role on the Role assignments tab now refreshes the Staff users list and any open Edit modal immediately instead of showing a stale role until reload. On the Staff users tab, the toolbar's role/status filters sit inline with search on desktop instead of wrapping onto their own row, and the pagination footer's "Page X of Y" label is vertically centered with the Previous/Next buttons. The Staff users and Role assignments rows-per-page picker offers 25/50 (matching what the API actually returns) instead of also listing 100/200, which silently returned a partial page with unreachable rows past the server's own 50-row cap.
- **Invite user modal cleanup.** Removed the disabled, unwired "Send invite email" switch. Added an "At least 12 characters" hint under the temporary password field (Invite user and Reset password), matching the shared password-policy minimum both flows actually enforce.
- **Edit user modal: five more gaps closed.** Clearing a previously-set phone number and saving no longer silently leaves the old value in place (the PATCH handler treated an explicit "clear it" `null` the same as "field not sent"). The Administrator role's organization scope picker no longer defaults to an org the target is already assigned to, which had no matching option in the (correctly filtered) dropdown. The Add/Change role button is no longer clickable before a role type is actually picked. The Role assignments tab count now shows on first load instead of staying blank until you open that tab, matching Staff users and Active sessions. The identity-provider cloud icon on a role badge now has real screen-reader text instead of relying on a `title` tooltip alone.
- **Recent logins in the Edit user modal no longer risk matching a second account.** It now filters by the target's exact user ID instead of a fuzzy email-substring search, which could previously surface another account's logins if that account's email happened to contain the target's email as a substring. Granting a different role type from the Edit user modal now closes the modal (instead of leaving it open showing the just-replaced role), since a role-type change can drop the target out of a filtered Staff users list and leave nothing for the modal to refresh from.
- **OIDC group sync retries Serializable conflicts more patiently** when two instance superadmins lose the mapped group at the same time (jittered backoff, up to 8 attempts), so concurrent logins no longer fail with a transaction write conflict after three immediate retries.
- **Staff timestamps that were bare UTC now use the right local zone where an operator just acted or where user timezone is known.** Organisation/Event mail **Send test** shows Sent/Attempted at in the browser timezone; Account → Sessions matches Users → Active sessions (UTC + viewer-local secondary line); Event Settings / Overview Created and Archived prefer the archiver/creator timezone when stored (same rule as Event archiving); Health check **Generated** is browser-local only (no UTC dual stamp). Audit and Security Time columns keep UTC on top with a second line under it: **user** icon (Audit: staff member's browser timezone when known) or **desktop** icon (Security: your browser timezone), so mobile cards are not widened by text prefixes.
- **The shared `Tooltip` component's trigger was completely unreachable by keyboard and invisible to screen readers navigating by Tab whenever its content had no focusable element of its own** — a plain icon or text hint, e.g. Identity providers' protocol icon, or the Audit log/Active sessions/Overview column-header "(i)" hints (flagged by CodeRabbit reviewing #658). `Tooltip` (`@admitto/ui`) wraps its trigger in a `<span>` with hover/focus handlers but had no `tabindex`, so Tab only ever reached it when a child happened to already be focusable on its own (a live button, a `Switch`). The wrapper now becomes a Tab stop exactly when there's content to show and nothing inside it is already focusable, re-checked on every render rather than just on mount, so callers like `ArchivedGuard` and `SessionListItem`'s revoke button — which toggle a child control's `disabled` state at runtime — correctly pick up the Tab stop the moment the control itself drops out of the tab order, without ever adding a second Tab stop on an enabled control that was already reachable.
- **The Archiving tab's `created_by`/`archived_by` backfill (above) would have left every pre-existing event's creator and archiver permanently blank on a real deployment, caught before this shipped in a release.** `backfill-event-actor-attribution.ts` — which recovers `Event.created_by_user_id`/`created_by_timezone`/`archived_by_user_id`/`archived_by_timezone` from the matching `event_created`/`event_archived` `AdminAuditLog` entry — is the last step in `packages/db`'s `db:migrate` script chain, but `deploy/docker-entrypoint.sh`'s production `migrate` container never called it, unlike the four backfills before it in that same chain (agency `public_ref`, event custom-field registry, ticket-type catalog, check-in session-id); it only ever ran in dev/CI via `npm run db:migrate`, never inside the actual production container. The entrypoint now runs it last in that same sequence, matching `db:migrate`'s script order.
- **`UsersPage` (Users & roles → Staff users) and `AttendeesPage` had the same unconditional-page-reset bug independently found and fixed in `AuditLogPanel`'s `useLogQuery` hook (#673): their own search-box debounce timers called `setPage(1)` on every tick regardless of whether the trimmed value had actually changed from what's already committed** — which is every mount, since both search boxes start (and often stay) empty. Besides a redundant refetch 300ms after every mount, an operator who paginated away from page 1 within that window could be silently snapped back to page 1 for no reason they'd understand. Both debounces now compare against a ref tracking the currently-committed search term and are a no-op when it hasn't changed, the same fix shape as `AuditLogPanel`.

- **The vertical gap between an admin page's header and its first content block (tabs, a table, or cards) was inconsistent across pages** — pages using the shared `.screen` flex wrapper (Communication, Reports, Event settings, Overview) showed noticeably more space than pages that didn't (Attendees, Requirements, Check-in, Users & roles, Settings), because a flex container's `gap` never collapses with a child's own margin the way normal block-flow margins do, so `.at-pageheader`'s own `margin-bottom` and `.screen`'s `gap` stacked instead of landing on one shared value; Reports and Attendee Detail each additionally carried their own one-off wrapper with the identical bug under a different class name. A single `.screen > .at-pageheader` override now normalizes every page to the same gap, and the two redundant one-off overrides are removed in favor of the shared rule.
- **The 6-digit MFA code entry on the login screen could overflow narrow phones (320–390px) instead of fitting the viewport.** Digit boxes, their spacing, and the card's own side padding now shrink slightly below 480px so the row always fits.
- **Bot review triage on the Branding tab PR (SonarCloud + CodeRabbit + an automated code-review comment).** A local upgrade from the previous single-file `font_family_url` format now converts that record into the new library format on its very first read instead of silently losing the font (and permanently losing the file the next time the theme is saved) - the real, verified concern behind the review's one P1 finding. A crafted `/api/staff/theme` request could smuggle CSS-breaking characters (`"`, `;`, `{`) through a "local" font path and have them land unescaped in generated `@font-face` CSS on the public ticket page; the local-path pattern now restricts the filename to a safe charset (both the admin-facing and the server-side copy of the check), and the CSS generator escapes the URL as a second, independent layer. Manrope and Space Grotesk (which ship no italic file at all) no longer show a real-italic preview with no "faking it" hint just because no *custom* family happened to be active - the built-in font's own real style list is now checked either way. A filename guessed as Thin/Extralight/Black (100/200/900) now has a matching option in the weight picker instead of silently mismatching what's shown against what's saved. Re-choosing a file for a still-uploading row no longer risks the earlier, slower upload overwriting the newer one if it happens to resolve later - each row now tracks its own upload generation. An org that already has 8 saved custom font families (the server-enforced limit) now sees a locked "Limit reached" tile instead of an upload flow that would silently vanish. A bookmark or link to Event Settings' old `?tab=branding` query now still opens the (renamed) Images tab instead of falling back to General. Also: a nested `<button>` inside another (SonarCloud-caught in an earlier pass) is confirmed still fixed; `role="menu"` removed from the font-styles popover (a static label list, not a menu); a nested ternary and a redundant credential-check helper simplified; a stale test fixture using the removed `font_family_url` field updated to the current shape; `handleDropzoneFiles`'s cognitive complexity reduced by extracting its file-partitioning and combo-dedup steps into named helpers. The image and font upload paths in `apps/web`'s branding-upload module shared a validate-then-write sequence (size check, magic-byte detection, extension lookup, UUID-named write) that only differed in the size limit, detector, and the image-only declared-Content-Type cross-check - extracted into one shared helper, the two detection functions themselves untouched. Follow-up (full SonarCloud pass, all 37 new-code findings triaged): the two font-path regexes (admin-facing and server-side) had a redundant `A-Z` alongside the existing case-insensitive flag; the new CSS-string escaper now uses `replaceAll` on plain strings instead of global regexes; three near-identical "drops a whole family" security tests collapsed into one parameterized test; `handleDropzoneFiles`'s own remaining complexity (still 2 over budget after the earlier extraction) reduced further by pulling the per-file apply-and-count loop into its own helper; a nested icon-name ternary extracted into a named function; a `BrandingUploadError` status cast repeated 3 times now uses one named type alias; 25 test assertions across two files switched from `waitFor(() => getBy…)` to the equivalent, more direct `findBy…` query. A `mailSaving` flag that was set but never read now actually gates the page's own leave-while-busy warning (navigation blocker and tab-close prompt), matching how its sibling `mailDirty` already gates the same two guards. Not fixed, and confirmed as deliberate rather than overlooked: the public ticket page still doesn't load the built-in fonts' own stylesheet at all (a separate, already-tracked gap); the modal backdrop's `role="presentation"` (Sonar's accessibility rule assumes this role is always about an `<img>`, but this is a plain decorative overlay div, the same pattern already used unflagged in `AddAttendeeModal`); a `TODO(multi-org)` marking a deliberate single-tenant simplification tracked on the v0.5+ roadmap, not a forgotten task. Follow-up (PO review, tenth pass): the remaining 3 SonarCloud findings after that pass closed out - a test that lost its own assertion in the `findBy…` conversion above (still functionally correct, since `findBy…` throws on its own, but now also has an explicit one for the linter), the CSS escaper's two search-and-replace pairs written with `String.raw` for the replacement half, and the two confirmed-false-positive/deliberate findings from the previous pass marked `// NOSONAR` with their reasoning inline instead of just left to keep reappearing in every fresh scan. Also: the duplicate-weight-and-style warning reworded to lead with "already loaded" instead of a browser-internals explanation; the "Create font family" dropzone's copy now matches the logo dropzone's own structure (a plain "Drop X here, or click to browse" line plus a supported-formats-and-size hint directly in the box, instead of bold text and a separate paragraph below); a built-in/custom font tile's own upload-button sibling ("Custom font" / "Upload files") now lines up at the exact same height as every other tile's label and hint, which needed two separate fixes: the icon's own font-size didn't match the sample text's, and (unlike every other tile's inner control) this tile's own outer `<button>` had no font/line-height reset, so its label was quietly rendering in the browser's default UI font instead of the app's. Follow-up (PO review, eleventh pass): the last SonarCloud finding (the `TODO(multi-org)` NOSONAR from the previous pass) turned out not to actually suppress anything - NOSONAR only registers as the first thing in its own comment, and appending it after an already-open `// TODO:` comment on the same line doesn't count, confirmed by re-checking the PR's own issue list rather than assuming the first attempt worked. Removing a saved custom font family now asks for confirmation first instead of deleting on the icon click alone, matching how every other destructive action in this app already works. The Edit and Remove icons on a saved family now show a distinct hover colour matching their action (blue for edit, red for remove) instead of both using the same neutral hover - the first attempt at the blue one used the same shade depth as the red one, but read as barely-there next to it, so it's one shade darker to match its actual visual weight. Follow-up (PO review, twelfth pass): SonarCloud's "0 new issues" doesn't cover code duplication, a separate metric that stayed at the same 0.9% across several pushes because the actual duplicated block was never in the file already fixed for a different reason - `apps/web`'s three upload route handlers (org logo, event logo, theme font) repeated the same parse-multipart-body-and-validate-the-file block and the same try/catch/audit/respond tail almost verbatim; both are now shared helpers, each handler keeping only its own access check and its own upload call plus audit fields. Follow-up (PO review, thirteenth pass): two more automated-review findings closed out - the per-family variant cap is raised from 12 to 18 to match what the "Create font family" editor's own nine weights (100-900) times two styles actually let someone add, since anything saved past the old, lower cap was silently dropped (its already-uploaded file left orphaned on disk) instead of rejected or accepted; and a custom family can no longer be saved under the same name as a built-in font (Manrope, Space Grotesk, IBM Plex Sans) - both picks wrote the same `font_family_name`, so the built-in became permanently unreachable once its name was reused for a custom family, now caught both in the modal itself (an inline error the moment the name is typed) and, independently, wherever the theme is saved. Follow-up (PO review, fourteenth pass): Codecov's patch-coverage gate raised from 84% by adding tests for real, previously-untested branches across the whole PR rather than only the latest changes - the styles popover's open/close-on-outside-click behaviour; a blocked Save when an already-loaded theme carries an invalid saved custom font family; a partial-save-failure toast when only the organisation half of a save fails; genuine HTML5 drag-and-drop on the font-upload dropzone (previously only the file-picker path was ever exercised); replacing an existing row's file (both a failed upload and a rejected extension); a still-loading upload correctly discarded when a newer file is picked for the same row before the first one finishes; the "all combinations already added" toast; the per-row Normal/Italic toggle; malformed (non-object) entries and non-string names within `custom_font_families` and its per-family variants now dropped instead of left to crash something downstream; a variant URL with only a password and no username also counts as credentialed; an unrecognized font extension omits the `format()` hint instead of guessing; every image (JPEG, WEBP) and font (WOFF, OTF, TTF) magic-byte signature the upload validator recognizes; an oversized or Content-Type-mismatched upload at both the application layer and (for fonts) the raw HTTP body-size middleware; a multipart body missing its file field or too malformed to parse at all; and the thin client wrapper around the font-upload endpoint itself, which every existing admin test had only ever exercised through a mock. Deliberately left uncovered: the generic catch-all 500 response for a non-validation error inside the upload route handlers, which would need a forced filesystem/database failure to reach safely and isn't worth an environment-fragile test for. Follow-up (PO review, fifteenth pass): that last call turned out wrong - setting `INSTANCE_ORG_ID` to an id with no matching organization forces the exact same real, non-validation error deterministically, so the 500 fallback is now covered on the upload route instead of skipped. Also closed: the styles popover staying open when clicking one of its own listed styles rather than treating it as an outside click; a load that resolves or rejects after the component has already unmounted; the plural "N variants" toast wording (only the singular case had a test); removing a saved-but-inactive custom family without disturbing the active pick; a missing organisation name or logo URL from the server handled as empty rather than crashing; the Font section's own description naming an active *custom* family instead of only ever a built-in one; an orphaned `font_family_name` (matching neither a built-in nor any saved family) defaulting to "assume real" rather than always flagging a fake bold/italic; an invalid loaded primary colour or `font_family_name` blocking Save the same way an invalid custom font family already did; the "both saves rejected" and "theme save rejected, org save succeeded" partial-failure toasts (only the third of four outcomes had a test before); a font weight the nine-preset picker doesn't cover falling back to its raw number instead of guessing wrong; a still-loading preview face correctly discarded once its own row has already been replaced or the modal itself has unmounted mid-load; an empty, never-uploaded row removed cleanly; plural wording for multiple files skipped or multiple files replaced at once; a filename that guesses only a weight/style keyword (no real family name left over) leaving the name field blank instead of something meaningless; every image and font magic-byte signature the upload validator recognizes (JPEG, WEBP, WOFF, OTF, TTF - WOFF2 and PNG were already covered); an upload with no declared Content-Type at all skipping the cross-check instead of tripping it; the uploads directory's own fallback to `./uploads` when `UPLOAD_DIR` is unset; a font URL with no file extension at all (not just an unrecognized one) also omitting the `format()` hint; and `file_required` now tested on all three upload routes instead of only one. A handful of branches stayed deliberately uncovered because they're provably unreachable, not just hard to trigger: several `?? fallback` / `?:` ternary branches whose "invalid" side can never actually occur given how the surrounding state is produced, and the event-branding-upload route's own internal eventId/access checks, which duplicate `withEventArchiveGuard`'s identical checks one layer up in `app.ts` and can never see a failing case by the time they run.

- **Instance Settings — Identity: Add/Edit provider and Cloudflare Access now open as modals over the list instead of navigating to a separate page (closes #415, #417, #418).** The list stays visible and scrolled where it was; closing (Cancel, Escape, or a successful save) refreshes it in place instead of a full page reload. Clicking the backdrop deliberately does not close the modal — a superadmin mid-edit shouldn't lose work to a stray click outside the panel; Cancel/Escape (both already gated by the dirty-changes confirm) are the only ways out. All existing behaviour is preserved unchanged in the new modal shell: the discard-unsaved-changes guard (including leaving via a different Settings tab), Discover/Test connection, and env-locked fields. Deep links to a specific provider (`/admin/settings/identity/providers/<id>`) or to Cloudflare Access still open the right modal directly. Layout polish along the way: the Cloudflare Access card on the Identity overview now carries its "Manage" action in the card header, matching "Add provider" above it, instead of reading like an afterthought below it; the provider editor's SSO login button field is now a plain single input — the live preview of the rendered button was removed (it never reflected the real endpoint state, just the label text, so it wasn't a trustworthy preview); the Cloudflare Access editor's Team URL and Application token (AUD) fields now share a row like every other settings panel — this also fixed a real dead-CSS bug where the intended full-width override never matched (`Input` renders its field inside a wrapper div, so the old `> .at-input` selector never applied), leaving Protected URL paths rendering at half width with an empty gap beside it.
- **Borders silently didn't render in several places (Attendees timeline rows, the staff shell's demo bar/sessions table/overview cards, Reports' admission log) because `--border-subtle` and `--border-default` were referenced throughout the admin CSS but never defined as tokens** (closes #437) — an undefined custom property inside a `border` shorthand invalidates the whole declaration per the CSS spec, so those hairline dividers just silently weren't there. All 15 occurrences across `staff.css`, `attendees.css`, and `reports-page.css` now use the existing `--border` token instead, the same resolution already used for this exact bug class in #357/#286.
- **Documented that logout revokes the "remember this device" trusted-device cookie** (closes #343). `docs/security/SECURITY-CONTROLS.md` now states this explicitly, with a note for shared operator check-in tablets to sign out before handing the device to the next person. No behavior change — PR #340 already did this; it just wasn't written down anywhere outside the code and tests.
- **Follow-up review of the same PR turned up several real regressions from converting `IdentityProviderEditor`/`CfAccessEditor` into portal-rendered modals, bundled in alongside the loading-delay work.** Clicking the modal's backdrop did nothing (both now close via `onClick={handleCancel}`, matching every other modal in the app). Initial keyboard/screen-reader focus never entered either modal when opened in edit mode or as Cloudflare Access - both always start in a `loading` state with no focusable content yet, and the shared `useModalFocusTrap`'s one-shot mount effect never retried once the real form appeared; the hook now accepts an optional `focusWhenReady` value (the view/loadState the caller's content depends on) so it re-attempts focus once that changes, with no change for its dozen other callers, which don't need it. The dialog's title and close button - and the `aria-labelledby` target the whole dialog depends on for its accessible name - were also only rendered once fully loaded, leaving both modals unnamed and uncloseable-by-mouse during their loading/error views; the header now always renders, with only its subtitle/status badge staying conditional. Separately, the SSO login button's live preview (an input hint box showing what the button will actually say) was silently deleted during the same conversion, though the changelog entry above claimed it was merely "repositioned" — restored, and actually placed in one row with its label field this time (`identity-editor__grid`'s existing 2-column layout), fulfilling what that entry described. `IdentityProviderEditor` and `CfAccessEditor` also lost their own route-level code-splitting once folded into one shared `IdentityProvidersPanel` (both were eagerly bundled into it); each is `lazy()`-loaded again now, confirmed as separate chunks in the production build. `IdentityMappingRepeater`'s row layout (moved from CSS grid to flex earlier in the same PR) had no responsive behavior left for narrow viewports, unlike the grid version it replaced — rows now wrap onto multiple lines below 860px instead of overflowing the modal panel; a dead CSS rule from the old grid layout was removed alongside it. `AuditLogPanel`'s viewer-timezone lookup was cached once at module load, so a Local-mode timestamp label went stale if the browser's timezone changed mid-session (e.g. resuming a laptop while traveling) without a reload — now recomputed on each use. `RequirementsPage`'s `EventBehaviourContent` was still fed the delayed flag under the `loading` prop name (the one remaining instance of the exact conflation bug fixed everywhere else above); wired correctly now, ahead of any future edit that would have reintroduced the flash. And the widespread copy-pasted "only render this once the delay has elapsed" one-liner (~6 near-identical local functions across `AttendeesTable`, `AttendeeDetailPage`, `CommunicationPage`, `EventMailSettingsCard`, `TicketTypesCard`) is now one shared `whenShown(show, content)` helper exported alongside `useDelayedLoading`. **Second round of bot review** on the same PR found three more: the identity editors' Cancel button was disabled while a save request was pending, but the new backdrop, close icon, and Escape all still called the same dismissal handler — confirming discard mid-save unmounted the modal while an uncancellable `create`/`updateIdentityProvider` (or `updateCfAccess`, particularly risky for enabling Cloudflare Access) kept running, silently applying the change the operator thought they'd discarded; the handler itself now no-ops while busy, so every dismissal path is consistently blocked, matching each file's own existing "busy" definition. `AuditLogPanel`'s From/To date filters always computed their UTC bounds regardless of the Local/UTC toggle (and didn't reload when the toggle changed) — a "Local" day picked in a non-UTC zone silently filtered a different UTC-day window than what the timestamps actually displayed; a new zone-aware `zonedDayStartIso`/`zonedDayEndIso` (a "double conversion" against `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, DST included) now bounds the filter in whichever zone is selected, and the load callback reloads when the toggle changes. And the audit log's Details popover sat inside `.sessions-table-wrap`'s own `overflow-x: auto` (which per the CSS spec also makes the wrapper vertically clippable) — a row near the bottom of the table opened its popover mostly or entirely outside the visible area; it's now portal-rendered into `document.body` with its position measured from the trigger at open-time and flipped upward when there isn't room below, the same viewport-collision approach `@admitto/ui`'s `Tooltip` already uses. SonarCloud cleanup on the same round: the new backdrop/close/Escape regression test's final assertion swapped from `waitFor` + `getByText` to `findByText` - no behavior change. **Third round of bot review** found two more: `zonedDayStartIso`/`zonedDayEndIso`'s "double conversion" is a single correction pass, which is only exact when the zone's UTC offset doesn't change between the naive guess and the corrected instant - false right across a DST transition, most visibly one that falls at local midnight itself (Africa/Cairo's 2023 transition jumped straight from 00:00 EET to 01:00 EEST, so `zonedDayStartIso("2023-04-28", "Africa/Cairo")` returned 2023-04-27T21:00Z - 23:00 the *previous* day locally - instead of 2023-04-27T22:00Z, the first instant that actually reads as April 28; a wall-clock time close to but not inside the gap could land an hour off the same way). The correction now re-validates the corrected instant and corrects again (converges within a couple of passes for a real offset change), and when the requested wall clock was skipped over entirely by the transition, resolves to the earliest instant that reads back on or after it instead of oscillating between the instant just before and just after the gap. Separately, `ImportPage`'s history card fed `useDelayedLoading` a derived `history === null` check instead of an explicit in-flight flag - that stays `true` continuously through a failed fetch and any following Retry (history is never set on failure), so the hook's one-time rising edge had already fired before the retry and its no-flash delay never got a fresh start, showing "Loading…" immediately instead of waiting; now driven by its own `historyLoading` state, toggled around each fetch attempt like every other panel already does. SonarCloud cleanup on the same round: the DST-gap fix's fallback lookup swapped `arr[arr.length - 1]` for `arr.at(-1)` - no behavior change.

- **Instance Settings → Logs & audit: Security audit log folded into the same System/Audit toggle as a third "Security" option**, replacing the separate `SecurityAuditLogPanel` card that used to sit stacked below the Audit log — two independently loading/erroring cards on one tab read as confusing overlap, not three deliberately distinct things. The backend is unchanged (still two separate tables, endpoints, and retention policies — only the UI is unified); the Security view reuses the same toolbar/filters/search/pagination/mobile-card conventions already established for Audit, as a parallel (not shared) set of components, since the two rows' shapes differ enough (no Scope column, no actor-timezone, no CSV export on Security) that a shared abstraction would cost more than it saves. Found and fixed along the way: `isInitialLoad` (deciding whether either view shows its loading skeleton) was computed as `loading && entries.length === 0`, which is true again on every later refetch that starts from — or resolves to — zero rows (an empty search, an event-type filter with no matches), not just the genuine first load; a filter change starting from an empty result used to swap the empty-state text out for the skeleton, then back, reading as a flicker. Both views now track a `hasLoadedOnce` flag (matching the existing convention already used by `AttendeesPage`) so the skeleton only ever renders once, on mount — every later reload, empty or not, updates in place with no flash. Follow-up (PO testing on the port-3003 mockup): the Security view's desktop header and both views' mobile cards now label the Time column "(UTC)" explicitly, matching the Audit log's own already-labeled column — a live demo made clear the timezone wasn't obvious without it. Both views also gained the same **Live/Paused** toggle, amber degraded-connection banner, and **Retry now** action already established by the System-logs panel, reusing its exact `POLL_INTERVAL_MS`/`POLL_DEGRADED_THRESHOLD` constants; unlike System's in-memory tail, Audit/Security are paginated DB snapshots, so "live" here silently re-runs the current page/filtered query every ~1.75s and replaces the rows in place, rather than appending. The poll never touches the table's own loading/dimming state (only `entries`/`total` update), so it can't reintroduce the flicker this same bullet's `hasLoadedOnce` fix removed above, and a page-count shrinking mid-poll no longer yanks the operator to a different page the way a user-initiated reload does. Follow-up (PO review, batch 08/09): the Settings tab itself is renamed **"Logs"** (was "Logs & audit" — redundant now that Audit is just one of the three views inside it, not the whole tab's identity), and the two other card titles drop the same redundancy for grammatical consistency with "System logs": "Audit log" → **"Audit logs"**, "Security audit log" → **"Security logs"**. Security gains its own **Export logs** button (a `GET /api/admin/security-audit-log/export` route, superadmin-gated, `EXPORT_ROW_CAP`-capped, self-audited into `AdminAuditLog` under a new `security_audit_log_exported` action type and into the System-logs Admin source, mirroring the Audit log's own CSV export exactly minus its Scope column — Security rows aren't event-scoped, same reasoning already given above for why Security has no Scope/actor-timezone columns) — previously a deliberate omission ("a shared abstraction would cost more than it saves") now reversed at explicit request now that the three views are directly compared side by side. System's **"Download .log"** and Audit's **"Export CSV"** are both renamed to the same **"Export logs"** label so the identical action reads identically in all three views. Finally, button order is standardized across all three (both the Card header on desktop and each view's own mobile toolbar): **Clear filters** (where the view has one) always leads, then **Export logs**, then **Live** last, immediately beside the System/Audit/Security selector it pauses/resumes relative to — previously each view ordered these differently, so the same two buttons common to every view jumped position when switching tabs. Follow-up (PO testing round 3): Audit's desktop Time column was missing the inline "UTC" label its own mobile cards and the header's info-icon tooltip already implied — now reads e.g. "2026-07-06 12:00:00 UTC" like mobile, instead of relying solely on a hover tooltip. Security's User column now shows a full-email second line under a known user's display name, matching Audit's own Actor column (e.g. "Alice Admin" / "alice@example.com"); a failed-login row that resolves to "Unknown" (no `user_id`) now surfaces its already-redacted email from metadata the same way, instead of showing nothing. The mobile footer's "Rows per page" selector moved out of the Previous/Page/Next pager group and in with the "Showing X of Y" summary text — below ~600px this used to wrap the pager's 4 items across an uneven 3-then-1 line break; each row now holds a tidy 2 items instead. Also fixed, found via DevTools while testing this same page: the Sessions panel's "Bulk revoke operator sessions" event `<select>` had neither an `id` nor a `name` attribute (Chrome flagged it as a form-autofill accessibility issue) — added both, plus an `aria-label`, matching every other `at-select` in the app. Follow-up (PO testing round 4): Security's Time column second line was still missing entirely (it only ever showed the UTC instant) — it now shows a second line too, like Audit's, but with an important difference: Audit's second line is the *actor's* own local time (from their stored `actor_timezone`), while Security's is whoever is *currently viewing the log's* own local time — a security event (e.g. a failed login from an unauthenticated request) doesn't always have a known actor to show a time for, but the superadmin reading the table always has one. The "Time" header on both views already carried an info-icon tooltip explaining the two-line format; Security's now spells out this actor-vs-viewer distinction explicitly, since it's the one place the convention quietly changes. Eleventh pass (SonarCloud Quality Gate, PR #614 — 17% duplication on new code, required ≤3%): the parallel-not-shared Audit/Security components above (AuditLogTable/SecurityAuditTable, their Cards and ListContent siblings, AuditLogView/SecurityAuditView, and useSecurityAuditLog/AuditLogPanel's own inlined equivalent) are now genuinely shared — a column-configured `LogTable`/`LogCards`, a `LogListContent` wrapper, one `LogView` toolbar+footer shell, and a generic `useLogQuery` hook, with only each view's own column list, empty-state copy, and filter-dropdown slot staying view-specific. Net -192 lines in `AuditLogPanel.tsx`. This reverses the "a shared abstraction would cost more than it saves" framing from earlier in this same bullet — that judgment call was reasonable pre-Sonar-gate, but duplicating this much view/data-fetching logic twice was never actually free, and the Quality Gate made the true cost visible. All 1794 existing admin tests pass unchanged (same DOM output, same ids/aria-labels/classes), confirming this is a pure internal refactor with no behavior change. Twelfth pass (PO request — "prefer 0% duplication"): two more rounds followed. First, the two remaining SonarCloud cognitive-complexity findings (AuditLogPanel itself, and useLogQuery's load()) plus a nested-ternary Card-title finding — fixed by extracting LogsCardActions/LogsPanelViews (the Card header's view-dependent actions and the three always-mounted views), a logsViewTitle() lookup, a countTruthy() helper for the active-filter-count badges, named hasActiveAuditFilters/hasActiveSecurityFilters functions, and a single generic LogFilterField (used by Audit's Action/Event selects and Security's Event-type select — the same labeled-select-with-an-"All ..."-option shape, three times) instead of three near-identical inline selects. Second, SonarCloud's remaining 1.3% duplication turned out to be a completely different pair of files, not this admin-side work: `audit-routes.ts` and `security-audit-routes.ts`'s own CSV-export handlers shared an identical self-audit-write + emitSystemLog + BOM/CSV-response tail (differing only in action type and filename) — extracted into two new `admin-helpers.ts` functions, `selfAuditCsvExport()` and `csvExportResponse()`, used by both routes. All 1794 admin and 1393 web tests still pass unchanged throughout. Thirteenth pass (CodeRabbit automated review on PR #614, 2 actionable + several nitpick findings): fixed a real Major-severity race — a silent live-poll tick could abort a still-in-flight non-silent load (mount, filter/page change, Retry), which skipped that load's own `finally` and left `loading` stuck `true` forever (the silent tick's own `finally` also never clears it, by design) — added a `nonSilentLoadInFlightRef` guard to `useLogQuery` so a silent tick simply skips its turn instead of aborting a real load in progress. Also fixed: Security's row-copy summary didn't filter `METADATA_KEYS_SHOWN_ELSEWHERE` the way Audit's own copy summary and the Details popover already do, so a copied Security row could repeat `email_redacted` verbatim; `handleCopyRow`/`handleCopySecurityRow` were identical modulo the summary-builder call, now share one `copyRowToClipboard` helper; and `.audit-log-poll-warning`'s text color now uses the semantic `--text-muted` token instead of a raw palette value, matching `.system-log-panel__notice`'s own convention. One suggested fix was tried and reverted after it broke 4 real tests: CodeRabbit's "stale closure" concern about `load`'s `[...Object.values(filters)]` dependency (unusual for exhaustive-deps) looked reasonable, but switching to a plain `filters` object dependency reintroduced a bug the spread was written to avoid — the search-debounce effect's `setFilters((f) => ({ ...f, search: ... }))` always produces a new object reference even when the trimmed value is unchanged (e.g. on mount), so depending on `filters` itself recreates `load` on a change that isn't a real one, which cascades into an extra unwanted reload (via the mount effect) and silently resets the poll-failure counter (via the poll effect) - confirmed by bisecting a real test failure, not just by inspection. Reverted to the `Object.values` form with a fuller comment on why. Left for the PO to decide, not actioned here (pre-existing and/or a deliberate trade-off, not a regression from this batch): pausing Audit/Security/System's own live-polling while their view is hidden (all three currently poll continuously regardless of which tab is active - a documented, deliberate choice to avoid a remount flash when switching tabs back); a rate limit on the two audit-log CSV export routes (mirrors the pre-existing, already-unlimited `audit-log/export` route, not a new gap); and hoisting `viewerLocalTime`'s per-call `Intl.DateTimeFormat` construction into a module-level cache (real but minor perf win, skipped given the risk of interacting badly with the test suite's own `Intl.DateTimeFormat.prototype.resolvedOptions` mock across cases sharing one module instance). All 1794 admin tests still pass.
- **OIDC/SSO login failed closed (redirecting to `/login?error=oidc_failed`, no session created) on any instance where the Postgres server's session `TimeZone` is ahead of UTC (e.g. Europe/Warsaw)** — the common case for a self-hosted database using its OS's local timezone rather than an explicit UTC setting. On a session timezone *behind* UTC instead (e.g. America/New_York), the same bug ran the other way and didn't fail login at all: a single-use OIDC state/nonce/PKCE-verifier row silently stayed valid and replayable for hours past its intended 10-minute TTL — a security-relevant lifetime extension with no visible symptom, easy to mistake for "this deployment isn't affected." `consumeOidcAuthState`'s single-use state/nonce lookup compared the naive-UTC `expires_at`/`consumed_at` columns against raw SQL `NOW()` (a `timestamptz`); Postgres implicitly casts that comparison using the session's `TimeZone`, shifting it by the zone's UTC offset in whichever direction that zone points — earlier for positive offsets (the login failure), later for negative offsets (the extended token lifetime). Fixed by comparing against `(NOW() AT TIME ZONE 'utc')` instead, which stays timezone-independent regardless of the database server's local clock configuration, closing both cases.
- **Health check version/commit matches the sidebar.** Overview and Markdown export use the running staff SPA build (`__APP_VERSION__` / `__APP_COMMIT__`); the API prefers `apps/admin/dist/build-meta.json` written at Vite build time over live `git HEAD`, so a stale lab SPA no longer disagrees with Health after a git pull.
- **Attendee PDF export truncates long emails with an ellipsis** instead of wrapping mid-word (e.g. splitting a domain across two lines) and overlapping the next row. pdfkit only applies `ellipsis` when a cell `height` is set; Email/Name stay wide for paper checklists, and Admitted at stays wide enough for `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm`.
- **Settings' tab strip could land with the active tab partly hidden under its own scroll-fade arrow**, most visible deep-linking straight to a tab near the end (e.g. `?tab=archiving`) on a narrow viewport — `scrollIntoView`'s "nearest" (`ScrollFadeTabs`) only checks a tab's geometry against the scroll container's raw width, with no way to know a 28px-wide arrow button sits absolutely on top of that container's edge. Tabs now carry a matching `scroll-margin-inline: 28px`, the signal `scrollIntoView` itself reads for "treat this much extra space as required," so it keeps scrolling until the tab actually clears the arrow. Organisation settings' "Documentation" button also now stays beside the title on mobile instead of stacking below it full-width, shrunk to match Reports'/Event settings' own mobile header button verbatim (icon hidden, compact height) — it had been left on the shared `PageHeader`'s generic stacked-below layout instead of the scoped override its siblings already use.
- **Attendee Detail still didn't match the rest of the app's header-to-content spacing after #629.** `.attendee-status-strip` (the Pass/Attendance/Ticket delivery/Check-in/Wallet row right under the header) and the Overview tab's `.attendee-detail-grid` (Profile/Event items) each carried their own `margin-top: 16px` on top of the shared `.screen` wrapper's `gap` — the identical margin-plus-gap stacking bug #629 fixed at the page header, just one boundary deeper on this one page. Both (and the loading skeleton's matching `margin-top`) are removed in favor of the shared gap alone, so the header-to-strip and tabs-to-content gaps now match every other page too.
- **Overview page's Check-in progress card "Last check-in" and "Busiest hour" kept showing a stale value after every check-in for an event was revoked.** Both were sourced from the append-only `CheckIn` scan log filtered to `status = 'VALID'`, but revoking or undoing an admission never deletes or updates that row — it only clears the attendee's own `admitted_at` and appends a separate `UNDO` row — so a since-revoked attendee's original scan kept being counted indefinitely. Both now source from `Attendee.admitted_at` instead (cleared on undo/revoke), matching the fix already applied to the Reports page's own peak-hour query, so revoking every check-in now correctly resets both to "none" instead of keeping their last value.
- **The Check-in status chip on the Attendees list and Attendee Detail page hid the check-in time on narrower screens, showing only the date.** The admitted-at value is now two explicit parts (day, time) instead of one string truncated to fit — on wide desktop they still share one line ("Today, 14:51 CEST"), but below 900px they stack onto their own lines so both stay visible instead of the time being clipped off. The chip itself was also occasionally rendering a third stacked line and growing visibly taller/wider than its sibling chips; fixed by giving its value a real max-width to truncate against instead of letting it grow past the chip's own edge.
- **The Pass/Attendance/Ticket delivery status badges on the Attendee Detail page could stretch into an oversized bar instead of staying a compact pill**, when their container's flex alignment gave them the full row width to grow into. Scoped the fix to just the plain-text chip values, so the badge pills themselves are unaffected.
- **The Ticket Types settings row (color swatch, name, preview badge, attendee count, delete) no longer wraps its individual controls apart from each other on narrow screens.** The name field with its swatch ("identity") and the read-only badge/count/delete ("meta") now each wrap as one group instead of every control wrapping independently.
- **The Attendees table was cramped between roughly 1024–1279px viewport widths** — not enough room for its 7 columns once the sidebar's own breakpoint widened past the icon-only rail at that width. The rail now spans the same range the table needs it to, closing the gap. The table's footer also no longer crowds its "Rows per page" picker against the Previous/Page/Next pager — the row-count summary and page-size picker now group and wrap independently from the pager, matching the Audit log footer's own fix for the identical problem.
- **Attendees list crashed with "PrismaClient is unable to run in this browser environment" on load.** Two files in `@admitto/tickets` (`attendee-card.ts`, `attendees-list-filters.ts`) imported status constants from `@admitto/db`'s main entry point instead of its `./status` subpath; the main entry point also constructs a Prisma client at module load, so any bundle pulling in those constants (including the admin SPA) shipped that construction — and Prisma's own browser guard — straight into the browser. Both now import from `@admitto/db/status`, matching every other call site already doing so correctly.
- **Attendees list — the bulk "Send tickets" action didn't guard against completing after the operator navigated to a different event while the request was still in flight, unlike its six sibling bulk actions.** No user-facing change for the normal case — send/check-in/revoke-check-in/change-ticket-type/delete/revoke-items/revoke-pass now share one `runBulkAction` implementation (internal cleanup, SonarCloud duplication flag), which incidentally closed this one latent gap as a side effect of unifying the shared skeleton. Bot review: unifying the skeleton had also propagated the opposite bug to all seven actions — the busy/spinner state stayed stuck on indefinitely (until a full reload) once the operator navigated away mid-request, since it was gated behind the same "still on this event" check as the toast/dialog-close side effects, but busy is this component's own local UI state, not tied to which event initiated the request. Busy now always clears once the request settles, regardless of navigation. Also extracted the catch block's error-resolution branching (401 redirect, message selection, toast-vs-inline) into its own helper to bring `runBulkAction` back under SonarCloud's cognitive-complexity threshold.
- **Custom attendee fields could display in a different order than they were created in — inconsistently, across the check-in operator card, attendee edit/create form, and CSV/XLSX import/export columns.** Root cause: fields created together in one request (e.g. via `createMany`) share an identical `created_at` timestamp, and the registry query ordered only by that column — leaving ties to whatever scan Postgres happened to pick, which could vary run to run. Now orders by `(created_at, id)`, using the field's row id as a stable tiebreaker so same-timestamp fields always resolve back to their original creation order. Bot review follow-up: the `GET /api/admin/events/:eventId/custom-fields` route — which feeds the attendee create/edit forms and the import page's accepted-columns table — read the registry with its own separate query that still ordered by `created_at` alone, missing the same tiebreaker; now matches.
- **Attendees list — action icons and pagination footer weren't vertically centered (closes #352).** Root cause: the actions `<td>` had `display: flex` set directly on the table cell itself, which overrides its default `display: table-cell` and pulls it out of the table's row-height algorithm — so that cell's height was governed only by its own icon content (49px) while every other cell in the same row (including Check-in) sized to the row's actual tallest content (62px, once the new Pass status column's badge pills are in the mix), leaving the action icons visibly off-center against their own row. Fixed by moving the flex layout to an inner `<div>` and leaving the `<td>` itself as a normal table cell. Also: row-level View/Restore/Revoke icon buttons now use the compact `size="sm"` variant, the pagination footer aligns "Page X of Y" with the Previous/Next buttons on one baseline, and the `ArchivedGuard` tooltip `<span>` wrapping Restore/Revoke is now explicit `inline-flex` so it sizes identically to the plain View icon next to it.
- **Attendees list — a revoked/cancelled pass showed a conflicting "Registered" RSVP badge next to a red "Revoked" pill in the same cell (closes #366).** RSVP (attendance) and pass status are different fields (`rsvp_status` vs `status`) that used to share one "Status" column; they're now two separate columns — "RSVP" and "Pass status" — so a revoked or cancelled pass no longer reads as a contradiction next to an unrelated RSVP badge. The RSVP badge also dropped its leading status dot (Pass status already reads as a plain badge; the dot was inconsistent between the two new columns), and Pass status's neutral "Registered" badge dropped a stray `outline` that made it render as a hollow pill while every other neutral badge on the page (RSVP's own "Registered", Pass status's "Cancelled") is filled.
- **Attendee detail — the Check-in field used a different, always-full date/time formatter than everywhere else check-in times are shown (closes part of #446).** Now uses the same `formatAdmissionDisplay` helper as the Attendees list and Event Overview: time-only when the admission falls on the event's calendar day, date + time otherwise.
- **Attendee detail Overview — the sidebar "Attendee status" card mislabeled a lone RSVP dropdown, and the Profile card's "Token" row served no real purpose (closes #362, #363).** The status card is now a single-column list of icon-led rows (RSVP — still editable, Ticket delivery, Wallet pass, Check-in) instead of a cramped 2×2 grid with a misleading title. Dropped the Token row entirely: `ticket_ref` was already truncated server-side for any non-trivial token (`buildTicketRefPreview`/`truncateTicketRef` in `attendees-api-routes.ts`, predates this batch, added specifically so list/export/detail responses never carry the full decrypted token), so it could only ever show a partial preview — with no copy destination that could use it (manual check-in entry needs the full 40-60 char token, which no admin surface has ever exposed; `token_enc` is only ever decrypted server-side at mail-send time) and no signal it added beyond what "Ticket delivery" in the Status card already shows.
- **Deep-review pass on the Danger zone / image asset library batch above, before release:** "Revoke all items issued" could silently reset a hand-out for an attendee whose pass had since been blocked (revoked/cancelled) — something the single-item "Revoke item" action already refused to do; both bulk actions now enforce the same guard, and a blocked-pass attendee revoking their own check-in individually is correctly rejected (409) instead of erroring. "Revoke all check-ins" could abort partway through a large event with no audit-log entry if it hit that same race; it now skips the affected attendee and finishes the batch, and both bulk actions process attendees in small parallel batches instead of one at a time so they don't stall on a large event. The "Revoke all items issued" success count could occasionally overstate how many items actually changed; it now reports the real count. The image asset library's upload/delete endpoints are now correctly superadmin-only, matching the rest of the Danger zone (they briefly allowed any event admin). Communication editor: a placeholder chip clicked between two existing blocks of email content (not just at the very start) could still silently vanish instead of being inserted — now handled the same way as the start-of-document case. Also fixed a keyboard-focus outline that the new countdown bar was clipping on every confirmation dialog in the app, and corrected the image-delete confirmation's wording to match what actually happens. PO review: confirmed dropping the per-event "header image" upload was the right call (organisation branding only ever manages a logo, so its fallback was never reachable) — the Communication editor no longer offers `{{header_image_url}}` as an insertable chip either, since it can now never be set anywhere.
- **Second bot-review pass, same batch:** the Branding tab's image asset panel now correctly shows a "Superadmin only" notice for a non-superadmin event admin instead of a card that fails to load and a form that would 403 on submit (the underlying routes were tightened to superadmin-only in the fix above, but the panel itself wasn't gated to match). Communication editor: clicking an image placeholder chip while the cursor sat inside an existing text paragraph produced invalid, uncompilable markup (`<mj-image>` isn't a valid child of `<mj-text>`) instead of the promised ready-to-use image — now redirected the same way as the other insertion edge cases. A concurrent-upload race could let two simultaneous requests both squeeze past the per-event image asset cap; the limit is now rechecked inside the same transaction as the insert. Event Settings' live Danger Zone counts now have a supporting database index, avoiding a full-table scan on every settings page load as the event grows. "Revoke all items issued"'s live count could show and enable the action for items that only belong to blocked-pass (revoked/cancelled) attendees — which the guard fix above means the action can never actually revoke — leaving the count stuck nonzero after every run; the count now excludes them, matching what the action actually revokes.
- **PO follow-up, same batch:** the Danger Zone's "Revoke all check-ins" / "Revoke all items issued" / Archive / Unarchive confirmations now warn inline when the page has unsaved edits elsewhere that would be silently lost (the underlying behavior — these actions reload the page's live counts on success — is intentionally unchanged, matching the pre-existing Archive/Unarchive pattern; it's now surfaced instead of silent). Also unified Danger Zone's tooltips: Export personal data, Revoke all Wallet passes, Archive event, and Delete event now use the same styled hover tooltip as the rest of the Danger Zone and Requirements, instead of the browser's plain unstyled one.
- **A load test showed bulk revoke scales linearly but can genuinely take several seconds on a large event** (~6s at 5,000 admitted attendees, ~13s at 10,000) — the confirm button's "Working…" label had no motion during that wait, which could look frozen. Every confirmation dialog's confirm button (Delete event, Archive/Unarchive, both revoke actions) now shows a spinning icon alongside the label while working.
- **Post-merge hardening on the image asset library above:** the per-event upload cap's in-transaction recheck (added in the second bot-review pass) was not itself race-safe under two genuinely concurrent uploads — Postgres's default transaction isolation lets both transactions read the pre-write count before either commits, so the cap could still be exceeded by a couple of assets under simultaneous uploads to the same event. Uploads for one event are now serialized with a database-level lock for the duration of the create transaction, so the recheck is always accurate. The same lock closes a second, independent race: deleting an asset and saving an event email template that newly references it could interleave so that the template ends up pointing at a token that no longer exists (silently vanishing from attendee emails) — delete and template-save for one event now can't run concurrently either.
- **Check-in — desktop: "Manual lookup is disabled for this event" showed as a persistent inline banner instead of the toast used everywhere else for a blocked search:** it now fires the same warning toast as "no attendees matched that search" for both the ways this message can appear (the client-side setting check, and the server rejecting a stale-cached request), matching the app's toast-vs-inline standard (`AGENTS.md`); the mobile check-in overlay's own in-context version is unchanged.
- **Check-in — connection-loss UI showed up to three overlapping "you're offline" indicators at once, and a stale "Request failed" banner could survive after the connection had already recovered (#458):** the app-wide connection banner, a second inline paragraph driven by the exact same signal, and the live-updates (SSE) reconnecting/access-error messages could all render stacked on top of each other during a backend restart. The duplicate paragraph is gone — the connection banner already covers it — and the SSE-specific messages now only show when the heartbeat is otherwise healthy, so they add information instead of repeating it. A "Request failed. Try again." banner caused by the outage (e.g. from clicking a Recent scans row while disconnected, on desktop or in the mobile overlay's manual search) now clears itself once the connection recovers, instead of only clearing on the next explicit retry — a genuine, unrelated business error caught while still connected is left alone, so an unrelated later reconnect can't silently wipe a real failure the operator hasn't seen yet. A stale banner also now clears the moment a different lookup succeeds, not only on reconnect or a retry of the exact same action.
- **Ticket types batch, review follow-up (#351):** every screen that reads the per-event ticket type catalog (Event Settings' own tab, the attendee edit form, the Communication bulk-send filter, and the attendees list "Type" filter) now shows a clear inline error with a Retry action if that fetch fails, instead of silently leaving the picker empty with no indication anything went wrong. Check-in stays deliberately silent on the same failure (badges already fail open to a plain gray label) so a transient catalog hiccup can't interrupt an operator mid-scan. Also fixed: a public ticket page rendered a ticket type's raw catalog key (e.g. `press_pass`) instead of its label; check-in operators without admin-panel access got a 403 fetching the catalog, leaving every badge stuck on the raw key; an empty-string edit to an attendee's ticket type bypassed catalog validation and was saved as `""` instead of clearing the field; a failed ticket-type rename left the unsaved edit showing with no indication it didn't save, and a failed delete closed the confirmation dialog instead of letting the operator retry. The add-attendee form's own ticket-type dropdown now shows the same inline error on a failed fetch too, instead of a silently empty picker.
- **Deep-review pass on the ticket-type catalog batch above (#351/#387), before release:** a type could be deleted at the exact moment it was being assigned to an attendee (create, edit, or CSV import), leaving the attendee referencing a type that no longer exists — those write paths now serialize against a type's deletion the same way deletion already serializes against them. CSV import's preview no longer writes an unmatched value's raw text into server logs (the preview shown to the admin is unchanged). The Danger Zone's delete-event guard now also blocks while a custom ticket type exists, the same way it already blocks on a custom event item — the auto-seeded default "Standard" type doesn't count. An attendee's ticket type no longer silently shows as blank on the edit form if it was deleted from the catalog after being assigned — it now shows as "*value* (not in catalog)" so the admin can see and fix it. CSV/XLSX export, the check-in Recent scans list, manual search results, and the event overview's live check-in feed now all show a type's current label and color instead of its internal key, matching what already showed everywhere else in the app. Existing events' one-time migration now deterministically keeps the earliest-created casing of a type's name as its label, instead of an unspecified one. Also fixed a stray CSV import help-text line that said an unmatched ticket type skips just that value — it actually skips the whole row, matching every other unmatched-field behavior in import.
- **Second deep-review pass on the ticket-type catalog batch (#496), before release:** creating or renaming a type to a label that's already used elsewhere in the same event is now rejected with a clear message, instead of silently producing two visually identical entries that CSV import would later refuse to resolve with no explanation. Navigating between two events' Event Settings pages, or switching events while the Communication "Send" dialog stays open, no longer briefly shows the previous event's ticket types. If a CSV import row was valid at preview but its ticket type got deleted before commit, the import result now lists it as an invalid row instead of silently vanishing from the created/updated counts with no explanation. The one-time migration that backfills existing events' catalogs is now safe to run from multiple instances at once during a rolling deploy (it previously could crash a replica's startup on a rare timing collision), and now also cleans up whitespace-only stored values instead of leaving them stuck outside the catalog forever.
- **Third deep-review pass on the ticket-type catalog batch (#496), before release:** a type deleted in the brief window between a CSV import's catalog snapshot and its write transaction actually starting could still get written onto a new attendee — closed for both the admin UI's import and the standalone CLI importer (which had no protection against this at all until now). The check-in scan bar's search suggestions and the Communication "Send" dialog's selected ticket-type filter (not just its options list) no longer show/keep a stale or meaningless value after switching events. The Add attendee form now actually blocks submitting while its ticket-type dropdown failed to load, instead of only showing the error — and now also blocks during the brief window while the dropdown is still loading, not just after it fails, so an attendee can't be created before the admin has seen what types are even available. The Event Settings "Ticket types" tab now shows a proper inline error with a Retry button when the catalog fails to load, instead of just a toast.
- **Deep-review pass on the Reports ticket-type breakdown batch (#387), before release:** an attendee whose stored ticket type has no matching catalog row (the type was deleted after being assigned, or an event's data was seeded/restored outside the app's normal write paths) still counted in the summary and showed in the admission log, but silently vanished from the "By ticket type" breakdown and had no filter option — it now gets its own "*value* (not in catalog)" entry there too, the same fallback the attendee edit form already uses. The admission log's "Ticket type" column now labels an untyped admission "(none)", matching the breakdown and filter above it, instead of the plain dash the shared Attendees badge uses elsewhere. The filter's internal "no type" sentinel could also collide with a real, unmatched raw value that happened to equal it — the two are now encoded so they can never be conflated.
- **Post-merge follow-up on the ticket-type catalog batch:** the Add attendee form's earlier "block submit while the catalog is loading or failed to load" fix (above) was too strict — ticket type is an optional field, so a slow or briefly-broken catalog fetch blocked adding an attendee at all, even one meant to have no type. A loading or failed catalog fetch no longer blocks submitting; the dropdown just has nothing but the blank option to offer during that window, same as always being allowed to leave it unset.
- **Ticket-type catalog batch, defense-in-depth follow-up:** an attendee's ticket type is now enforced at the database level too, on top of the catalog checks every write path already performs — a database constraint that only data written outside those paths (raw SQL, a restored backup) could ever violate. No visible change for normal use; a type already can't be deleted while any attendee has it, and this doesn't change that. Existing self-hosted installs that still have pre-catalog data at the moment they upgrade are unaffected — the constraint only rejects new writes, not data already in the database, and the existing one-time migration normalizes it as before.
- **Second post-merge follow-up, same batch:** that same fix re-enabled submitting while the catalog was loading, but a ticket type already selected before switching to a different event (with the modal left open) stayed selected underneath — the dropdown visibly reset, but the previously-picked key could still be submitted onto the new event, where it might mean something else entirely or not exist at all. Switching events now clears the selection itself, not just the dropdown's option list, the same fix already applied to the Communication "Send" dialog for the identical pattern.
- **Fourth deep-review pass on the ticket-type catalog batch (#496), before release:** renaming a type's label while a genuinely concurrent request patched only its color could silently revert the rename back to the old label, since the color-only request always re-included the label it had read moments earlier — closed by only ever writing a label back when it actually differs from what was just read, so a same-label patch can no longer clobber a rename that lands in between. The standalone CLI importer's commit transaction now has the same explicit timeout budget as the admin API's import commit, instead of Prisma's default 5s, which a large CSV competing for the per-event catalog lock could exceed. The CLI importer and the admin API's import routes no longer maintain two separately-drifting copies of the same catalog-loading helper.
- **Public ticket page rendered with no visual styling for every attendee, on every event — not a rare edge case.** `TICKET_LAYOUT_CSS` references `--surface-page`, `--surface-card`, `--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--text-muted`, `--border`, and `--surface-sunken`, but the ticket page is a fully self-contained document that never loads `packages/ui`'s design-token stylesheet, and `resolveThemeVars` (the only thing populating its inline `<style>` block) only ever emits `--primary`/`--primary-hover`/`--primary-active`/`--primary-tint` and, optionally, `--font-sans` — so every one of those properties silently resolved to nothing, leaving the card background, borders, and text color unstyled aside from the primary-color-derived bits (buttons, badges, the brand mark). Found incidentally while verifying #503 (ticket page logo) in a browser; fixed here as its own change, out of scope for that PR. Each affected declaration now carries an inline `var(--x, <light-mode-literal>)` fallback matching `packages/ui`'s existing token values, the same pattern this file already used for `--radius-lg`/`--shadow-md` — confirmed by rendering a real ticket page and reading `getComputedStyle` in a browser, not just checking the generated CSS text.
- **UX polish bundle (closes #271):** the Attendees table's first load showed bare "Loading attendees…" text and gave no indication a re-fetch was in progress when changing filters/pages while rows were already on screen — it now shows a skeleton placeholder on first load and dims the existing rows during a re-fetch. The check-in column's raw "✓" text glyph is now a Tabler icon, matching status indicators everywhere else in the app. The check-in scan bar's submit button was 36×36px, under the ~44px touch-target guideline — bumped to 44×44px. The shared `BrandMark` (Admitto logo SVG) — already used by four of the five admin shells — is now a real component instead of a static value, so the one remaining holdout (`SetupWizardPage`, which had its own copy-pasted SVG) can use it too while still sizing itself independently via `className`. Audited `prefers-reduced-motion` coverage in `packages/ui`: the switch toggle's slide, the mobile nav drawer's slide, and the toast entrance animation are now skipped entirely for users who've asked for reduced motion; the loading spinner and skeleton shimmer are slowed/stilled rather than fully stopped, since a fully static spinner loses its "still working" meaning. The bundle's other three items (drawer focus trap, an inline-style cleanup, and check-in lookup feedback consistency) turned out to already be resolved by unrelated work since the issue was filed — verified against current code, not just re-read from the original report.
- **Adding a check-in note with a valid, own `eventId` but an `attendeeId` belonging to a different event returned a raw 500 instead of a clean 404.** `addAttendeeNote`'s composite `{id, event_id}` lookup already correctly rejected the write (zero rows written, no security impact), but it threw a plain untyped `Error` that `handleCheckinNote`'s catch block didn't recognize, falling through to the generic 500 branch. Added a typed `AttendeeNotFoundError` (matching the file's existing `NoteTooLongError`/`OperatorRequiredError` pattern) and a matching catch branch returning `404 {error: "not found"}`, consistent with `getAttendeeCard`'s existing 404 for the same scenario.
- **The no-flash `useDelayedLoading` rollout above introduced a worse flash of its own in several panels with a 3-way loading/empty/data branch, caught by external code review.** `showLoading` (the delayed flag, meant only to decide whether a spinner/skeleton renders) had been wired in as if it were the raw `loading` flag deciding the branch itself — so for the first ~200ms of every genuine load, before `showLoading` had even flipped true, these panels fell straight through "still loading" into their confirmed-empty state instead: Event Overview's 3 KPI tiles briefly showed "—" (unavailable) instead of blank, the Setup checklist / Check-in progress cards briefly claimed "Unavailable", and Pinned note / Key contacts / Links & files briefly showed their empty-state "Add a…" prompts; Requirements' Event items table and Custom attendee fields card briefly claimed "No items yet" / "No custom fields yet"; the Ticket types tab (via Event Settings' call site) briefly claimed "No ticket types yet"; and the Attendees list footer briefly claimed "0 attendees" on the very first page load. Fixed by threading both flags through everywhere this pattern occurs: the raw `loading` flag now always gates the loading/empty/data branch, with `showLoading` only deciding whether the spinner/text renders within the loading branch — restoring the original flash-free behavior without resurrecting the premature-empty-state flash it was meant to prevent. Added stash-verified regression tests (failing against the pre-fix code, passing after) for every affected panel. Follow-up CI round: a new Audit log test asserting the Time column's label changes when switching to "Local" was flaky under CI's UTC runner (it only holds when the runner's own local timezone differs from UTC) — it now computes the expected label the same way `AuditLogPanel` itself does instead of assuming "Local" never reads "UTC"; two abort-race regression tests (resolving/rejecting the audit-log fetch after unmount) had no actual assertion, now asserting no console error was logged; SonarCloud cleanup on the same PR - two nested ternaries in `EventOverviewPage`'s Setup checklist/Check-in progress cards extracted into a shared `unavailablePlaceholderText` helper, one in `TicketTypesCard` into a small `TicketTypesLoadingHint` component, `AuditLogPanel`'s `replace(/_/g, ...)` switched to `replaceAll`, an icon-then-text JSX pair in `IdentityProviderEditor`'s protocol picker given the same explicit `{" "}` spacing already used elsewhere, and a test's `waitFor`+`getByText` swapped for `findByText` - no behavior change.
