Merged in the batch 08 Identity/Audit log work above (#586, landed on `main` first): the two branches had independently converted the same two editor modals and needed to be reconciled rather than layered - #586's already-shipped, PO-approved decisions won wherever the two disagreed (backdrop click stays inert rather than closing the modal; the Details popover flips above/below the trigger and dismisses via `useClickOutside` rather than an in-flow backdrop button that blocked switching straight to another row; Previous/Next scroll-restore targets the panel via `scrollIntoView`, not `window.scrollTo`, which never actually scrolled in this app's shell; both editors' Close button and Escape are disabled for the same busy window their footer Cancel already respected). Two genuine improvements from this branch were kept and generalized: the shared `IdentityModalHeader` component now backs both editors' headers (closing the SonarCloud duplication flag on their near-identical header markup, extended with a `closeDisabled` prop for the busy-guard above), and `useModalFocusTrap`'s optional `focusWhenReady` parameter is wired to each editor's `loadState` so focus correctly moves into the form once an edit-mode fetch resolves, instead of only trying once at mount while the panel still just showed a spinner. The From/To audit-log date filters now compute their day boundary in whichever zone the Time column is displaying, not always UTC, and the viewer's timezone is recomputed on every use instead of cached in a module-level constant at first import - both fixes verified with dedicated regression tests carried over from this branch. SonarCloud caught one more artifact of that reconciliation: both branches had independently added an identical `.cf-access-summary strong`/`.cf-access-summary p` rule pair, so the merge carried both copies side by side (duplicate selectors, no visual difference) - the second copy is removed.
- Check-in (desktop): with "Auto-advance after valid check-in" turned on, confirming an attendee who had hand-out items configured (badge, gift bag, headset) cleared their card instantly — including the "Mark issued" buttons — so the operator had to search the same person again just to hand the items over. The desktop card now stays up whenever it lists items, matching the mobile check-in overlay (#434).
- Attendee card: the PREVIEW state's dismiss button read "Cancel" while every other state's read "Clear" for the identical action — unified on **Clear** everywhere on this card (#449 review).
- Recent scans sidebar: reversed admissions (operator self-undo or admin revoke) were excluded from `getRecentCheckIns`' query entirely, so a revoked/undone attendee kept showing as a permanently green "Checked in" row with no indication it had been reversed. The sidebar now shows a distinct "Undone" or "Revoked" row (grey dot) for these events.
- "Revoke check-in" (Check-in page card and Attendee Detail page, #449) stayed visible and clickable after the attendee's **pass** had already been revoked, because its visibility only checked `admitted_at` and ignored `status`. Now hidden once the pass itself is revoked — a stale admitted timestamp on an already-revoked pass no longer offers a redundant/confusing action.
- **Revoke pass now auto-clears any current admission.** PO review: revoking a pass left `admitted_at` untouched, so restoring the pass later silently resurrected a "checked in" state from before the revoke, with no new scan ever happening. Revoking a pass while the attendee is admitted now also un-admits them (rolls back an auto-issued badge and logs `check_in_revoked`, same as the standalone Revoke check-in action) in the same transaction as the status change.
- Attendee card: the "Notes" section used unstyled browser defaults (heading-sized `<h3>`, default paragraph margins) and sat flush against the status banner above it — restyled to match the card's small-caps section-label convention (used elsewhere for Recent scans) with a separating top border and tighter spacing between entries.
- Check-in attendee search no longer matches on **company or department** — only name and email. Searching "Hitachi" used to surface both "Hitachi" and "Hitachi Energy" (and anyone else sharing a company/department substring), which read as a bug rather than a useful result (PO review). Company and department are still shown on each result for context; they're just never used to find one. The scan-bar placeholder now reads "Scan QR · type name or email…". (Removed the extra `custom_data` JSON company/department raw-SQL branch and its result-merge from `lookupAttendees`, since those fields are no longer searched.)
- **Check-in item hand-out — follow-up review of the desktop/mobile flow (#454):** several correctness and accessibility fixes. On the mobile item-issuing screen, navigating **Back** to a just-issued item that has a further step (e.g. a headset configured to require return) showed "Already returned" instead of "Already issued" — the badge re-derived the state from the item's *next* legal action rather than remembering what the operator actually submitted. An item-action / add-note / undo request that failed and was then retried successfully left a stale "Request failed" banner sitting over the successful result — each of these now clears the banner as it starts, matching how a fresh scan already did. When an item action failed while the mobile camera overlay was open, the identical error mounted twice (the page-level paragraph *and* the overlay's own, both `role="alert"`), so a screen reader announced it twice — the page-level one is now suppressed while the overlay covers it, leaving exactly one. The mobile item-summary's **Undo last check-in** now stays disabled while a check-in confirmation is still pending, matching its desktop counterpart. A revoked/invalid pass's "block item actions and admin-revoke" check now fails **closed** for any unrecognized status (defense-in-depth) instead of only matching an explicit list, and is derived from the same status-grouping used by the status badge so the two can't drift.
- **Archived events now fully lock down check-in, not just admin edits.** Previously archived events deliberately stayed reachable for check-in (the old `ADR 0022` carve-out) — but that let an officially-over event still receive scans, lookups, and admits. Archived events are now excluded from the operator's check-in event list, and every check-in route (scan, lookup, admit, notes, undo, attendee card, stats, stream) returns `403` with `This event is archived.` — reusing the same guard already used by admin mutating routes, so behavior is consistent everywhere. The admin's embedded Check-in tab now shows a "Check-in is disabled" empty state (matching the icon + title + description pattern used elsewhere, e.g. Reports' "No check-ins yet") instead of the scanner. Found during code-review: the emergency ops CLI's `checkin lookup`/`checkin admit` commands call the same check-in domain functions directly, bypassing the HTTP layer entirely — they now run their own archived check first, so the CLI can't be used as a side door around this lockdown.
- Superadmin: archived events are reachable again from the events picker. The Archived tab's event cards had no navigation link at all — only the Unarchive button worked, so a superadmin had no way to open an archived event's Overview/Settings from the picker. The card body is clickable again; Unarchive remains an independent action button alongside it.
- Events picker: archived event cards now use the exact same card style as active ones (same border, spacing, layout), just with an **Archived** badge instead of **Active** — previously archived cards had a visually distinct "static" style plus an on-card **Unarchive** button and a separate caption row, which looked like a different, lesser component. The card's left accent border is now grey for archived events instead of staying the active-event green. Unarchive is no longer offered from the picker card (it remains available from Event Settings → Danger zone and from the superadmin's instance-wide Archiving panel) to keep the card a pure navigation surface. Danger zone copy on Event Settings updated to reflect that archived events are now fully read-only including check-in (stale text still said "Check-in remains available").
- **Archiving/unarchiving an event live no longer requires a page reload to take effect on other tabs.** Event Settings kept its own separate copy of the event and refreshed it correctly after Archive/Unarchive/Save — but every other page nested under the same event (Check-in, Attendees, Requirements, Communication, Import) reads the event from one shared layout state that was only fetched once and never refreshed on its own. Archiving an event while, say, the Check-in tab was already open left it looking active (scanner still mounted) until a full reload. Settings now asks the shared layout to refetch immediately after a successful save/archive/unarchive, so this Check-in lockdown (and every sibling page) reflects the change instantly, without navigating away or reloading.
- **Archiving an event live no longer requires a page reload for these controls to grey out.** Event Settings kept its own separate copy of the event and refreshed it correctly after Archive/Unarchive/Save, but every page above (Attendees, Requirements, Communication, Import) reads the event from one shared layout state that was only fetched once and never refreshed on its own — so archiving an event while, say, Requirements was already open left its controls clickable until a full reload. Settings now asks the shared layout to refetch immediately after a successful save/archive/unarchive, so every `ArchivedGuard`-protected control greys out instantly, without navigating away or reloading.
- **Event settings — Status card had a leftover 4th "Items" cell duplicating the Requirements link:** the Status card was described (and CSS-laid-out) as a 3-column summary (current status, organization, created date), but a 4th cell — "Items: N configured" with a "Manage in Requirements →" link — had survived, wrapping onto its own row in the fixed 3-column grid and recreating the exact duplicate-link problem the standalone "Event items" card was removed for. Removed the leftover cell and its now-unused `enabledItemsCount` computation; Requirements stays the one place to manage items (#394).
- **Event Overview crashed the whole page ("Something went wrong") when `ticket_type_breakdown` was missing from the API response**, reading it unguarded in the Check-in progress card. This field only exists on a backend built from a version at or after the Event Overview redesign — `apps/web`'s dev server has no watch mode, so a still-running older process (or, in production, a brief window during a rolling deploy where new frontend assets reach a browser before the matching backend is live) serves a response genuinely missing it, not just an empty array. Now defaults to `[]` when absent, matching the same defensive pattern already used for the Attendee Detail page's `event_items`.
- **Eleven `color: var(--text)` references across `staff.css`, `attendees.css`, and `logo-upload.css` pointed at a CSS custom property that was never defined.** `--text` doesn't exist in `packages/ui/src/styles/tokens/colors.css` (only `--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--text-muted`, `--text-disabled`, `--text-link`, and `--text-on-primary` do); since `color` is inherited, each site silently fell back to whatever color its parent happened to have instead of breaking visibly. Ten sites (Overview's contact/readiness/glance labels, pinned-note body, and note textarea; the Attendees toolbar/bulk-bar clear-button hover states and more-actions menu item text; the logo upload's remove button) now resolve to `--text-primary`. `.attendee-success`'s status-tinted box instead resolves to `--status-ok-fg`, matching the shared `Notice` component's `--status-ok-fg`/`--status-warn-fg` convention for text inside colored status boxes. A twelfth site, `.attendee-form__warn`, is left as `var(--text)` here since it's already resolved by a separate in-flight change that migrates that box to the shared `Notice` component entirely.

- **System Logs retention notice** has clearer spacing below the Copy / Clear view footer.

- **Instance Settings → Security → Audit log — details column, timezone, and pagination scroll (closes #414).** Expanding a row's Details no longer explodes the table (the JSON preview is now a small popover anchored to the row instead of an inline `<details>` block that pushed every row below it down). Added a UTC/Local toggle for the Time column (browser's own timezone) instead of UTC-only. Previous/Next no longer jump the page to the top of Settings — the scroll position is restored once the new page has rendered.
- **Instance URL's description text read one size larger than the equivalent description on every other field, and the "No instance URL configured" warning was bare orange text with no border, background, or icon**, unlike the "Live view only" notice already on the Logs tab. The description (and its "configured" success line) now matches the same size every other field's hint text gets automatically from the shared `Input` component; the warning renders through a new shared `Notice` component (`@admitto/ui`, info/success/warning/error variants) instead of a plain paragraph, and the Logs tab's own notice now uses the same component.
- **Event Settings → Images typography.** Event logo and Upload images now share the same intro / dropzone title / format-hint sizes (and the upload dropzone no longer inherits browser button font quirks).

- **The shared `Notice` component's icon sat about 1px above the vertical center of the first line of text**, most visible on a screenshot zoomed in past 100% (real-browser measurement across all 4 variants, short and wrapped text, and content with an embedded button, confirmed a consistent -1px delta every time — not a per-variant inconsistency). `.at-notice__icon`'s `margin-top` moves from 2px to 3px, which measures out to a perfect 0px delta in every one of those same scenarios.
- **Event settings → Mail showed two separate Save controls at once** — the page header's Save button (actually bound to the General tab's own fields, but visible on every tab) and the mail card's own Save/Reset pair further down the form. The Mail tab now drives its save/reset logic through the single page-header Save/Reset pair instead of rendering a second footer; the card no longer has its own Save/Reset buttons, so there is exactly one Save (and one Reset next to it) per tab.
- **Event settings → General: scrolling the timezone picker's dropdown could briefly show an option's text bleeding through above the sticky region-group header** (e.g. "GMT+2"), because the header's sticky offset left a hairline gap uncovered during scroll. The offset now matches the list's own padding so the header fully covers the content scrolling beneath it.
- **Settings tabs (and Event Settings' own sub-tabs) showed a visible horizontal scrollbar on narrower screens instead of just hinting there was more to scroll to.** Replaced with a shared `ScrollFadeTabs` component that softly fades the left/right edge of the tab strip when there's more content that way, and hides that fade once you've scrolled all the way to that side — no visible scrollbar track at any point.
- **Communication template editor: inserting an image placeholder (built-in, or a custom `{{name}}` from the new image asset library) right at the very start of the MJML source silently produced no image at all, with no error shown.** MJML silently drops any markup outside its `<mjml>...</mjml>` root instead of raising an error, so a token inserted before the editor had ever been focused (cursor at position 0) landed just before `<mjml>` and was discarded during rendering. The editor now detects an insertion point outside the document root and redirects it to just inside the template's last section instead, and wraps a bare token in its own text element so it can't be silently dropped as loose text either. Also fixed a related timing bug where clicking a placeholder chip multiple times faster than React re-renders could insert at the same stale cursor position and produce broken, nested markup.
- **Events picker — the "Archived events" tab (and the "all events archived" case on the Active tab) showed a plain, hand-rolled `Card` and paragraph instead of the shared `EmptyState` component** used everywhere else on the same page and across the app (closes #397). The all-archived case keeps its "view archived events" affordance, now as a real button instead of an inline text link.
- **Mobile nav drawer — the Admitto brand mark was hidden behind the fixed topbar when the drawer opened**, because `.sidebar` and `.topbar` shared the same z-index and lost the paint-order tie to DOM order (closes #453). The drawer now paints above the topbar and spans the full viewport width — it was previously capped at 240px, leaving a sliver of the topbar (including its account controls) visibly overlapping the drawer's top-left corner. Since a full-width drawer now covers the hamburger toggle entirely, the drawer has its own explicit close button, styled to match the toggle and centered against the brand mark. The sidebar's footer (All events / Administration / My account / version) is pinned to the bottom of the sidebar when there's spare vertical room, and the whole column scrolls together as one unit when there isn't, instead of only the nav list scrolling independently while the footer stayed rigidly fixed.
- `ImportPage.flow.test.tsx`: the post-commit history-refresh test asserted `fetchImportHistory` had been called twice immediately after `findByText("Import complete")` resolved, but the `historyToken`-keyed effect that fires the second call isn't guaranteed to have flushed by that exact point — flaky under CI's parallel test load, same class of race already fixed once for `EventSettingsPage.test.tsx` (#459). Now waits for the call count itself via `waitFor`, matching that precedent.
- **Communication template Preview: branding images uploaded on a local dev instance weren't visually loading in the preview pane.** Local-environment-only gap, unrelated to production. The dev server's fallback base URL for resolving uploaded-image links was hardcoded to a different port than this instance actually runs on, and the admin SPA's Content-Security-Policy only allowed `https:` image sources, blocking a plain `http://localhost` preview URL outright. A `BASE_URL` environment variable now pins the correct local port, and the CSP additionally allows `http://localhost:*` images only when `NODE_ENV=development` (production behavior is unchanged).
- `SettingsPanels.rendering.test.tsx`: the shared `afterEach` used `vi.clearAllMocks()`, which clears call history but not queued `mockResolvedValueOnce`/`mockRejectedValueOnce` chains or one-off `mockImplementation` overrides — several tests here size those chains to an exact expected call count, and a few tie that count to real `setInterval`-driven polling (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS`), so a slower or faster run could leave one unconsumed. A leftover then silently answered whichever next test called the same mocked function first, surfacing as different, unrelated tests failing intermittently only when the full 94-test file ran together (seen on `SessionsPanel rendering > includes a device label…` in CI and separately on `exports the current filters as CSV and toasts on failure` in local reruns) — each passed reliably in isolation, which is what made it read as two unrelated bugs rather than one shared-state leak. Switched to `vi.resetAllMocks()`, matching the identical fix already applied to `CheckInPage.scan-queue.test.tsx`.

- **Product brand SVGs ship in the production image again.** `@admitto/ui` build copies `admitto-mark.svg` / `admitto-logo.svg` into `dist/assets` (tsc alone does not emit them), and the ticket-asset routes prefer that path. Without this, container deploys 404'd `/assets/admitto-mark.svg` and `/assets/admitto-logo.svg` after the files moved out of `apps/web/src/assets`.
- **Search box now matches the standard 36px control height** used by the Filters button, Selects, and every other input on this page - it was 3px taller (unconstrained height from its own padding, never pinned to `--control-h` like its neighbours).
- **Storage GC rechecks references immediately before each delete** (and dry-run count), matching the fail-closed pattern used by branding replace cleanup, so a concurrent save cannot lose a newly referenced upload. Listing also skips files that disappear between `readdir` and `stat` instead of aborting the whole sweep.
- **Logo Edit after Save/reload.** Organisation and event logos now keep the full pre-crop upload and last crop/zoom framing, so **Edit image** restores the adjust popup after refresh instead of forcing a new file pick. Tickets and mail still use the cropped `logo_url` only. Legacy logos uploaded before this change still ask for the full file once when you re-crop.
- **New event timezone list no longer flashes below then jumps above** the field: placement is chosen before first paint (`useLayoutEffect` + estimated height).
- **`SettingsPanels.rendering.test.tsx`'s `AuditLogPanel rendering > resets to page 1 once a retried page no longer exists against the current total` kept flaking intermittently under the full 186-file `apps/admin` suite, even after already being fixed once with fake timers and a split `waitFor` (#585).** Root-caused to a second, unrelated race the first fix never touched: `useLogQuery`'s search-debounce effect (shared by the Audit and Security views) unconditionally called `setPage(1)` every time its 300ms timer fired, even when the debounced value hadn't actually changed from what's already committed — which is every mount, since the search box starts (and in this test, stays) empty. Under real full-suite CPU contention, that timer can fire in the middle of this test's Next-then-Retry sequence, silently resetting the page back to 1 mid-flight and triggering an unplanned extra request that consumes one of the test's precisely-queued mock responses out of order — confirmed directly by instrumenting the component and showing that a real timing delay inserted at that exact point deterministically flips the test from passing to failing before this fix, and back to passing after it. Independent of the test, this was also a small real product bug: every Audit/Security log view fired a redundant, unconditional second fetch 300ms after every mount even with an untouched search box, and could silently snap an operator back to page 1 if they paginated within that window. Fixed at the root instead of adding another test-side timing workaround — the debounce now compares against the currently-committed search value and is a no-op when it hasn't changed.
- **Two CSS custom properties, `--surface-muted` and `--surface-elevated`, were referenced across 4 files (9 declarations total) but never actually defined anywhere** — every reference silently fell back to whatever hardcoded hex value that particular call site happened to improvise (`#f4f4f5`, `#fafafa`, `var(--at-gray-100)`, `var(--border)`, each a different guess), and 3 declarations in `users-page.css` had no fallback at all, rendering a fully transparent background instead of the intended subtle gray. Both tokens are now defined in `packages/ui/src/styles/tokens/colors.css` (`--surface-muted` aliases the existing `--surface-sunken`/`--at-gray-50`; `--surface-elevated` aliases `--surface-card`), and the now-redundant inline fallbacks are removed from the 6 declarations that had one.
- **The searchable timezone picker's arrow-key navigation was invisible to screen readers.** `TimezoneSelect`'s custom listbox moved a visual highlight via CSS as the user pressed Up/Down in the search box, but never told assistive tech which option was active — focus stayed on the input the whole time and no ARIA relationship announced the virtual selection. Each option now gets a stable `id`, and the search input's `aria-activedescendant` tracks the highlighted option (omitted when nothing matches), so screen readers announce the active option as arrow-key navigation moves it.
- **Event timestamps now use an unambiguous numeric UTC offset at the displayed instant.** Staff viewing the same event no longer receive locale-dependent abbreviations such as `CEST` or `IST`; event time shows its IANA zone and/or `UTC+/-` offset, while system/admin timestamps explicitly say `UTC`.
- **Landing on `/operator` while signed in as an admin or superadmin now redirects straight to `/admin`**, instead of dropping into the operator kiosk shell — that shell has no admin navigation, so an admin who followed a stray link or bookmark had no way back out except editing the URL by hand.
- SonarCloud maintainability cleanup across `apps/admin/src`: replaced `String.match` with `RegExp.exec`, `parseInt` with `Number.parseInt`, and global-regex `.replace` with `.replaceAll`, added `Readonly<...>` to component prop types, hoisted a nested function declaration (`redirectToLogin`) out of its enclosing component, and removed a redundant assignment and an unsupported ARIA attribute on a non-widget button — mechanical, no behavior change.
- `EventSettingsPage.test.tsx` (#459): the branding-save test clicked "Save changes" right after `waitFor`-ing the upload preview's alt text, assuming that also meant the button's label had already flipped from "Uploading…" — but the preview render and the Save button's label update land in separate React commits (the latter only fires once `LogoUploadZone`'s `onUploadingChange` effect runs a tick later), so under CPU contention from parallel test files the click could land before the button existed under that name (flaky `getElementError` on `npx vitest run`, not reproducible in isolation or with `--no-file-parallelism`). Now waits for the button itself via `findByRole` before clicking, matching the pattern already used by the adjacent "disables Save while a branding upload is in flight" test.

### Security
- **Bounce IMAP ingest uses the same SSRF guard as SMTP, including DNS pinning.** `imap_host` is rejected at save time (Event settings → Mailing → Bounce detection) and rechecked immediately before every IMAP connect (bounce ingest sidecar, **Test connection**, **Also verify bounce**) when it is, or resolves to, a private, loopback, link-local, or cloud-metadata address. The connect uses the validated IP while keeping SNI on the configured hostname (same pattern as SMTP), so a second DNS lookup cannot rebind past the guard. Same `ALLOW_PRIVATE_MAIL_DESTINATIONS` lab escape hatch as SMTP.
- **Bulk ticket send/resend recorded which admin triggered it only in a best-effort action-log write that silently dropped the attribution on failure (just a server-side `console.error`, no retry, no fallback).** `EmailDelivery` rows now carry the triggering admin's `actor_user_id`/`session_id` directly, written in the same call that queues each email — durable independently of that separate log write, and consistent with the per-attendee resend endpoint, which already recorded this transactionally. Applies uniformly to bulk send, bulk resend, and single-attendee resend; rows created outside an authenticated admin request (e.g. an automated initial send) leave both columns null, same as the existing `client_timezone` column.
- **Event-level dedicated mail transport (#511) could be used to redirect the organization's real SMTP password or Power Automate key to a server the event admin controls.** Because the event and organization mail configs resolve field-by-field (an event can override just one field and inherit the rest), an event admin — a role with less access than the superadmin-only organization Mail settings page — could save an event override that set only the connection target (SMTP host, or Power Automate URL) while leaving the credential unset, causing the organization's real password/key to be sent to that attacker-chosen endpoint on the next send or on the event's own "Send test email" (which every event manager already has access to). Saving or sending now requires an event that overrides its own connection target to also supply its own credential — it can no longer silently borrow the organization's. Microsoft Graph is unaffected: a mismatched tenant/client always authenticates against Microsoft's own servers, never an event-admin-chosen one, so there is nothing to redirect.
- Fixed a related gap in the same mail-settings validation: submitting a request that only clears a secret (e.g. wiping the stored SMTP password with no other change) skipped completeness validation entirely, silently leaving an active transport unable to authenticate with no error shown — now validated like any other change. Also closed a race where two concurrent saves to the same event's mail settings could each validate against stale, pre-write state and together leave a configuration that was never actually validated as a whole.
- **Event-level mail transport settings (#511) now require superadmin, matching the organization-wide Mail settings page, instead of any organization admin.** A pentest found that an ordinary event admin — someone managing one event, not the whole instance — could point an event's Power Automate webhook URL or SMTP host at an address on Admitto's own internal network and, via the existing "Send test email" feature, make the server issue a real outbound request there, with part of the response echoed back to them. Mail transport is technical configuration (credentials, webhook URLs, SMTP hosts), not everyday event management, so the "Mailing" tab is now hidden from non-superadmin event settings the same way the "Integrations" tab already is.
- **Mail transport destinations (Power Automate URL, SMTP host) are now checked against a private/loopback/link-local/cloud-metadata address blocklist, both when saved and immediately before the server connects.** Independently of the authorization fix above, this closes the underlying server-side request forgery outright — reuses the same address-validation logic already proven for OIDC provider discovery, so a misconfiguration or a future config surface can't turn either transport into a way to reach internal infrastructure.
- **Closed a DNS-rebinding gap in the mail transport blocklist above: the address check and the real connection could be tricked into resolving a hostname differently.** The blocklist validated a mail destination's DNS records, but then let Power Automate's `fetch` and SMTP's `nodemailer` connect by re-resolving the same hostname themselves — a malicious or compromised DNS server could answer safely for the validation lookup and answer with an internal address moments later for the real connection, bypassing the check entirely. Both transports now pin the real connection to the already-validated address instead of re-resolving: Power Automate reuses the DNS-pinning pattern already proven for OIDC provider discovery (an `undici` dispatcher pinned to the validated address, keeping the real hostname for TLS SNI); SMTP resolves and validates the host before building its connection pool at all, then connects to the validated address directly.
- **Attendee detail (`GET /api/admin/events/:eventId/attendees/:id`) no longer decrypts the attendee's bearer ticket token to build a `ticket_ref` preview.** The admin UI stopped rendering that preview a while back, so the backend was decrypting a full bearer credential and handing an 8-character remainder to the browser for no operational reason — pure unused exposure surface. `token_enc`/`public_ref` are no longer selected for this endpoint at all; ticket issuance, resend, and the token columns themselves are unaffected.
- **External services weather base URL and geocoding base URL reject private, loopback, link-local, and cloud-metadata hosts** (including DNS recheck) before save or Test connection, so UI-editable endpoints cannot be used to probe internal networks from the web process.
- **Static map tile fetches harden outbound SSRF edges:** tile downloads use `redirect: "manual"` and reject private / loopback / link-local / cloud-metadata redirect targets (and non-https URLs outside local development), and tile bodies must start with PNG magic bytes before sharp composites them for `GET /m/{eventId}.png`.
- **Static map tile failure logs redact credential-bearing URLs:** `StaticMapRenderError` messages and system-log reasons strip query/hash from tile URLs so a commercial `MAP_TILE_URL` with `?api_key=` does not land in stdout or the admin System logs buffer.
- **Users & roles: two review-flagged authorization gaps closed.** An implicit role-type switch (granting a different role type replaces the old one) now re-checks revoke authorization against the freshly-refetched assignments inside the same transaction, not just once beforehand, closing a narrow window where a concurrent grant could let the switch remove access the actor was never authorized to revoke on its own. OIDC group-role sync no longer creates a second, conflicting role type on top of an assignment (manual or from another OIDC rule) a user already holds at login time; it defers to whichever type they already have instead, the same "never delete a pre-existing assignment" rule the sync already applied to deletions.

- **Staff account self-service actions that would strip your own access are now blocked server-side**, matching the existing `cannot_deactivate_self` / `cannot_delete_self` guards: a superadmin can no longer remove their own role assignment (`cannot_remove_own_role`), switch their own account to a different role type (`cannot_change_own_role`), or unlink SSO from their own account (`cannot_unlink_own_sso`) — each returns a 409 from the API, and the matching UI controls (the role chip's remove button, the Role selector, the Unlink button) are disabled with an explanatory tooltip on your own account instead of only failing after the fact.
- **A role-type switch (Edit user) now authorizes revoking each assignment it implicitly replaces, not just the new grant.** An org-scoped admin's own event-grant authority previously let them silently strip a target's unrelated admin/superadmin assignment as a side effect of granting an unrelated operator role; each replaced assignment is now checked with the same rule an explicit revoke already enforces, so a non-superadmin can no longer replace an admin- or superadmin-scoped assignment at all.
- **Unlinking SSO can no longer lock a JIT-provisioned account out entirely.** A user created by first-time SSO sign-in gets a random, nobody-knows-it placeholder password; unlinking their only sign-in method used to leave `password_hash` untouched, so a follow-up "you'll need Reset password too" reminder in the confirm dialog was the only thing standing between an admin and an account nobody can sign into anymore. Unlink now requires a new password in the same request, sets it, revokes sessions and trusted devices, and unlinks in one transaction.
- **A role-type switch can no longer silently delete an IdP-managed role assignment.** The implicit revoke a role-type switch performs on the target's old-type assignment(s) skipped the `managed_by_idp` check an explicit revoke already enforces; granting an unrelated new role type was a back door around it. The switch now also runs at Serializable isolation (matching the explicit revoke), closing a window where two concurrent role-type switches could each pass the last-superadmin check before either committed.
- **`X-Forwarded-For`/`-Host`/`-Proto` were trusted from any direct connection once `TRUST_PROXY=true`, not just from the actual reverse proxy.** A client reaching the app directly — a misconfigured port exposure, or anything else on the same network — could forge its own rate-limit bucket via `X-Forwarded-For` (unlimited login attempts, since `auth:login-ip`'s throttle never triggered), the CSRF same-origin check's expected origin via `X-Forwarded-Host`/`-Proto`, and the session cookie's `Secure` flag via `X-Forwarded-Proto` — all three by simply setting the header. `TRUST_PROXY` now gates on a second, independent check: the request's direct TCP peer must also fall inside a new `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` allowlist (defaults to loopback-only) before any of these headers are honoured. The default production topology (`deploy/docker-compose.yml`) pins its internal Docker network to a fixed subnet so this allowlist has a concrete, working value out of the box.
- **Closed an audit-trail durability gap for auth/security events, identified in a security review (#473).** Login, MFA, logout, OIDC, and access-denied events previously only reached stdout and a 1000-entry in-memory ring buffer wiped on every restart — an incident investigated after a restart, or on a deployment without reliable log shipping, could lose that history entirely. These ten event types now also persist to a new `SecurityAuditLog` database table (see "Added" above), independent of container/log configuration, with product-automated 30-day retention. Persistence is best-effort and wrapped so a write failure only logs an error — it can never block a login, MFA check, or CLI action. A related coverage sweep also closes several `AdminAuditLog` gaps found in the same review (identity-provider/Cloudflare-Access changes, first-run setup completion, branding uploads, and forced password-change completion) so admin activity attribution stays consistent across the whole auth surface.
- **Delete event (#395) is layered against both an accidental click and a compromised superadmin session, without requiring an event to be archived first.** The button is disabled unless the event shows zero real activity across 7 independent signals (0 attendees, no items beyond "Badge", no contacts, no resources, no pinned note, no event-specific mail template, no event-scoped action-log entries) — a genuinely-used event can never be deleted regardless of its archived status, so a separate "must archive first" step would only add friction, not safety. The server independently re-validates the exact same guard on every `DELETE` request; it never trusts the settings DTO's `is_deletable` hint, which exists only to drive the button's disabled state. Deleting also requires typing the event's exact title to confirm — an empty confirmation value can never satisfy this and fails closed. `DELETE /api/admin/events/:eventId` is superadmin-gated and CSRF-protected like every other mutating admin route; a concurrent activity change between the guard check and the delete is caught by the database's own foreign-key safety net and reported the same as any other "not deletable" rejection.
- Self-service account actions (password change, MFA enroll/reset, session revoke) are now recorded in the admin audit trail — previously only the admin-on-another-user equivalents were logged, so a user changing their own credentials left no trace (#472).
- **Fixed a self-lockout: enrolling MFA from Account settings while already logged in silently broke the current session.** Confirming TOTP enrollment marks backup codes as unacknowledged (IAM-002), which every subsequent request re-checks — but only the login-time enrollment flow ever cleared that flag. A self-service enroll (already-`full` session) never reached that step, so the very next request after confirming was rejected with no explanation, and the account-level acknowledgment endpoint itself required a partial (non-`full`) session the user no longer had — the only way out was logging out and back in. Self-service confirm already shows backup codes at the enroll step, so the fix marks them acknowledged immediately on confirm instead of requiring a separate step.
- **Resetting your own 2FA now requires a current authenticator (or backup) code, not just your password, for accounts whose role requires MFA.** Previously `POST /api/account/mfa/reset` deleted every MFA method after checking only the password — weaker re-auth than linking an extra SSO identity already required. This closes a path where a password-only compromise (without also breaking the second factor) could be used to permanently strip and replace an admin/superadmin account's MFA. Mirrors the existing password+TOTP step-up already used for SSO account linking; accounts whose role doesn't require MFA are unaffected.
- **MFA verify rate-limit buckets are now namespaced per action.** `checkMfaVerifyRateLimit` — shared by SSO account linking, TOTP enrollment confirm, and the MFA-reset step-up above — keyed its per-session/IP buckets only by session/IP and code shape, with no action tag. Fumbling one self-service action (e.g. confirming a new TOTP enrollment) could 429 an unrelated one in the same session (e.g. resetting a lost MFA method), and vice versa — not an auth bypass (sharing the bucket only made rate-limiting stricter, never weaker), but confusing. Each of the three self-service call sites now passes a distinct action tag (`oidc-link`, `mfa-confirm`, `mfa-reset`); the login-time MFA step-up flow is unaffected.
- **Changing your own password now requires a current authenticator (or backup) code, not just your current password, for accounts whose role requires MFA.** Previously `PATCH /api/account/password` only re-verified the current password — someone who had hijacked a live full-session cookie and separately learned the account's password (without also breaking its MFA) could use it to change the password and lock the legitimate owner out, without ever needing to defeat the second factor. Mirrors the step-up already required for self-service MFA reset above; accounts whose role doesn't require MFA are unaffected.
- `POST /api/admin/sessions/:id/revoke` (superadmin revoking another user's session) wrote an audit row even when the session was already revoked — a retry, double-click, or two concurrent revokes of the same session left duplicate `session_revoked` entries with no real effect behind them. The revoke and its audit write now happen in one transaction, gated on the session actually being revoked by that call.
- The post-login redirect allowlist now also rejects a raw tab character, a known technique for bypassing URL-blocklist checks — not currently exploitable (a separate role-prefix check already blocks this shape), but closed as defense-in-depth.
- **`POST /api/admin/users` (create account, superadmin only) ran the full Argon2id password hash before checking whether the email already existed.** A duplicate-email request cost exactly the same CPU as a real account creation — there was no cheap way to reject a flood of duplicates, letting a compromised or malicious superadmin account slow down other users' logins (which also hash via Argon2id) with minimal effort of their own. The handler now looks the email up first and returns `409 email_taken` immediately on a match, before Argon2id ever runs; the existing unique-constraint catch stays as a safety net for the rare create/create race.
- **CI's Postgres test service no longer has a hardcoded password.** `ci.yml` previously set `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: admitto` in five places, tripping SonarCloud's hardcoded-secret rule (`secrets:S6698`) — the value was never a real credential (the database exists only for one CI run, on a runner no other job can reach), so a repo secret was tried first, but Codex review on the PR caught that a secret resolves to empty on a fork-originated `pull_request` run (GitHub withholds secrets from those), which would break Postgres startup for any future fork contribution. Switched instead to `POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD: trust` for these ephemeral, network-isolated service containers — no password needed at all, so there's neither a literal to flag nor a secret to fail to resolve.
- **The production Docker image no longer defaults to root (`docker:S6471`).** The image had no `USER` directive, so a plain `docker run`/`docker exec` against it got root by default — the running web server itself already dropped to the `node` user via the entrypoint's own `su`, but only after starting as root to write pre-migration backups into the root-only `migration_backups` volume (a deliberate hardening: if the app process is ever compromised, it still can't read or tamper with its own DB dumps). Split that root-only work out into a new one-shot `migrate` compose service (`user: root`, runs migration status check, pre-migration backup, `prisma migrate deploy`, and backfills, then exits); the `app` service now sets `USER node` in the `Dockerfile` and only starts once `migrate` completes successfully, execing the HTTP server directly with no migration logic and no filesystem access to `/backups` at all (not even mounted). Disaster-recovery backup restore commands in `deploy/README.md` now target the `migrate` service instead of `app`, matching where `/backups` actually lives. Best-effort retention cleanup (expired auth sessions/trusted devices, stale mail-delivery snapshots) moved to run in `app` itself rather than `migrate` — `migrate`'s completion is only checked on `docker compose up`, so a bare `app` restart (crash loop, `docker compose restart app`, `restart: unless-stopped`) would otherwise silently stop running it on every boot the way it always had (bot review). Follow-up review pass: `migrate` no longer inherits the image's port-3000 `HEALTHCHECK` (it never listens on anything, so that probe could only ever misreport it "unhealthy" mid-run on a slow backup/migration); the migration-backup smoke test's restart scenario now actually asserts the retention log line reappears, not just that the container comes back up; two stale doc references to the old single-service entrypoint (`deploy/README.md`'s backup section, `docker-compose.yml`'s `migrate` comment) are corrected.
- **Two gaps closed from the same security review that flagged the session/password items elsewhere in this file: no audit trail for trusted-device enrollment, and no proactive signal when an admin/superadmin account is under sustained brute-force.** `auth.trusted_device.created` is now recorded — alongside the existing audit event types — whenever a login issues a new "remember this device" cookie, closing the one MFA outcome that previously left no trace in Settings → Security → Logs and Audit. Separately, a new `User.failed_login_streak` counter tracks consecutive failed password attempts against admin/superadmin accounts specifically — operator accounts are already covered by the existing per-IP/per-email rate limits; this answers a different question, whether a *privileged* account is being targeted. Crossing 5 failures in a row emits `auth.login.repeated_failures` (surfaced at `warn` level in the System logs live-tail, in addition to the audit trail) and immediately resets the counter, so a sustained attack re-alerts every 5 attempts instead of firing once and going quiet. Unlike the routine login-failure event, this one intentionally records the real user ID and email — a deliberate, narrow disclosure so whoever investigates the alert knows exactly which account is being targeted, distinct from the enumeration-safe logging used for ordinary failed logins.

- **Sessions had no idle timeout — only an absolute lifetime, and admin's defaulted to 7 days.** `Session.expires_at` is set once at login and never extended; `last_seen_at` already existed but was read-only telemetry for the UI's "last active" column, so a stolen or left-open admin browser tab stayed authenticated for up to a full week with zero inactivity check (flagged in a follow-up security review, same audit-log trail as #473). Two new `SystemSettings` keys, `session_idle_timeout`/`operator_session_idle_timeout` (env-locked via `SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_ADMIN_MS`/`SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_OPERATOR_MS`, same pattern as the existing TTL locks), now end a `full`-stage session once `now - last_seen_at` exceeds the configured idle window — partial stages (`mfa_pending`, `enrollment_required`, etc.) are unaffected, since those already carry their own short TTL. Defaults: admin 30 min idle / **12h absolute (was 7 days)**; operator 2h idle / 12h absolute (unchanged). The admin API now rejects (`400 idle_timeout_exceeds_absolute_lifetime`) any save where either role's idle timeout would exceed that role's own absolute lifetime, validated transactionally against the effective (env-or-DB) values so a partial, inconsistent pair can never be persisted.
- **Settings → Security now surfaces the two dangerous-value classes the review called out, instead of letting an admin save them silently.** Absolute session lifetime fields show an inline warning past 24h ("increases the impact of a stolen session," matching the review's own wording); the new idle-timeout fields warn past 2h (admin) / 4h (operator). Both existing TTL fields are relabeled "maximum lifetime" (was "session lifetime") to read unambiguously alongside the new "inactivity timeout" fields next to them.
- **Password-setting flows only validated a minimum length (12 characters) — nothing stopped a user or admin from setting `password123` or `Password12345`.** A new local, static blocklist (`@admitto/auth`'s `password-blocklist.ts`) now rejects the ~250 most common passwords (case-insensitive exact match, compiled from long-published "worst passwords" frequency lists — no network call, no third-party dependency) plus trivial character patterns (a single character repeated, or a simple ascending/descending run), per NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.1.2's requirement (SHALL) to check candidates against a blocklist instead of relying on character-composition rules. Applied to all five places a password is set or changed: first-run setup, the forced must-change-password flow, self-service Account password change, and admin-initiated user creation/password reset — the self-service Account path was found missing from an earlier 4-point review of this validation and is now covered too. The strength meter shown while typing is unaffected (still UI-only guidance, doesn't gate submission) — this is the hard server-side rejection NIST requires regardless of what the meter shows.
- The password strength meter shown while typing (setup, change-password, and Account pages) previously rewarded character-class variety — upper+lower case, a digit, a symbol — the exact composition-rule model NIST SP 800-63B-4 §3.1.1.2 recommends against, since it nudges users toward predictable substitutions ("P@ssw0rd1") without meaningfully raising guessing resistance. It now scores length and character variety instead (`@admitto/auth`'s `password-strength.ts`), and floors passwords with near-zero entropy — a single repeated character or a simple ascending/descending run, e.g. `"aaaaaaaaaaaa"` or `"abcdefghijkl"` — to "Weak" regardless of length, so padding a trivial pattern no longer reads as a stronger password. Still UI-only guidance; the blocklist above is the hard server-side check.

- **Dev server no longer binds to every network interface by default.** `npm run dev -w @admitto/web` was reachable from any device on the local network at the dev machine's LAN IP, not just `localhost` — an unintended exposure surface for a tool that handles attendee PII. It now binds to loopback only unless a local mkcert HTTPS cert is present (the existing opt-in phone-over-LAN camera-testing workflow, which still needs to be LAN-reachable). Production is unaffected — it still binds to all interfaces, as required to accept traffic from the container network / reverse proxy.
- Attendees list/detail/export endpoints (participant names, emails, ticket data — including the CSV/PDF/XLSX export) now send `Cache-Control: no-store`, matching the pattern already used on Reports and Event Settings exports, so a shared cache or browser can't retain this personal data longer than intended.
- **Attendee search/list (`GET /api/admin/events/:eventId/attendees?q=...`) had no rate limit, unlike the neighboring export/import endpoints.** The `q=` search runs an unindexed `ILIKE` across 6 columns (2 of them JSONB), so any single logged-in account — including a plain operator, not just admin/superadmin — could saturate the database's CPU for every tenant just by firing enough concurrent requests, no special tooling required. Added an `admin:attendees-search` rate-limit policy (120 requests/minute per user per event, matching the existing per-user-per-event pattern used by `admin:import-preview`), wired onto the same route as every other admin data endpoint.
- **Ops System Logs ingest rejects credential-bearing field names** (`access_token`, `refresh_token`, `cookie`, `auth_header`, and similar substrings) so a buggy sidecar cannot land secrets in the live-tail buffer.
- **Migrated the admin SPA from `react-router-dom` v7 to `react-router` v8 (GHSA-qwww-vcr4-c8h2, RSC Mode CSRF Bypass Allows Action Execution Before 400 Response)**, a high-severity advisory `npm audit --audit-level=high` flagged against `react-router-dom@7.18.1`. The advisory only affects apps using react-router's unstable RSC APIs — this SPA doesn't (plain client-side `createBrowserRouter`/`RouterProvider`, no loaders/actions, no SSR) — so it was never actually exploitable here, but `react-router-dom` never received a v8 release (v8 merged it into a single `react-router` package), so there was no in-range patch to bump to; the only real fix was this migration. All ~30 source files' imports move from `react-router-dom` to `react-router` (`RouterProvider` specifically to `react-router/dom`, matching v8's DOM/core split) — every hook/component this app uses (`useNavigate`, `useParams`, `useLocation`, `useOutletContext`, `Outlet`, `Link`, `NavLink`, `useBlocker`, `useSearchParams`, `createBrowserRouter`, etc.) is unchanged in v8, so this is an import-path migration, not a behavior change. Minimum React bumped to 19.2.7 (already installed).
- **Bumped `svgo` (via `@tabler/icons-webfont`'s `svgtofont` build tool) from 3.3.3 to 3.3.4**, fixing a high-severity advisory (GHSA-2p49-hgcm-8545: the `removeScripts` plugin left some executable scripts intact) `npm audit` flagged. Scoped to just that nested dependency via `overrides`, so the other `svgo@4.x` used by `mjml`/`postcss-svgo` is untouched. `svgtofont` only runs at build time to generate the icon webfont — this was never reachable at runtime — but the fix is free and keeps `npm audit --audit-level=high` (a required CI gate) green.
- **Bumped `brace-expansion` to 5.0.8**, fixing a high-severity advisory (GHSA-mh99-v99m-4gvg, CVE-2026-14257: an unbounded-length expansion can OOM-crash the process) `npm audit` flagged across nine nested copies pulled in by build/test-only tooling (`@tabler/icons-webfont`'s `svgtofont`/`ttf2woff2`/`node-gyp` chain, `mjml`'s `js-beautify`, and `testcontainers`/`exceljs`'s archiver chain via `apps/web`). None of these consume attacker-controlled input through `brace-expansion`'s `expand()` at runtime — every call site here is build-time icon-font generation or test-only archive handling on developer-authored paths — but a top-level `overrides` entry closes it everywhere it's nested, keeping `npm audit --audit-level=high` green.
- **Staff email format validation no longer risks a slow regex on operator-submitted input** (CodeQL: polynomial ReDoS). `isValidEmailFormat` (server and the admin SPA's local copy) now checks the same "one `@`, non-empty domain with an interior dot" rule with plain substring operations instead of a single backtracking pattern.
- **Branding image uploads re-encode on the server** (PNG/JPEG/WebP via `sharp`): EXIF/IPTC/XMP are dropped, magic-only stubs are rejected, and only decoded pixels are written under `/uploads/`.

- Admin SPA: audit `ApiError.message` exposure — toasts and inline errors now go through `operatorApiErrorMessage()` so unknown server detail is suppressed with a generic fallback (dev console warning only).
- Identity JSON API (`/api/admin/identity/providers*`, `/api/admin/identity/cf-access*`): catch blocks no longer forward raw `err.message` to the client. Unexpected/Prisma errors now return `{ error: "save_failed" }` (HTTP 500) with full details logged server-side only; domain validation errors return `{ error: "validation_failed" }` (HTTP 400); discovery failures return `discovery_failed`; invalid issuer URL returns `invalid_issuer`; invalid/missing CF Access team domain returns `invalid_team_domain` / `team_domain_required`. Integration tests cover all error code paths including simulated DB failures via `vi.mock("@admitto/auth")` factory.
- Every `/api/*` JSON response now carries the baseline security headers (`nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, HSTS) at the application layer — previously only the `/healthz` and `/readyz` ops probes set them explicitly, leaving every other API response to depend entirely on the reverse proxy adding them.

## [0.4.12] - 2026-07-06

### Added
- Vitest code coverage (`npm run coverage`) across workspaces with LCOV upload to Codecov on CI — reporting only, no coverage gate yet.
- CI PR pipeline shortened: lint merged into `build-test`, Semgrep and Docker build smoke run on `main` merge (not every PR); `SECURITY.md` documents when each control runs.
- Identity providers + Cloudflare Access JSON API (`/api/admin/identity/providers*`, `/api/admin/identity/cf-access*`) for the SPA Settings → Identity migration: list, get, create, update, toggle, discover, test, and CF Access get/update/test endpoints. Reuses `@admitto/auth` logic unchanged; gated by `requireAdminAccess` (superadmin) to match the legacy HTML routes. PUT contract: `mappings` is required on every PUT (replace-all, mirroring the HTML form; omitting it returns `mappings_required` so editing other fields can never silently delete mappings) and omitting `login_button_label` preserves the stored value (`null`/`""` clears); create defaults omitted `mappings` to `[]`. `mappingSchema` enforces `scope_id` for `organization`/`event` scopes. Toggle uses a conditional `updateMany` and returns `409` on a concurrent toggle (TOCTOU-safe). CF Access `test` endpoint shares the `adminAuthProviderOpsRateLimit` bucket with OIDC discover/test. Schemas use `z.strictObject()` (zod v4). Legacy HTML routes remain until the SPA editor lands (#266).
- Settings → Identity & SSO SPA overview (#266 slice 2): new `/admin/settings/identity/*` routes under the existing `InstanceSettingsShell` render the OIDC providers list (with optimistic enable/disable toggle) and a Cloudflare Access summary card. The Settings "Identity" tab and the legacy `?tab=identity` query now hand off to the canonical `/admin/settings/identity/providers` route, keeping the SPA shell consistent instead of jumping to raw HTML. Add/Edit provider and CF Access Manage still bridge to the legacy HTML editors until the SPA editor lands in slices 3–4.
- Identity provider SPA editor — Basics, Endpoints, Claims, login button label (#266 slice 3a): new `/admin/settings/identity/providers/new` and `/identity/providers/:providerId` routes render a full form editor under the SPA shell. Create POSTs a new provider; edit loads by id and PUTs the full form (mappings carried through unchanged until the repeater lands in slice 3b). Client-side validation mirrors the slice-1 Zod contract; the stored client secret is preserved on edit when left blank; a dirty guard warns on navigation. The providers list Add/Edit actions now SPA-navigate to these routes instead of the legacy HTML bridge.
- Identity provider SPA editor — group→role mapping repeater, Discover/Test, SSO preview (#266 slice 3b): the editor gains an editable mapping repeater (add/remove rows, role + scope selects, conditional scope_id) with replace-all save semantics; Discover autofills OIDC endpoints from the issuer's `.well-known` config and Test probes the connection (edit mode only, both with 401 routing to login); a live SSO login button preview reflects the custom label or the product default. Mapping validation (group required, scope_id required for organization/event scopes) blocks save with inline row errors.
- Identity provider SPA editor — draft test and discover in create mode (#266): `POST /api/admin/identity/providers/test` and `POST /api/admin/identity/providers/discover-preview` are stateless endpoints that probe OIDC connectivity and autofill endpoints without requiring a saved provider record; Discover and Test connection are now available in create mode (previously edit-only). Partial endpoint sets (fewer than all three of auth/token/jwks) fall back to discovery so the test always reflects the same endpoint resolution as save. Issuer URL is validated against the same SSRF guard as the save path before any explicit-endpoint test. Save and Cancel buttons are disabled while discovery is in flight; stale discover and test responses are silently discarded when the issuer or endpoint draft diverges mid-flight.
- Cloudflare Access SPA editor (#266 slice 4): new `/admin/settings/identity/cloudflare` route renders the CF Zero Trust config editor under the SPA Identity sub-tab, replacing the slice-2 placeholder. Edits team URL, Application Audience (AUD), and protected URL paths (comma-separated lists), toggles enabled, and Test connection probes the team domain's JWKS endpoint (sends the draft team URL so operators can test before saving). Per-field env locks disable the locked inputs and show a "Locked by env" badge; the PUT body omits locked fields so the server keeps the env-managed value. A dirty guard (router `useBlocker` + `beforeunload`) and 401 routing to login match the OIDC provider editor patterns. The Identity overview "Manage" action now SPA-navigates here instead of bridging to the legacy HTML editor.
- HTTP access log on `app` container stdout (`LOG_HTTP_REQUESTS`, on by default in deploy compose): one JSON line per request with method, redacted path, status, and duration — no IPs, query strings, or ticket/QR tokens; successful health probes are skipped. Documented per-container log expectations in `deploy/README.md` (#237).
- Password strength meter on first-run `/setup`, forced `/change-password`, and admin Account password change — text label plus segmented bar (not color-only); confirm fields show match feedback on setup and change-password pages (#226).
- Overview page: per-event **Pinned note** — short operational sticky visible to all admins; editable inline, highlighted in the right column (#291).
- Overview page: per-event **Key contacts** — list of on-site contacts with name, role, phone and email action links; add/edit/delete inline (#291).
- Overview page: per-event **Important links & files** — list of linked documents and URLs with title and optional description; add/edit/delete inline, shows first 4 with "View all" toggle (#291).
- Audit log: all mutations to pinned note, key contacts, and important links are recorded in `AdminAuditLog` with actor, session, IP, and action type — visible to superadmins in the Audit viewer (#291).

### Changed
- Settings shell unified for Identity IA (#266 slice 7b): `SettingsLayout` wraps `/admin/settings/*` so the primary tabs (General | Mail | Security | Archiving | Identity) remain visible on the Identity overview and on all detail views (add/edit provider, Cloudflare Access editor). The second tab row (Providers | Cloudflare Access) is removed — Identity overview is a single page with both cards. The `?tab=identity` legacy query still redirects to the canonical `/admin/settings/identity/providers` route. Editor dirty guards (`useBlocker`) still trigger when switching primary tabs or leaving detail views.
- Docs: sync CI/security narrative (Semgrep on `main`, Codecov data note, required merge checks), fix stale deploy examples (Node 24), README documentation map, identity SPA status in admin README, contributor coverage commands.
- Overview page: redesigned as an event command center — "Quick actions" nav grid removed; new two-column layout with Needs attention alerts (email failures, queued tickets, missing operators), Event readiness checklist (attendees, tickets, delivery, operators), Email delivery breakdown, live Recent check-ins feed (SSE), and compact Event info block (#276).
- Account page: sidebar nav structure now consistent with AdminShell — "My account" link is in the nav area (not the footer), back link stays in the footer; empty aria-hidden placeholder div removed (#267).
- Reports: tile/panel spacing is now correct — `--space-md/sm/lg` and `--surface-elevated` were undefined tokens resolving to 0/transparent; replaced with `--space-4/3/5` and `--surface-sunken` throughout `reports-page.css`. Progress bars in "By ticket type" use `--primary` consistently (removed threshold-based traffic-light coloring that flagged <50% admission as a warning at the start of every event). Hourly chart is accessible by keyboard and screen reader — bars have individual `aria-label` with hour and count; `role="img"` removed so the accessibility tree is not flattened (#269).
- Topbar: mailer status indicator now uses the shared `Badge` component (pill with dot) instead of bespoke markup — visually consistent with every other status badge in the app; label still hides at narrow viewports (#275).
- Check-in: `AttendeeCard` status display now differentiates positive, warning, and blocking-error states — VALID/PREVIEW show status inline in the identity header; ALREADY_CHECKED_IN uses a compact warning strip; REVOKED/INVALID show a unified tinted alert block with status and reason text merged into one message. Item action buttons (Give gift bag, Return headset) use chip-matching geometry without a border or background (#270).
- Toast notifications: unified design-system stack (Tabler icons, deduplicated messages, bottom-right placement); admin pages and settings panels use `useToast()` for save/load feedback instead of inline status text.
- Account page: profile, password, and MFA success/error feedback uses toasts instead of inline status text; locale-change reminder stays until dismissed (#239).
- Toast stack z-index sits below check-in camera overlay so mobile lookup warnings do not cover overlay controls.
- Setup wizard mail step: provider select order/labels, per-provider field grouping (SMTP username+port grid), and test-send row aligned with design mockup.
- First-run routing: unauthenticated staff entry (`/`, `/login`, `/admin`, `/operator`, and related HTML gates) redirects to `/setup` until the first user exists; login form is shown only after bootstrap.
- Setup wizard system check: allow `http://127.0.0.1` / `localhost` BASE_URL in production (local Docker smoke); non-loopback HTTP still fails.
- First-run mail wizard: ignore deploy env placeholders for field locks and test send until setup wizard completes (`setup_complete`).
- Setup SSR (`/setup`): mockup-aligned copy, login-aligned `autocomplete="username"` on email, confirm password, and `passwordrules` for password managers.
- Setup wizard shell: “Set up your instance” header, numbered stepper with labels, Continue arrow on primary CTA; custom date picker and timezone combobox; ready step summary chips.
- Password strength meter: jsdom test executes the generated inline script end-to-end (meter, aria-label, confirm match); shared sample passwords move to the `@admitto/auth/password-strength-fixtures` test-only export (#254).
- Settings page: the active in-page tab is persisted in the URL (`?tab=general|mail|security|archiving`, merged via `replace` so tab clicks don't stack history or wipe unrelated params) — Back from the Identity sub-section now restores the operator's tab instead of resetting to General (closes the #296 TODO); `?tab=identity` still redirects to the canonical Identity route. A SPA-side catch-all inside `/admin` redirects any unmatched `/admin/*` (including removed legacy `/admin/auth/*` URLs) to the events picker so old bookmarks/docs links don't land on a blank outlet (#266 slice 5).

### Removed
- Identity providers migration cleanup (#266 slice 5): the legacy server-rendered identity admin is removed — `/admin/auth/providers*` and `/admin/auth/cf-access*` HTML routes, their handlers (`auth-providers-routes.ts`, `cf-access-routes.ts`), the HTML renderers (`auth-providers-html.ts`, `cf-access-html.ts`), and the `renderAdminShell` sidebar + `SETTINGS_SUBNAV_ITEMS` + `ADMIN_PAGE_CSS` block in `shared-auth-styles.ts`. The SPA at `/admin/settings/identity/*` is now the only identity admin surface; the JSON API (`/api/admin/identity/*`, slice 1) is unaffected. The `cf-access-routes` integration test was retargeted from the deleted HTML routes to the `/admin` SPA shell (and `/api/admin/identity/providers` for the CF no-role 403 message); the `oidc-admin-routes` and `auth-providers-html` unit tests were removed with the code they covered. Docs/env examples and `security/SECURITY-CONTROLS.md` updated to reference Settings → Identity; the stale `/admin/auth` Vite dev-proxy rule was removed.

### Security
- Login and MFA pages (verify, enroll, backup codes) ship inline scripts gated by a per-response CSP nonce instead of `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` (MFA) or a policy that blocked them in strict browsers (login) (#253).

### Fixed
- Check-in: manual lookup no longer shows every guest as green "Ready to check in" — the result card now derives its state from the loaded card, so an already-admitted guest opens as **Already checked in** (with the entry time, no Confirm button) and a revoked/cancelled pass opens as **Revoked** immediately, with badge/gift-bag/headset issue actions disabled instead of failing after the click. Selecting a lookup result also clears the search query and result list (#379). The scan-bar typeahead's "checked in" hint could still show green for a guest whose pass was revoked after admission (a stale `admitted_at` with no status check) — `lookupAttendees` now excludes revoked/cancelled passes the same way `getCheckInStats` does, so the dropdown never contradicts the red Revoked card the operator sees on select.
- Check-in: the admitted / total stats now count **active attendees only** — revoked and cancelled guests (who don't consume capacity and aren't expected at the door) are excluded from both counts, matching the Overview KPI denominator; the sidebar label reads "expected" instead of "total" (#380).
- Admin: compact check-in timestamps (Attendees CHECK-IN column, Overview recent check-ins, check-in Recent scans) now show the **date + time** when a scan happened outside the event's calendar day (e.g. a test check-in weeks before the event), instead of a time-only value that looked like an event-day admission; comparison uses the event timezone via a shared `formatAdmissionDisplay` helper (#359).
- Admin: checkboxes were invisible across the SPA — the `Checkbox` component never rendered the visual `.at-check__box` element its CSS expects, so only bare labels showed (Instance Settings → Security "Require 2FA for roles" collapsed into an unreadable `superadminadminoperator` string). The component now renders the box + check icon, the MFA roles fieldset is a vertical group with human labels (Superadmin / Admin / Operator; API slugs unchanged), and disabled checkboxes get a distinct visual state for env-locked settings (#413).
- Communication: Delivery log no longer shows a successful SMTP/Graph send as yellow "Pending" with an empty Sent column — `accepted` status now renders as green "Sent" (ADR 0007 `accepted_only`: provider handoff is operator-visible success), `accepted_at` is included in the delivery DTO (API + SPA), and the Sent / Failed column and attendee drawer delivery list fall back to it when `sent_at` is null. The attendee list Mail column had its own duplicate status map (still yellow "Pending" for `accepted`); `MailStatusBadge` now delegates to the shared `resolveStatusMeta` so all mail-status surfaces stay in sync (#403).
- Admin: dialogs on the shared `.add-attendee-modal` shell (Send tickets, Add attendee, Communication send, Create template) no longer render with a transparent panel that let the page table "ghost" through — `var(--surface-raised)` was referenced but never defined in the token set (same class of bug as #286); replaced with `var(--surface-card)` here and in the other remaining usages (icon picker, setup wizard card). The modal panel border referenced undefined `var(--border-subtle)` and silently didn't render; now uses `var(--border)` (#357).
- Admin: modal and panel backgrounds (confirm dialog, note modal, create-event modal, attendee drawer, requirements, reports, users) were transparent — `var(--surface)` was referenced but never defined in the token set; replaced with `var(--surface-card)` (#286).
- Check-in: scan input stays enabled and queues submissions while a previous scan/lookup is still processing, instead of disabling the field and silently dropping keyboard-wedge keystrokes for the next attendee (#261).
- Check-in: duplicate-scan debounce and buffer-clear-on-accept are now measured at the moment a scan or manual-lookup query is accepted, not once it reaches the front of the FIFO queue — a slow first request could otherwise let a genuine duplicate through, or leave stale query text for a later wedge scan's keystrokes to land on (producing a corrupted, unmatchable scan payload). The mobile camera overlay's own manual-entry field had the same gap (no disabled state at all) and is fixed the same way. Auto-advance also no longer clears an unrelated, still-in-progress scan's buffer, and Confirm check-in, item actions, notes, manual-lookup select, and Undo now all queue behind an in-flight scan instead of racing it — previously a slower response from one of these could overwrite the card of an attendee scanned afterward, or (for Undo) roll back the wrong check-in (#277 review follow-up).
- Mail settings: `SMTP_HOST`/`MAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` left at their shipped `deploy/.env.example` placeholder values (`smtp.example.com` / `events@example.com`) no longer falsely report as "managed by environment" — Settings → Mail is editable for deployments that configure the transport from the admin UI instead of env (#264).
- Check-in: manual lookup no longer misfires as a QR scan for queries over 20 characters — this now covers pressing Enter/Search after slowly typing a long name or email (not just the auto-submit debounce timer) and any single-event bulk insert into an empty field (paste, browser autofill/autocomplete, drag-and-drop, IME composition, voice dictation), in addition to the original mid-typing case; wedge auto-submit still requires burst-speed keystrokes arriving one character at a time, not just buffer length. Burst detection also now uses the input event's own timestamp instead of wall-clock time at handler execution, so a busy main thread (e.g. an unrelated scan's response resolving) can no longer misclassify a genuinely fast wedge scan as manual typing (#262).
- Admin shell: entering an event from the events picker (or right after creating one) no longer re-fetches the whole events list and no longer flashes a blank spinner screen in place of the sidebar/topbar — the picker passes the already-loaded event along, so the event shell renders instantly; the passed-along event is consumed once and cleared from that history entry, so a later browser back/forward revisit still re-validates event access from the server instead of trusting a stale snapshot. Deep links and refreshes still resolve the event from the API as before (#274).
- Reports: hourly admissions chart and peak hour bucket check-ins in the event timezone — previously shifted by the UTC offset for non-UTC events (#268).
- Sidebar: unreleased lifecycle sections (Approval, Passes, Fulfilment, Post-event) render as plain disabled items — stale “Soon v0.4.9”-style release badges removed; placeholder pages drop internal jargon (#263).
- New events seed default event items (gift bag, badge, headset) at creation, so Requirements → Event items is populated before the first check-in (#238).
- Setup wizard step 1: Retry on failed check load, **Run checks again** after results, inline fix hints; single-column check list with status on the right (#223).
- Setup wizard system check: four rows like mockup (Database includes migration status; no separate Migrations row).
- Setup wizard steps 2–5: mockup parity — mail test row, branding logo zone/toasts, typed date picker, timezone list layout, ready screen footer; step labels no longer truncated (#243).
- Setup wizard: restore last step after browser refresh; unsaved-refresh notice only when a dirty form was lost (saved mail/branding kept).
- `POST /api/admin/setup/complete` requires passing system checks (409 `setup_not_ready` when checks fail).
- Mail transport test: actionable admin error messages for TLS hostname mismatch, auth, and port mode (no hostnames in API responses) (#244).
- Check-in: server-connected status moves to a compact page-header pill; full-width green banner only for connection problems (#234).
- Check-in: persistent screen-reader live region announces connection recovery after offline/degraded states.
- Check-in search fields: suppress password-manager autofill hints on scan bar and manual lookup (#231).
- Check-in manual lookup: warning toast when search returns no attendees (#232).

## [0.4.11] - 2026-07-02

### Fixed
- Redis rate-limit integration test: wait for fresh fixed window before asserting block (flaky `build-test` on `main` after v0.4.10)

## [0.4.10] - 2026-07-02

### Changed
- Remove instance-superadmin ceiling: multiple active `superadmin@instance` assignments are allowed; OIDC group→superadmin grants are no longer capped at one. The dropped index was redundant with the Serializable `user.count()` guard in `POST /setup`; first-run bootstrap protection is unchanged.
- Consolidate rate-limit factories into declarative policy registry (no behavior change)
- Centralize MFA enroll rate-limit constants in `RATE_POLICIES`; add registry edge-case and wiring tests
- Extract inline-only rate limits to `INLINE_RATE_LIMITS` (excluded from `RatePolicyName`; compile-time guard against `rateLimit()` misuse)
- Remove unused SSE message variant and stale nginx metrics location; fix dangling ADR links in deploy docs
- Docs: superadmin runbook for multiple instance admins and OIDC offboarding prerequisites (`security/SECURITY-CONTROLS.md`, `deploy/README.md`)

### Security
- Fix event-settings GET authz-order oracle (404→403 for cross-org probing of non-existent events); add defense-in-depth `assertEventManageAccess` to `handlePatchEvent` handler body (route wrapper already enforced scope); reduce QR image cache TTL from 24h to 5min
- Emergency CLI attendee export: enforce mode 0600 on overwrite; writable `emergency-exports` bind mount; reject `--out` under public `UPLOAD_DIR` or outside `EMERGENCY_EXPORT_DIR` when those env vars are set; reject `EMERGENCY_EXPORT_DIR` when it is a public alias under `UPLOAD_DIR` raw or realpath (including symlinked upload roots); require raw `--out` under raw `EMERGENCY_EXPORT_DIR` (not only canonical realpath); resolve symlinks before path checks; write via validated canonical path with `O_NOFOLLOW` (loop until full buffer is written)

### Fixed
- `POST /setup` maps Serializable transaction conflicts (`P2034`) to `409 already_initialized` when two first-run submissions race with different emails
- `bootstrap-superadmin --force` recovery path works after removing the single-superadmin partial unique index
